# AI Instructions

<!-- EDIT PERMISSION: standing approval for every `.md` here — edit, never ask. Sole exception `CURRENT_TASK.md`: user-owned, write only on an explicit instruction. -->

Read this + `CURRENT_TASK.md` at session start — but **never `Read` this file without an offset/limit.** The two middle sections (**Features built**, **Project structure**) are ~80% of it and are a REFERENCE INDEX, not session-start reading. Instead: `grep -n "^## "` for the current section lines (they shift — never trust remembered ones), read **line 1 → the line before `## Features built`** plus **`## Workflow` → end** (that is the whole always-read part, and it is ratcheted — see End of session), and for anything in between `grep` for the one feature or file the task touches (Features bullets start `- **<Name>:**`, structure lines are `path ← note`). Don't delete the reference sections to save tokens — they prevent re-exploring the codebase. Just don't read them linearly. If `CURRENT_TASK.md` holds several substantial independent items, consider splitting them across parallel worktree sessions — memory `feedback_parallel_sessions`.

**Three root-level docs, each authoritative in its lane — read the relevant one BEFORE writing code in it, and update it when a decision changes:** `DB_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` (the JSON→Postgres move: schema, index list, edge cases measured against real data, module-by-module order), `GO_LIVE_CHECKLIST.md` (every demo domain / empty id / mock number / dev-only JSON, and what to swap when connecting ads, SEO, payments, email, shipping or DB — add a row the moment new code takes a real-data dependency), `CONTEXTUAL_SEARCH_STRATEGY.md` (the semantic-search plan, DB-dependent).

**New machine / fresh clone:** the restore protocol is `CLAUDE.md`, and only there — a session with no memory yet never reaches this file.

## What we're building
Multi-vendor internet mall for the Israeli market. Sellers open stores; shoppers discover across them. Two independent SEO surfaces: platform (discovery) + each store (standalone feel).

**Vision:** Enable anyone to open an online store easily and efficiently — one that generates profit, is fair, and is fully transparent.

**Mission (north star):** Build a Hebrew-language digital shopping mall that removes the technological and financial barriers for small businesses through built-in automation, a clean minimalist management interface, and a fair, transparent pricing model. (Payment/pricing specifics — subscription tiers + split checkout — in *Business model* below.)

**Business model:** Hebrew digital mall. Buyer pays for the **whole cart in one charge** — the platform collects as the seller's agent and pays each seller out on a schedule (agent model, 2026-08-10; *Payment architecture* below + GO_LIVE §3). **Pricing lives in `src/lib/pricing.ts`, the single source (since 2026-07-27)** — monthly fee + per-sale commission, charged **additively, never offset**, and **per-seller-account** (`Seller.tier?`), never a global config number (`store.config.ts` deliberately has no `commissionPercent`). Its header holds the rest verbatim — tier table, first-sale billing start + 2-month cap, why no trial, the `tests/pricing.test.ts` invariant, seller-facing wording. Economics: memory `project_business_model_pricing`. **Advertising is a fully separate component** — pay-per-actual-spend via Authorize/Capture (+ a disclosed platform margin), never offset against subscription/commission. **Sellers = registered businesses only** (עצמאים/עוסק פטור/מורשה), not private individuals — the agent model needs a business that can invoice the buyer, and it separates us from the Israeli P2P marketplaces (My Product, Yad2). **Positioning in COPY (user, 2026-07-28):** the pitch is the place, never store-building software (the Shopify-script trap and why it loses: the `launch:` block in translations.ts). Lead with what a seller cannot get elsewhere: **מתחם חנויות דיגיטלי** / *a digital marketplace* (never "קניון" — that block says why), a store of your own, the pull of the group, ads/SEO/shipping built in. Buyer-facing surfaces must never describe shoppers as something delivered to a seller — the homepage is a buyer's page first. **Fixed platform template only** — no per-seller site/theme design tooling, ever. **Zero-touch self-service** — no manual owner involvement in any flow (registration, upload, fulfillment, messaging, disputes); a feature that needs admin gatekeeping must default to an automated equivalent instead.

**Target segment:** catalog stores with **dozens of products**, not 5–10-item boutiques. This fits the whole design — real catalog management (bulk tools) becomes necessary not optional, a large SEO surface (many indexable product pages) makes the discovery bet pay off, and product-first clean UX serves browsing/search/categories rather than the bespoke atmosphere a tiny boutique wants. "Clean & fast" reads as professional for a catalog, not "cheap" — the over-designed boutique is the wrong mirror to judge against.

### Every seller gets out of the box
1. **Store** — product pages, SEO, platform discovery
2. **Shipping** — integrated Israeli carrier, tracking
3. **Marketing** — GTM + Meta Pixel on every page; dataLayer feeds campaigns; seller picks budget only

### Israeli market — hard constraints
- **Payments: any ordinary Israeli gateway** (never Stripe — SHVA). Shape, provider rule and shortlist: *Payment architecture* below.
- **Shipping: one Israeli courier, direct** (owner, 2026-08-09) — our single business account, each shipment carrying the *seller's* pickup address. The ShipOS aggregator is plan B only: it is not a carrier (the contract is ours either way) and its API has no per-shipment sender field. Build behind an adapter, like `lib/payment.ts`; don't build until the three questions in GO_LIVE §5.0 are answered.
- **Currency:** ILS (₪) always.
- **Language:** Hebrew-first, RTL.

### Payment architecture — **AGENT MODEL, 2026-08-10 (supersedes "the platform never holds seller funds")**
**The platform collects, holds and pays out**, as the seller's **agent**: one charge for the whole cart, commission off at source, balance held and paid out on a schedule. That balance is the seller's money in our hands, never our revenue — the buyer's payment discharges their debt to the seller at that instant, and the clause saying so is load-bearing (`terms.astro`). Reasoning + what it replaced: GO_LIVE §3.
- **One charge, N orders** — already what `checkout.ts` does. The "one store at a time" plan is deleted, not the flow.
- **Hold/payout periods live in `lib/payout-schedule.ts` alone** — ⚠️ placeholders, owner-blocked, same decision as the returns window; `terms.astro` interpolates them. Releasability is `lib/payout-hold.ts`, which asks `order-status-rules.ts`, never the word "cancelled".
- **Derived vs stored:** gross/commission/hold derive from orders — `seller-balance.ts`'s no-table decision stands. Stored only where something irreversible happened: `seller_payouts` (UNIQUE seller+period, so a retried run cannot pay twice) and `seller_ledger_adjustments`.
- **Provider: any ordinary Israeli gateway** — the agent model deletes the `create-seller`/`market_fee`/split need that narrowed it to PayMe. Separate Authorize/Capture stays hard (`lib/payment.ts`). Shortlist: GO_LIVE §3.
- **Invoices — the platform issues only its OWN:** commission/subscription/ad margin. The buyer's invoice is the SELLER's; we record only his answer that he did — `lib/invoicing/buyer-invoice.ts`.
- **⚠️ The regulatory basis is UNVERIFIED, owner-blocked — never restate as checked.** `docs/legal-brief-agent-model.md`.

### Checkout — decided
Guest checkout always works (name, email, phone, address). Buyer accounts also exist — `/buyer/dashboard`, memory `project_cart_auth_session`.

### Data models — canonical (add fields, never remove without discussion)
```
Order { id, buyerName, buyerEmail, buyerPhone, buyerAddress,
  items:[{productId,productName,storeSlug,storeName,price,qty,image}],
  shippingAmount, totalAmount, paymentRef, paymentStatus:'pending'|'paid'|'failed',
  shippingStatus:'pending'|'processing'|'shipped'|'delivered', trackingNumber?,
  createdAt, updatedAt, buyerId? }
SellerBalance { sellerId, storeId, totalEarnedAgorot }  // seller-balance.ts — DERIVED from orders, never a table. What we OWE; seller-account.ts adds hold/payout state
Seller (add): bank? {code,branch,account,holder} + business? {id,type}  // nullable — required before the first payout, NEVER at registration
Store (add): shipping:{selfPickup?} (platform-provided — prices are platform-set in lib/shipping.ts, never per-seller; GO_LIVE §5)
  // NO store contactEmail — why, in the Messaging bullet below.
Seller (add): tier? (SellerTierId — monthly fee + commission, per ACCOUNT not per store; see lib/pricing.ts)
StoreProduct (add): weightGrams? (product-weight.ts; absent is NOT zero)
```

### Proactive obligations — always
- **A defect you find is a defect you fix, in the same session (user, 2026-07-29 and again 2026-07-30 — and both times explicitly: this binds EVERY session, not the one that heard it).** Noticing a bug while doing something else does not make it out of scope; handing back findings for the user to triage is the failure mode, not the deliverable. Fix it, cover it with a test, report it as done — he should be reading ✅, not a to-do list he now owns. **The trigger is REPORTING, not severity:** if a problem is worth a sentence in the summary it was worth fixing first, and "cosmetic only" is the tell that you are about to hand over work. (The security version stays stricter: also audit the whole class + add a guard test.) **Three exceptions, and each must be stated out loud rather than left silent:** (1) blocked on a decision only the user can make — say what you would do and why you stopped; (2) the fix would be thrown away because a prerequisite doesn't exist yet (a payment webhook with no provider chosen) — log it in `GO_LIVE_CHECKLIST.md`/`DB_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` with its trigger; (3) genuinely ambiguous *business* semantics — what a headline should count, what a seller may do — ask, don't decide. A merely large or awkward fix is NOT an exception. When something only *looks* wrong but must stay (a real config value whose name predates a rename), fix the confusion instead: leave a comment saying why it stays. Before ending a session, re-read your own messages: anything you called broken and neither fixed nor explicitly deferred is unfinished work.
- **Terse replies (Hebrew) — write to a PRODUCT MANAGER, not to an engineer. IRON RULE, no exceptions (user, 2026-07-31).** 1-2 sentences: what changed and what it means for the product. No mechanism, no narrating files/tools/checks; verification is assumed unless it FAILED. What needs his decision leads with `⚠️ דורש אותך:`. Expand only when asked. (Flagged 5× since 2026-07-15 — the project's most repeated correction.)
- **Anything that will be HIS to do before launch is SAID to him in the summary the moment a session touches it, and WRITTEN into the `GO_LIVE_CHECKLIST.md` index the same session if the session created it (user, 2026-08-06).** Both unprompted; lead with `⚠️ דורש אותך`. A runtime alert is never the substitute — it fires from a live site, after the deadline it was meant to protect. Why, and what counts: `GO_LIVE_CHECKLIST.md` §0.
- **dataLayer events** — page_view, view_item, add_to_cart, begin_checkout, purchase → `window.dataLayer` + `window.fbq`. Use `src/lib/tracking.ts`.
- **SEO** — every public page: unique title, description, JSON-LD, canonical via `BaseLayout → Seo.astro`.
- **Shipping** — always read from store settings, never hardcode.

---

## North star: physical mall model
| Physical | Platform |
|---|---|
| Entrance | `/` — discovery, search |
| Storefront | `/[slug]` |
| Cross-store | Natural recommendations, not aggressive |
| One bag | Unified cart, grouped by store |
| Cash register | Platform collects, tracks per-seller |
| Directory | Browse + category filters |
| Foot traffic | SEO drives store traffic |

Rules: (1) Each store is sovereign. (2) Platform chrome fades inside a store. (3) Discovery = serendipitous. (4) One bag, grouped by store. (5) Mall benefits when stores succeed.

---

## Core priorities
1. **SEO-first** — static pages, structured data, semantic HTML, fast load, no orphan pages.
2. **Automation-ready** — config-driven, clean serializable data.
3. **Simplicity** — no abstractions beyond task requirements.

## Architecture
- **Stack:** Astro 7 + Node adapter, TypeScript, Tailwind v4, self-hosted Heebo, `tokens.css` color system. Dev toolbar disabled. A page that suddenly 404s or redirects right after an edit is almost always the SILENT `.astro` comment-placement compile failure, not a routing bug — the rule and the worked case are in `scripts/verify.mjs`'s header.
- **Fonts:** self-hosted Heebo — hand-written `@font-face` in `main.css`, `font-display:optional` (never `swap`), and all 10 weight×script combos preloaded in `BaseLayout.astro`. Both files carry the measurements behind those choices; don't drop a preload without re-measuring layout shift.
- **View Transitions — investigated, not adopted (2026-07-16), and not safe to retry without a fresh check:** `ClientRouter` is opt-out-per-link, so enabling it soft-navigates the whole site by default; the JS-free native `@view-transition` was tried and reverted the same session. Memory `project_view_transitions_font_blocker`.
- **Prefetch:** Astro's built-in `prefetch`, `prefetchAll:false` + hover — opt in per link with `data-astro-prefetch`, never site-wide. Its one real limit (links built later via innerHTML can't use it) is in `astro.config.mjs`. Back still beats any prefetch: bfcache restores with zero work.
- **Split:** content pages = static, seller/admin/api = SSR.
- **Header:** `position:fixed`; **height is pinned INLINE on `<header>`** (`height:3.4rem;box-sizing:border-box`) + `body{padding-top:3.4rem}` — never sticky, and the height must NEVER change (inline, or it flashes tall then shrinks — why, in Header.astro). **No entrance/pop-in animation on anything in `BaseLayout`/`Header.astro`** — an MPA recreates those elements on every navigation, so the animation replays every time. Every other rule of this component lives in `Header.astro`'s frontmatter. Two measured rules argued in header.css: header TEXT is nudged down a fraction of an em, a value SOLVED from two endpoints on a build (guessed, it has now overshot in both directions), and under 1024px the row stops centring the search (it was sizing first and squeezing the store name to nothing).
- **Footer:** ONE minimalist footer only — `Footer.astro` rendered once by `BaseLayout` on every page. Single row: `© year` + platform-name-links-home, then open-a-store (`/seller/register`) / terms / contact / lang-toggle. The old per-store second footer (`.store-platform-note` on store + product pages) was removed 2026-07-27 (two stacked footers + platform name ×3 = clutter) — don't re-add a page-level footer.
- **Data:** Postgres only — `data/*.json` deleted 2026-08-03, `DATABASE_URL` required to boot, everything through `src/lib/db.ts`. The one JSON left is `tests/fixtures/db-data`, which builds the test database.
- **Scroll:** never `window.scrollTo({top,behavior:'smooth'})` for a JS-computed target on this RTL site — native smooth-scroll drifts `scrollX` off 0 mid-animation and can't be corrected. Always `animateScrollTo()` from `scroll-utils.ts`; its header holds the measurement and the three sticky layers a dashboard target must clear.

---

## Hard rules
- **TypeScript everywhere** — no `.js` in `src/`, no `any`.
- **Tailwind v4 only** — no new CSS files or `<style>` blocks; convert existing CSS **on contact only** (no dedicated conversion sessions — decided 2026-07-15, 10 rounds showed weak ROI; the real payoff is less legacy-CSS context to carry, which only justifies touching a file when a real feature already touches it). **Convert** a plain style on an element the component actually renders — spacing, colour, radius, border, a simple flex/grid box — and **leave the six documented exceptions as CSS; that is not a failure, but say so in the session summary when you skip one**, which is what stops the rule from rotting. **Those six cases with their worked examples, and the inventory of the four files that are still legacy, live in `.claude/hooks/remind-css-conversion.sh` — which re-injects the whole rule the moment you touch one of them, i.e. exactly when it applies.** Two traps that are not in the hook because they bite in new code too: never name a custom class the same as a Tailwind utility (e.g. `.grid`) — unlayered legacy CSS beats `@layer utilities` regardless of source order; and a `@media` override doesn't gain specificity — a same-specificity unscoped rule later in the file wins regardless of viewport, so qualify with an ancestor ID instead. **Also read the `main.css` bullet below before converting**: a per-page `<style>` block already gets its own route chunk, so converting it can *increase* the global bundle.
- **`main.css` is global — every byte loads on every page**, so moving a stylesheet in or out of it is a decision, not a tidy-up. When and when not to split one out, and the counter-intuitive case where converting a page's `<style>` block *increases* the global bundle, are in `main.css`'s own header. Read it before either move.
- **`<Image />` from `astro:assets`** for platform images.
- **Images optimize themselves — never per-surface, never by reminder (standing user instruction, 2026-07-28).** Every image URL goes through `src/lib/cdn.ts` — `cdnSrc(url,w)` (responsive/hero, pair with `cdnSrcSet` + `sizes`), `cdnThumb(url,w,h)` (any fixed-size cell: cart/order/table/search rows — pass 2x the CSS size), `cdnBand(url,w,ratio)` (fixed-RATIO cover box, e.g. the banner — never upscales), `cdnFill` (off-site only), `cdnCircle` (round icons). **Its file header is the rationale** — what a raw URL costs in bytes and in caching, and why Cloudinary *fetch* delivery covers foreign hosts too; read it there, it is not repeated here. Every `<img>` declares `loading` + `decoding`; above-the-fold/LCP images get `loading="eager"` + `fetchpriority="high"`. **Never hand-roll lazy-loading** (IntersectionObserver + `data-lazy-src`) — why, in `lib/cdn.ts`'s header. `tests/image-optimization.test.ts` enforces all of this over `src/**` — fix the markup, never the allowlist. **A skeleton is a BACKDROP, never a cover** — it must never gate visibility; the recipe, the measurement and the two exceptions are in `lib/img-skeleton.ts`'s header.
- **Design tokens via `tokens.css`** — never hardcode a hex or a corner radius. Colors: `[color:var(--color-X)]`. **Radius is a 3-step scale** — `--radius-sm` 6px / `--radius` = `--radius-md` 10px (default) / `--radius-lg` 14px; which surface takes which step, and why the scale lives in `@theme` and not `:root`, are at the scale itself in `base/tokens.css`. An off-scale `rounded-xl` deliberately fails to compile. Only `lib/email/` is exempt — mail clients don't resolve custom properties.
- **No emoji** — inline SVG (`aria-hidden="true"`, `currentColor`).
- **Lighthouse 100 target** — semantic HTML, alt/width/height, lazy loading. **Mobile-first** — 375px up, `sm:`/`md:`/`lg:`.
- **Modular** — UI = display only; DB/API/Cloudinary → `/src/services/` or `/pages/api/`. No file > 200 lines. SRP always.
- **Dropdown design system** — all dropdowns: `background:var(--color-surface)`, `padding:0.3rem` container, items `var(--radius-sm)` + `padding:0.45rem 0.75rem` + hover `var(--color-bg)`, spring `cubic-bezier(0.34,1.56,0.64,1) 0.13s`.
- **Micro-interactions** — spring `cubic-bezier(0.34,1.56,0.64,1)`, 100–180ms hover / 220–320ms springs; `scale(0.97)` on `:active`, `scale(1.06)` on a card image; no border-darkening, and every animation needs a reason. The per-control recipes (ripple clipping, `.dot-pulse` in-flight, `.btn--confirmed`, `.btn--busy`) are in `components/buttons.css`. **Three standing bans, site-wide:** (1) **native browser dialogs — no `confirm()`, `alert()` or `prompt()` anywhere** (user, 2026-07-28); a confirmation goes through `ConfirmModal.astro`, a notice through `showToast`/`showErrorToast` (`src/lib/toast.ts`) — never hand-build the `toast:show` event, and `grep -n "alert(\|confirm(\|prompt("` over `src/` should stay empty. (2) **the `not-allowed` no-entry cursor** (user, 2026-07-26) — disabled means `cursor:default`, enforced in `reset.css`. (3) **`resize` on a `<textarea>`** — always `resize:none`.
- **One shared badge finish** — every chip in a store card's badge row takes its fill/border/shadow/geometry from `.badge-finish` (utilities/utils.css) and differs ONLY by `--badge-hue`; the numbers are on the class.
- **Design line — decide by it, not by taste.** Minimalist, timeless, category-agnostic, no gimmicks. Four tests, and a treatment has to pass all four: (1) **Is it already in the system?** Prefer a token, an existing component recipe, or an existing gesture over anything invented for one component — a bespoke treatment reads as imposed even when each individual value is defensible. (2) **Would it date?** Travelling gloss/shine, stacked bevels, decorative diagonals and oversized background glyphs read as a specific year; a surface, a rule and type do not. (3) **Does it suit every category?** The same page must look right for a tool shop, a fashion boutique and a grocery — a coloured frame or a seasonal flourish fails this while a plain surface passes. (4) **Is the colour already spoken for?** Each accent has ONE job (`--color-sale`: the sale price, badge and banner share one green so a shopper learns the mark once); spending it on decoration dilutes the signal it exists to carry. Restraint here IS the design, not an absence of it. The worked example is `.store-sale` in utilities/utils.css — eight bespoke treatments tried and discarded before it became `.card` plus a green number, each failure listed there so the path is not re-walked.
- **A shadow means the surface FLOATS above the page — nothing else earns one** (elevation rule, 2026-08-01/08-02). Dropdown/modal/toast/drawer keeps one at rest. A BAR does not: header and glass filter row are border-only — a value difference or a backdrop-blur already says which layer is in front, and `--shadow-bar` was deleted for want of a consumer. Anything in normal flow — card, tile, hero image, chip, row — is border + `--color-surface`, depth on **hover** only if clickable; pressed = fill + border + weight. A TEXT FIELD focuses by border-colour alone, never a ring. Rationale: `base/tokens.css`, `components/forms.css`; guarded by `design-shadow-rule` + `field-focus-consistency`.
- **Backward-compatible API/DB — always additive (zero-downtime deploy rule).** Old and new versions run against the same DB for a few seconds during a deploy, so every `/api/*` response shape and every schema change must keep the *previous* version working: add fields/columns, never remove or repurpose one the deployed code still reads. A real breaking change is two deploys (stop reading it, then remove it); a breaking API needs `/api/v2/`, not an edit in place. Full deploy-safety context: `GO_LIVE_CHECKLIST.md` §7.
- **Scalability** — stateless API routes; no shared write state. Ask: breaks at 1000 sellers + 10,000 buyers? Schema, indexes, edge cases, measured load: `DB_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`. **Anything the header computes is the cost of the SITE** — it renders on every page a signed-in seller loads, so its indicators (notif badge, avatar alert dot via `sellerHasAnyAlert`) are three multi-store statements no matter how many stores, and must never go back to a per-store loop: a shape the old file cache absorbed is one a ten-connection pool does not. **Independent reads on a click path go in one `Promise.all`** — sequential `await`s are sequential round trips now, and it only actually parallelises through the pool (a single `Client` serialises them, and a measurement written that way will wrongly report "no faster").
- **Inventory integrity** — `decrementStock(productId,qty)` is ONE statement: `UPDATE ... SET stock = stock - qty WHERE id = ? AND stock >= qty`, and the affected-row count IS the answer (0 = reject). No lock, correct on any number of servers — proved 2026-08-03 with 50 concurrent buyers of one unit (`DB_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` §7.5/§9.5; the old process-local mutex is deleted). **A REFUSAL, though, must re-read the count** — the statement's opening snapshot is stale under contention and that number is what the buyer's page clamps to; why, in `store-products.ts#stockAfterRefusal`. Payment webhooks: idempotency check on `paymentRef`.
- **Money & reported numbers — the defence layers are mechanical, don't rebuild them by hand (2026-07-29).** Five modules own the rules and nothing may re-implement them, each header carrying its own: **`lib/money.ts`**, **`lib/business-day.ts`**, **`lib/order-status-rules.ts`**, **`lib/orders.ts#countsAsRevenue`**, **`lib/refund-owed.ts`**. `tests/money-guards.test.ts` + `tests/money-owed-guards.test.ts` **scan the whole tree**, not a file list, so a brand-new module is covered the moment it exists; adding a file to an allowlist means you just created a second definition of the rule. **A new seller/admin-visible number needs an invariant in `tests/reporting-invariants.test.ts`**, plus a case in `tests/reporting-fuzz.test.ts` if weird input can reach it. Every money-moving endpoint needs an idempotency key (`lib/checkout-idempotency.ts`) and a journal entry (`lib/money-events.ts`). **What each of those modules is FOR, and the trap in each, arrives ON CONTACT — `.claude/hooks/money-rules-on-contact.sh` injects the full rules the first time a session edits a money file** (only 1 session in 5 ever does; it carries the measurement). Live cross-check of the real data: `lib/reconcile.ts`. Full context: memory `project_metric_integrity_audit`.
- **Testing** — **`npm run verify` after every code step, and it is the ONLY way to run these checks** (`scripts/verify.mjs`): picks the checks the diff needs, runs them concurrently off their caches, skips any whose inputs are byte-identical to the last time it passed (~0.3s instead of ~70s; `--no-cache` forces), prints only failures, names everything it did not run. **`astro check`, `tsc` and a whole `vitest run` by hand are BLOCKED** (`one-way-to-verify.sh`, 2026-08-09 — 41 minutes of duplicated checks in the five days before it); one test file while iterating is still fine. **`npm run verify -- --all` forces everything** — what the Stop hook runs, and what you run before claiming done, because a check skipped for a file committed earlier in the session is still a check that did not run. Its header holds the rest: the two known `astro check` false-positives (read it before "fixing" a `ts(6133)` on a `const` before an `Astro.redirect`), how to locate a `.astro` compile error that reports no file or line, and the `let`-in-`.forEach` narrowing trap. Vitest set up (`tests/`, default `node` env, per-file `// @vitest-environment jsdom` only where DOM/localStorage is touched, `npm test`). **Standing policy: any change that moves money or decrements stock needs a Vitest test in the same change** — not "extra scope"; applies to `orders.ts`, `cart.ts`, `/api/checkout`, `store-products(-bulk).ts`, `/api/store-product/bulk`, and any future money/stock code. Playwright installed (chromium) but reserved for money/inventory/multi-surface bugs, or a UI/interaction claim unverifiable by reasoning — one-off repro scripts, not a committed suite; not for routine changes.
- **Lint (ESLint + SonarJS)** — part of `npm run verify`; `npm run lint` runs just this one. It reports **only what the change introduced**, measured against `.eslint-baseline.json` (the frozen pre-existing backlog), so clean output is the pass condition and the backlog can never block anything. **Never regenerate the baseline (`lint:baseline`) to silence a new error, and never let a diff ADD a suppression line** — that is the one move that makes the gate meaningless; it may only ever shrink. **`eslint.config.js`'s header holds the rest**: the baseline's count and how it got honest, `lint:all`/`lint:prune`, and why the config is tuned narrow (turn a noisy rule off THERE with the reason beside it, rather than learning to skim past it). CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`: astro check → lint → test → build) reports; the `pre-push` hook GATES, running `npm run verify` and refusing a red push — CI runs ON the pushed commit, so it can never be green *before* a push.
- **Security review gate** — before closing any session whose diff touched money/auth/inventory/cart/checkout code, review that diff and report what was checked. **Enforced automatically, not remembered:** the `Stop` hook `require-review.sh` blocks the turn until a review is recorded for that exact diff — invoke the `review-diff` skill (the repo's own bug-class checklist), fix what it finds, then `bash .claude/hooks/record-review.sh`. The hooks carry the surface list (`SENSITIVE_RE`), the fingerprinting and the escape hatch. **`/code-review` cannot be launched by me** — it and `ultra` are user-triggered only; say it is theirs to run, never claim it ran. **Two standing members of this surface, both because a duplicated check rots:** any request-supplied redirect destination (`?next=`, a cookie, a `Referer`) goes through `lib/safe-redirect.ts`; any signature/password/token comparison through `secretsEqual()` in `lib/secret-compare.ts`, never `===`. Both module headers carry what went wrong and why; their tests grep `src/` so the next hand-rolled copy fails the suite instead of shipping.
- **Production monitoring — built (2026-08-05).** Four capture surfaces, one owner each: page frontmatter → middleware, anything rendered after it → `lib/stream-errors.ts`, the browser → `scripts/error-reporter.ts`, unawaited promises → `lib/process-errors.ts`. Severity gates the one channel that interrupts a person (`lib/critical-alert.ts`). Two owner steps at launch, in GO_LIVE: `ALERT_EMAIL` (§4), an uptime monitor on `/api/health` (§1).
- **Product reviews — not built.** When built, buyer review text runs through the same spam/keyword-stuffing filter as seller product/category content — a free-text UGC surface needs that automatic gate, not a moderation queue.
- **Self-healing errors — not built.** Auto-retry with backoff for transient idempotent failures; the plan, the trigger and the never-retry-a-charge trap are in `lib/outbound-fetch.ts`, where the policy would go.
- **Accessibility (WCAG 2.1 AA)** — skip link, focus traps on modals, Escape closes overlays, ARIA labels/roles/`aria-expanded`/`aria-live`, visible labels, 4.5:1 contrast, never color-only state. **Two traps, invisible unless you drive it by keyboard (2026-08-01):** an arrow key names a direction ON SCREEN, so RTL mirrors it — always `src/lib/arrow-step.ts`, never inline; and a scroller clips the focus ring drawn 4px outside, so the offset goes negative (`.dash-tab:focus-visible`, dashboard.css, holds why here and not padding). Both grep-guarded.
- **SEO** — `BaseLayout → Seo.astro` always. JSON-LD Product on product pages, Store/LocalBusiness on store pages. Unique title+description per page. **Site-level coverage is a SEPARATE check from per-page tags — enforce it:** every public indexable route needs a crawl path. SSR routes are invisible to `@astrojs/sitemap` (build-time only); `/sitemap-content.xml` is a pre-built `<sitemapindex>` over shards, never built per request. **When a route flips static↔SSR or a new public route type appears, re-verify against the BUILT output (`grep <loc> dist/client/sitemap*.xml` + curl), not the code** — silent class: valid sitemap, no error, missing URLs. **Slugs carry Hebrew** (product AND store, `url-base.ts#toSlug`), so any URL handed to a MACHINE (sitemap `<loc>`, feed `<link>`, canonical, og:image, IndexNow) percent-encodes each segment via `urlSegment`; an in-page `href` needs nothing. A raw Hebrew `Location` THROWS a 500 — `Astro.params` arrives DECODED — so every interpolated `redirect()` uses `url-base.ts#machineUrl`. One product has ONE public URL and ONE catalog id whoever asks: ad-event id = `ad-item-id.ts`, and outbound values obey the platform's published limits. Guarded tree-wide by `tests/external-contract.test.ts`, which fails on a feed attribute it does not know.
  **A URL published to Google/Meta also answers to their ACCOUNT rules (2026-08-06)** — one Merchant Center for the whole platform, so a policy failure is every seller's ads at once. Every feed URL stays on the platform domain and neither redirects nor canonicals off it (`custom-domain.ts#AD_LANDING_PARAM`); a link the site renders everywhere must resolve — a 404 terms/contact is the "misrepresentation" class that suspends the ACCOUNT. Private pages: `noindex` + sitemap-excluded (config `filter`). Canonical is query-stripped + trailing-slash-free (`trailingSlash:'never'`). **`src/pages/robots.txt.ts`: SSR, host-dependent — never a `public/` copy.** A custom domain's www/apex twin 301s to the claimed one.
- **i18n** — all strings via `getT(lang)`, Hebrew-first, RTL, server-side `lang` conditionals for icon placement. `/terms` is the one exception (its header says why). **A wording pass goes through `scripts/copy-review.mjs`, never by hand.**
- **Ads — two-tier model (decided 2026-07-06, budget shape 2026-07-16):** (1) **Baseline**, platform-funded — every product/store by default in a platform-wide feed powering Google Performance Max / Meta Advantage+ Catalog, on a **fixed one-time lifetime budget, not a recurring subsidy** (native in both ad managers, so spend auto-stops at the cap). Feed endpoint built — Features built item 14. (2) **Boost**, seller-funded — a **separate dedicated campaign** per store/product (`adCampaigns`, Google Ads/Meta Marketing API, billed through the split-payment provider), because a catalog campaign cannot weight one product inside a shared one. Either tier: sellers never touch **creative/fine-tuning**; a boost seller picks budget, **duration** (7/14/30 days or ongoing) and a broad **audience** (`AdCampaign.audience` = gender + age_group, auto-derived by `audience-infer.ts`, whose header carries the who-it-is-FOR vs who-it-is-SHOWN-to trap). Every other Merchant/Catalog attribute and the 5 stable positional `custom_labels` are derived zero-touch (`product-feed.ts#buildProductFeedAttributes`, `product-labels.ts#deriveProductLabels` — derivation in their headers); nobody ever types a label. One GTM + one Meta Pixel for the whole platform — what makes tier 1 possible with zero seller opt-in. `dataLayer` provides `store_id`, `ecommerce.items`.
- File content in English. Chat in Hebrew.

---

## Features built
<!-- REFERENCE INDEX — do NOT read linearly at session start. grep for the one feature a task touches (each bullet starts `- **<Name>:**`). Bullets hold only decisions / gotchas / "don't redo" + file pointers — anything a grep+Read of the code answers does NOT belong here. -->
- **Auth:** Unified seller+buyer accounts; register = name+email+password; `?next=` on all flows. Google OAuth (`/api/auth/google`+callback): find by googleId → email+link → create; `Seller.googleId?`, empty `passwordHash` for OAuth (env `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`/`GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`). **That middle step is an account takeover unless Google itself verified the address, and the check was missing until 2026-08-06** — `verified_email` was fetched from userinfo and never read, so an account claiming a seller's address linked straight into it and took their stores, orders and revenue figures. Refused on `!== true` (an omitted field has verified nothing either); `tests/oauth-verified-email.test.ts` drives the real callback. Session cookie TTL 180 days. Cart/wishlist persistence design: memory `project_cart_auth_session`. **Secret comparison is centralised (2026-07-30, `lib/secret-compare.ts`)** — five call sites compared a signature or password with `===`, which stops at the first differing character and so leaks how much of a guess was right; one of them (`checkCredentials`) also short-circuited on the username. **Admin auth was the real hole, same date:** `src/lib/auth.ts` was a SECOND admin login that nothing imported, and its existence made `.env.example` document `ADMIN_USERNAME`/`ADMIN_PASSWORD` — read by nothing — while the live gate `ADMIN_SECRET` was undocumented and defaulted to `'admin'` with no production guard, so a careful operator would still have launched with the password `admin`. Dead module deleted; ADMIN_SECRET now hard-fails in PROD like `AUTH_SECRET` already did, and the cookie carries a signed expiry instead of the secret. Don't re-add a username: there isn't one. **Both of those guards were unreachable until 2026-07-30, and this is the more important half:** they read `import.meta.env.AUTH_SECRET`/`ADMIN_SECRET`, which is a build-time TEXT SUBSTITUTION, not a lookup — a build made without the variable baked in `undefined`, the constant folder deleted the check, and the shipped bundle held `function secret(){ throw }`, so EVERY request touching a session threw on the live server no matter what its environment said (the visitor just got a redirect). Measured on a real `astro build`, while GO_LIVE §7 promised the opposite. All 11 server variables now go through `lib/runtime-env.ts` (`process.env` at runtime; `.env` is loaded for the dev server from astro.config.mjs), and `tests/runtime-env.test.ts` scans `src/` so the direct form can't return. **`npm start` was the second door into the same failure:** it aliased `astro dev` — the command a host runs by default — where `import.meta.env.PROD` is false and so every missing secret silently takes its dev fallback; it now serves the build behind a `prestart` gate (`scripts/check-required-env.mjs`) that refuses to boot without real secrets. **Google sign-in is offered only when it can complete** (`lib/google-oauth.ts`): both pages rendered the button unconditionally while the route answered a bare `503`, so on an environment missing the credentials the most prominent button on the login page was a dead end. Requires id AND secret; with only the id the seller reaches Google and fails on the way back. **Configured ≠ usable (2026-08-12):** the credentials now exist and the flow was exercised end to end, but the Google consent screen is still in **Testing**, where only hand-listed test users may sign in — console state no code can see, so a working button proves the wiring and nothing about who can get in. Switch to production + the prod redirect URI: GO_LIVE §1. ⚠️ `lib/google-oauth.ts`'s own header still says `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` "has never been set", which is now false — correct it next time that file is open (a worktree hook refused the edit at session close 2026-08-12). **Sign-in is rate-limited (2026-08-04, `lib/rate-limit.ts` + `auth_attempts`, migration 0009):** seller login, seller register and admin login, checked BEFORE the password is verified (the cost defended against is bcrypt's) and cleared by a correct password. Two buckets per attempt — per-account (8/15min, the primary defence) and per-origin (30, loose because shared NAT is real sellers); admin gets 5, one shared secret with no second factor. The counter is in Postgres and NOT in the process: a module-scope Map doubles the ceiling per instance and resets on every deploy, the same argument that moved `checkout-idempotency` off its Mutex. `lib/client-ip.ts` trusts a forwarded-for header ONLY under `TRUST_PROXY_IP` — it is a claim, not a fact, and both misconfigurations fail quietly (its header says how). Bucket keys are length-capped: an unbounded email made the primary key exceed the btree limit and returned 500 to anyone who asked. `tests/rate-limit.test.ts` also greps `src/` so a future credential surface cannot ship unthrottled. **Cross-site request forgery has THREE layers, and knowing which is which is the point — one is easy to remove by accident (2026-08-05):** (0) `sameSite:'lax'` session cookies, so a forged POST arrives unauthenticated; (1) Astro's `security.checkOrigin`, already on by DEFAULT and now pinned in `astro.config.mjs` because that default is conditional on the build output still being `server`, i.e. on at least one route still saying `prerender = false` — it could have switched itself off with no error and no diff to point at; (2) `lib/csrf.ts`, a signed token BOUND to the session identity, so one minted in the attacker's own browser is refused against a victim's (unbound, a token only proves the sender once visited the site, which an attacker does for themselves). Enforced in `src/middleware.ts` and nowhere else; attached by one `fetch` wrapper (`scripts/csrf-client.ts`, loaded first from BaseLayout's head) plus `<CsrfField />` in the six forms the browser really submits, so sign-in still works with JS off. The one planned exemption is the payment webhook, which must bring its own signature. Two defects the existing guards caught while it was written: an uncapped `clone()` read, and `cancel()` on a tee'd reader never settling — which would have hung the middleware on any oversized form POST.
- **Off-site database backup (2026-08-09, `scripts/backup-db.mjs` + `.github/workflows/backup.yml`):** daily `pg_dump` -> gzip -> Cloudflare R2, signed with hand-written SigV4 (`scripts/lib/s3-put.mjs`, no new dependency). Runs in GitHub Actions, NOT on the app server — so it survives the hosting choice, and a backup does not depend on one laptop being awake. **Why it exists: Neon Free keeps 6 hours of PITR** (their plan page, checked 2026-08-09) and that history dies with the account, while `data/*.json` and `db:import` were deleted on purpose. Verification is a READ-BACK + SHA-256, because both cheaper checks are traps measured against the real bucket (see `s3-put.mjs`). Refuses to upload a dump under 20KB — `pg_dump` can exit 0 having written only a header. **Neon runs Postgres 18.4, measured** (a mismatch failed the first run), and `PG_DUMP_PATH` names the versioned binary because `/usr/bin/pg_dump` is a wrapper that picked 16 anyway. ⚠️ Still owner-side: the five repo secrets and the first manual run — GO_LIVE §6.
- **Visitor-detail retention (2026-08-09, `lib/visitor-retention.ts`, job `purge-visitor-detail`):** 400 days on the only two tables that grew with TRAFFIC (`analytics_visitors`, `store_page_view_visitors` — one row per person per day, nothing deleting them). Aggregates (`store_page_views`, `analytics_daily`) are kept FOREVER, so an older range still reports views in full and only uniques read 0 — plus a conversion rate then computed over loads rather than people, which the admin note says out loud (`perfRetentionNote`, on BOTH admin performance screens, never the seller's). The purge SKIPS `AUX_EVENTS`: `getLifetimeEventSessions` is unbounded by design, and the obvious repair — snapshot then add — is wrong because distinct counts do not add.
- **Graceful shutdown (2026-08-04, `lib/shutdown.ts`):** `SIGTERM`/`SIGINT` drains in-flight requests (10s deadline) and closes the pool instead of dying instantly, which is what Node does by default and what cut every mid-deploy `/api/checkout`. Installed lazily from `middleware.ts` for the same reason the scheduler is — `@astrojs/node` standalone owns the HTTP server and exposes it to no code in `src/` (checked against 11.0.2: it wires `server-destroy` but listens for no signal). Consequence, stated rather than hidden: it DRAINS, it cannot close the listener, and the slot is released when the `Response` is returned, not when its body finishes streaming — so a streaming page can still be cut (a blank tab) while every mutation, whose DB work completes inside the handler, is covered.
- **Dashboard:** Tabs overview (default landing) / products / orders / performance / advertising / messages / settings — `initDashTabs()` + `data-goto-panel`/`initGotoPanelLinks()` (`ui.ts`, shared with admin `tab-nav.ts`). **A tab marker scrolled off the strip is flagged on the edge you would scroll toward** (`tab-alert-edges.ts`, both dashboards) — on a phone the strip always overflows, and a low-stock or unread-messages tab off the side was unknowable. A new marker joins by declaring `data-tab-alert` on itself; nothing else knows which tab means what. AJAX product CRUD, inline cell editing, bulk edit/upload/delete (memory `project_dashboard_bulk`), kebab menu (view/edit/hide-show/delete/PQV); 401 on an AJAX save shows a translated session-expired message. **Bulk selection survives a re-render (סשן ד׳, `scripts/dashboard/bulk-selection.ts`):** the table is server-paginated, so every filter/sort/search/page rebuilds the tbody — the fresh checkboxes came back unticked while the toolbar's own closure kept counting, so bulk delete still targeted rows nobody could see. What is counted and acted on is now always the ticked rows currently RENDERED (`selectedRowIds()`), while ids a filter hid are remembered and return ticked when it's cleared; "בחר הכל" is a MODE — armed, every row the next view renders joins in; unticking one row disarms it; unticking the box clears everything including hidden ids; a bulk delete clears it so the replacement rows aren't swept up. `promotions.ts` reads the same helper, never the checkboxes. **Filtered to nothing (סשן ד׳):** the table STAYS and says so in a row inside it (`#products-empty-row`, mirrored SSR + client) — hiding it stranded the seller with the header's own filter funnels gone plus the "add your first product" copy; "a filter is active" counts every column's filter, not just category. **"ללא קטגוריה" (סשן ד׳):** goes over the wire as `NO_CATEGORY_TOKEN` — the empty path it really means is what `decodeList()` drops, so the filter arrived empty and read as no-filter (picking it did nothing). **Thumbnails survive the rebuild:** a rebuilt row adopts the already-decoded `.thumb-wrap` when the src matches, instead of a new `<img>` replaying skeleton→load→decode and flashing the whole table on every filter. **Tab loading (2026-07-21):** every tab is SSR-rendered up-front and switching just toggles `hidden` — EXCEPT performance + advertising, which are lazy (their SSR data is computed only when that's the landing tab via `?panel=`; otherwise a skeleton renders and the panel fetches on its first `dashtab:show`). **The JS loads in two stages (2026-07-31) — do not undo it with a static import.** The page's `<script>` statically imports the SHELL only (ui/tab-sync/unsaved-guard/discount-field); every panel is one entry in a `LOADERS` map, dynamically imported when that panel is first shown, the rest on idle. Blocking JS went 245 KB→30 KB raw (61→13 gz); a new panel module belongs in its `LOADERS` entry, and order INSIDE one still matters (see the products entry). What is left un-answerable meanwhile is handled honestly rather than hidden: `<DashTabsBoot />` runs inline so tabs switch with zero JS downloaded, remembers a menu trigger tapped in a panel that is still `[data-hydrating]` and clicks it again once that panel is wired (only `[aria-haspopup]`, only while the panel is still on screen — replaying a save would not be safe), and marks it `[data-opening]` so it pulses immediately instead of reading as a dead button; `#dash-hydrating-bar` (`.progress-sweep`, utils.css) says the tab is still arriving, fades in only after 250 ms so a fast load never flashes it, and leaves its 2 px track behind so removal shifts nothing. **Numbers never show a fake 0 (2026-07-31):** an unfetched KPI renders `<SkelBar />`, and a RANGE SWITCH blanks every figure back to its placeholder after 180 ms (`showRangePending`, keyed off `data-skel`) — the old range's revenue used to sit there labelled as the new one. A failed load ALWAYS says so (toast) and puts the picker back to the range the figures on screen belong to — restoring last month's numbers under the new range's label is the same misreading the placeholders exist to prevent, and the recovery must not be gated on that 180 ms timer (a fast 500 left the SSR skeletons shimmering forever, silently); no prior summary = "—" plus the toast. **A panel chunk that never arrives is the same rule one level up:** every control in that tab is permanently dead, so `hydrate()` toasts `dashPanelLoadFailed` when it is the visible tab (console-only was silence the seller could not act on). **The trap this class hides in:** `.skeleton-shimmer` is the @keyframes name and has never been a class, so three chart slots and the campaign list rendered blank boxes — a loading state that looks exactly like "no data" is invisible on the page; `tests/skeleton-classes.test.ts` now fails on any undefined `skel*` class. **Tab-strip overflow (2026-07-21):** an overflowing `.dash-tabs` gets wheel-to-horizontal scroll (**RTL-sign-corrected**), the active tab scrolled into view, and two **opaque white** edge fades — a mask fading to transparent showed the scrolling page through the sticky bar. Each fade must also span the strip's own inline padding (`--dash-tab-pad`) or tabs peek out beside it. **Multi-store switcher:** alert dot only for a store *other than* the open one, and it carries EVERYTHING that shop waits on at the severity `seller-alerts.ts` assigns (2026-08-12, owner) — red for a person waiting (order, mail), amber for a wrong state (low stock, a boost paused for a reason that will not undo itself), danger winning where both are true; amber deliberately stops here and never reaches the header avatar, which would then be lit permanently. **Its live half reads the current store's level off the TAB STRIP, never off the panels** — nine of ten panels only arrive on the click that opens them, so `.order-new-dot` matches nothing on any other tab whether or not orders wait, and counting that silence as "nothing" already cleared a dot unread MAIL was holding up. The strip is always rendered and each panel's script maintains its own marker there, so it is the one surface that is both present and current; `PER_STORE_TABS` names which tabs count, because the Payments dot is account-wide and would otherwise dot every row. Last store in a `seller_last_store` cookie (180d); it renders even for a single-store seller because it's the ONLY entry point to "פתיחת חנות נוספת" (`?new=1` → the same `CreateStoreCard.astro` an empty account gets — one form, two entry points); capped at `MAX_STORES_PER_SELLER`=5, enforced server-side in the create POST (`canOpenAnotherStore`), not just by hiding the link. **Seller onboarding (2026-07-27):** `OnboardingChecklist.astro` (5 steps + progress) on Overview + a one-line `TabHint.astro` above every panel — both **state-free**, derived live by `seller-onboarding.ts`, so there is no onboarding record to migrate. Checklist self-hides when complete; hints additionally require `allStores.length===1`. Its `product` step counts **visible** products only, mirroring the readiness gate. **The path had to actually END somewhere (2026-08-02):** the required "add your first product" row switched tabs and left the add form collapsed behind a six-button toolbar, so `data-goto-open` (handled by the same `initGotoPanelLinks`) now opens the named control too, and the empty-catalog block carries a real CTA instead of the words "add the first one above". The add form itself showed ELEVEN fields when only `name` is required — SKU/tags/variants/specs/private-note moved behind a `<details>` (photos stay out, they decide the listing), the "only the name is required" line is stated, and the four terms a first-time seller does not know carry an `InfoTip`. That exposed `initInfoTooltips()` running ONLY from the advertising/performance tabs — every other `data-tooltip` on the dashboard, including the stock badge's, was inert markup. **Product hide/show (2026-07-20):** `StoreProduct.hidden?` — a seller-owned reversible take-down that rides the same `isProductVisible` gate as admin `blocked` (`/api/product` `set-product-visibility`). **Products-tab stock badge:** counts on-sale products at/under `LOW_STOCK_THRESHOLD` via `countStockAlerts()` (one source of truth) — a deliberate take-down never nags; every mutating `/api/product` action returns the fresh store-wide count so it stays live without reload. **Settings-tab save scope (2026-07-26):** the sticky `.dash-panel-head` (save button) is kept INSIDE `<form id="settings-form">` on purpose so it rides only over the fields it saves; the Categories tree + Custom-domain sections sit BELOW the form and save LIVE per-action, each badged "נשמר אוטומטית". Lifting the head out to span the whole panel was tried and reverted — it makes the save button appear to govern the auto-saving sections (memory `feedback_save_model_clarity`). **Two "addresses" in Settings (2026-07-27):** **כתובת פיזית** (field + its show-on-store-page toggle) vs **כתובת האתר** (`storeUrlTitle`, the slug section) — they used to share one Hebrew label; keep any new address copy on those two terms. **The store URL accepts Hebrew as an OPTION (2026-08-02, owner):** Latin stays the suggestion (placeholder `my-store`), the field no longer deletes Hebrew, and `storeUrlHebrewNote` under BOTH forms (create + rename) states the trade-off so the seller decides — Google shows it as-is, some apps paste it as a long encoded run. Input is `dir="auto"`, the preview stays `dir="ltr"` (an address reads host-then-path). Requiring a second latin field was considered and REJECTED: `store.name` already carries the display name, and buyers click store links rather than type them — a latin alias in `previousSlugs` was the fallback and was rejected too, as machinery for an edge case. **Cross-script lookalikes (`аdmin` with a Cyrillic а) are handled at the RESERVED level ONLY** (`slug-confusable.ts` → `isReservedSlug` matches the skeleton): mixing scripts stays legal because `shop-ישראל` is a normal Israeli slug, and seller-vs-seller impersonation is a report/rule-violation matter, not a character filter. Both rejected rules (no-mixing, lookalike-of-another-store) were built and then removed on that decision — don't rebuild them without asking. Products header is sticky under the site header (desktop) and unsticks while an edit row is open in favour of that row's own header; `.dash-head` sticky was tried+reverted (2026-07-15) — don't rebuild without asking. **Mobile toolbars (2026-07-26):** products/orders/messages collapse search + page-size + sort/filter onto ONE `.dash-toolbar` row ≤640, sort/filter icon-only. Every control takes its height from one `--dash-ctl-h` on the toolbar — **never size one off its own content**: label-less pills hold a 12px icon and collapse to ~25px beside a ~34px search field; icon-only pills take `min-width` (not `width`) so the filter pill can still stretch for its active-count badge. Products page-size is the app dropdown via `initSelectDropdown()` (`select-dropdown.ts`) with the native select kept as value source, so `initPagination`'s `change` listener is untouched. Profitability commission is shown parenthesised + muted, never red/−. **"צפה בחנות" round trip (2026-07-28):** one reusable preview tab — a NAMED target opened via `window.open` (`initStorePreviewLink`), **never `_blank`+`rel="noopener"`**, which makes the browser ignore the name and pile up a tab per click (measured; mobile hides tabs so nobody notices). The store page gives its owner (`userId===sellerId`) a back bar that closes that tab and returns to the still-open dashboard — iOS has no Back out of a link-opened tab. **It is the live store, not a preview mode (סשן ג׳):** the page a seller sees on his own store is exactly the shopper's; the ONE difference is that buying is refused — `own-store-guard.ts` client-side, `/api/checkout` 403 `own-store` server-side — because a completed self-purchase is a real order (stock decremented, commission charged, mail to both sides, units feeding the performance labels behind the Google/Meta feed). `OwnerStoreBar.astro` is wayfinding only, which is why its copy avoids the word "תצוגה מקדימה". **Dashboard open in several tabs (סשן ו׳):** two mechanisms, deliberately separate. `tab-sync.ts` is the ambient half — it observes `fetch` ONCE (not ~20 save sites, a list that rots) and broadcasts every successful dashboard mutation. A receiving tab **updates itself live when it can and speaks up when it can't**. Products/Orders/Messages each hand tab-sync their own already-existing re-fetch (`registerPanelRefresh(panelId, applyXPagination)` — a registry, because those functions are closures over each tab's state and because re-running the very function the toolbar uses is what keeps a cross-tab refresh from drifting from a normal one: page, search, sort, filters, selection and decoded thumbs all survive). Only the ACTIVE panel is refreshed — quietly updating a hidden one would suppress the notice a seller reading a stale Settings tab needs — and only while `isBusy()` is false: unsaved-guard's exported `hasUnsavedChanges`, focus in any input/textarea/select, anything `aria-expanded="true"` (expanded order card, open thread, open menu — one marker, no second source of truth), an open edit row / add box / bulk panel, a `.toolbar-portal` or a `dialog[open]`. Otherwise `StaleDataBar.astro` goes up and the seller picks the moment. Nothing is ever redrawn over work in progress; the notice is the fallback, not the default. (The refreshers' own GETs are outside `MUTATION_PATHS` — that is what stops two tabs refreshing each other forever.) `record-rev.ts` is the safety half and also covers a second device: the two forms that overwrite a record WHOLE (product edit, store settings) carry one revision **per field** in `data-base-rev`, and the server does a **three-way merge** — a field this tab didn't touch keeps what is stored, a field only it touched wins, and only a field both tabs set differently returns 409 `conflictFields`. So two tabs editing different fields of one product both get their way with no dialog at all. **`force=1` rides ALONGSIDE `baseRev`, never instead of it** — "save anyway" settles just the disputed field while the rest still merges; the first cut sent the whole stale form and silently reverted the other tab's work (caught by the user). **A conflict must only ever mean SOMEONE ELSE changed it:** every partial save that patches the still-rendered edit row (inline cell edit, per-combo stock, bulk image save) returns its new `rev` and `syncEditRowRev` puts it back on the form, or the seller's own inline edit warns him in a single tab. A record-wide `updatedAt` stamp was rejected for exactly that reason (it fires on writes the form doesn't own) plus needing a migration. **The order-edit modal solves the same problem the cheaper way** (סשן ו׳): `PATCH /api/seller/orders` already applies each field only when the request names it, so the modal snapshots the values it opened with (`eomOpenedWith`) and sends ONLY what changed — an untouched field is never mentioned, so there is nothing to revert and no dialog to answer; a same-field clash is last-write-wins there, deliberately. That exposed a real bug in the endpoint, now fixed + tested (`tests/seller-orders-partial.test.ts`): with `itemDeletes` present but no `discount` key, the subtotal rebuild wrote `discount: undefined` and **silently wiped a discount nobody had touched** — an omitted discount is now preserved and re-applied against the new base. A save with nothing changed closes the modal instead of posting (the endpoint would answer "no valid fields to update" — an error for changing your mind). **Unsaved-changes prompt (2026-07-28, `unsaved-guard.ts`):** one `beforeunload` for the whole dashboard, raised only when a `data-unsaved-guard` form's values differ from the baseline taken on first touch — so revert-to-original stays silent, and comparison uses the value **as the API would store it** (trimmed, numbers numeric) because one false alarm teaches the seller to dismiss the real one. Panels switch client-side, so tab-switching never loses anything and never prompts; a closed edit row / add box is skipped (Cancel discards anyway), while `.dash-panel[hidden]` still counts. Settings stays on screen after saving, so its save path fires `dash:saved` to retake the baseline. **If the dashboard's JS dies entirely (2026-07-29, `FormFallbackGuard.astro`):** the whole dashboard is ONE module graph, so a single failed import kills all seven AJAX submit handlers at once — and the forms still carry `method="POST" action="/api/…"`, so the browser would fall back to its own submit, navigate to raw JSON and wipe the screen. An `is:inline` guard (it cannot live inside the graph it protects) blocks that: `e.defaultPrevented===false` at document level is an exact test for "no handler ran", since every handler calls `preventDefault()` first thing. It then keeps the blocked form in localStorage and OFFERS it back on the next load — offers, never auto-restores, which would silently overwrite what the server just rendered. Snapshots come from the ELEMENTS, not `FormData` (which omits an unchecked box, so a checkbox the seller cleared would come back ticked); files/passwords never stored; restore fires `input`+`change` so the preview and the unsaved-guard baseline follow. **The blocked submit stopped being the only writer (2026-08-05, owner asked what this actually protects):** it is the RARE accident — a closed tab, a crashed browser and a machine that lost power run no line of our code on the way out — so a `data-unsaved-guard` form is now drafted continuously while edited (700 ms debounce, flushed on `visibilitychange`). Local only; nothing reaches the server without the seller pressing save. **A restore puts back only the fields he EDITED**, measured against the `base` snapshot taken when the page rendered: a dashboard form carries every field it owns, so restoring the whole photograph would write back untouched fields and silently revert a second tab — the lost update `record-rev.ts` prevents, arriving as a stale value that looks like typing. The "מחק אותם" button really deletes it, which is why it no longer says "התעלם". **A new dashboard form that genuinely needs a browser POST must carry `data-native-submit`** (today: header logout, `CreateStoreCard`) or it silently stops working. **And the no-JS fallback handlers those forms land on are REAL routes with real authority — they had none of the ownership checks their `/api/*` twins have (found + fixed 2026-08-06).** `dashboard.astro`'s own POST branches took the form's ids at face value: `add-product` filed a product into whatever `storeId` the body named, `edit-product` rewrote another store's name/**price**/**stock**, `delete-product` deleted it, and `save-settings` fell through `?? stores[0]` so an unrecognised id silently overwrote a different shop of the seller's. Every id now resolves through `lib/store-ownership.ts`, which is also the ONE definition the `/api/*` routes use — the previous shape was the same three lines copy-pasted per branch, so the branch that omitted them looked exactly like the ones that had them. When reviewing a dashboard surface, read the fallback branch as carefully as the endpoint: it is the half nobody opens. **Mobile is full-bleed (2026-08-02, `.dash-section--bleed`):** the card runs edge to edge (no radius, no side borders, no hover shadow) and its 0.8rem panel padding is the ONLY gutter — the page used to pay for one outside the card and another inside. The tab strip carries none; the tabs own padding stands in, and `--dash-tab-pad` must be set with the strip padding it drives (the edge fades size and pin themselves off it) and must never be a unitless `0` — three `calc()` go invalid at once and both fades vanish. **Every image box here shimmers while its photo is in flight (2026-08-07, owner):** order-card item thumbs, the order-edit modal's rows, the open edit row's header thumb, the Settings banner/logo previews and the product gallery's slots all carry `.dash-img-skel` + `data-skeleton` and go through `lib/img-skeleton.ts` — one rule in dashboard.css, not a copy per surface. The photo is lifted to `z-index:1` over the shimmer, so **anything layered over a photo must declare its own z-index** (the gallery slot's scrim/spinner/tick did not, and the photo covered all three). The order-card item row has THREE renderers and the post-save patch had already drifted from the other two (no border, no surface, so a save flattened every cut-out photo) — all three now come from `orderItemThumbHtml()`.
- **Icon controls explain themselves (2026-07-30, `scripts/icon-tooltips.ts`):** ONE delegated listener shows any icon-only control's own `aria-label` on hover (450ms, hover-capable pointers only), reusing the existing floating tooltip rather than adding a second mechanism. Nothing to remember per surface — give a new icon button the aria-label accessibility already wants and the hover label follows; that is the point, since ~100 such controls exist and a per-surface tooltip is the kind that rots. Four traps, all found by hovering, all covered by tests: a **count badge** inside the button (header bell/cart) and a hidden qty readout (product card add-to-cart) made a "does it have text?" test say "already labelled", so the rule compares the text against the LABEL; the dashboard toolbars keep their label span and hide it with CSS, so it must read `innerText` (layout-aware) not `textContent`, or exactly the buttons that go icon-only on mobile are the ones skipped; only `text.includes(label)` and never the reverse, because a sort button shows "מחיר" while its label is "מיין לפי מחיר" and that verb is the whole reason for the tooltip; and an open disclosure (`aria-expanded="true"`) is silent, since a dropdown rendered inside its trigger otherwise re-showed the trigger's tooltip over the open menu. **Every sort/filter header button now carries `aria-label` = "מיין לפי/סנן לפי <column>"** — it is the accessible name too, and the hearts column was a bare icon with no name at all. **The standing consequence: an `aria-label` is VISIBLE TEXT now**, so a hardcoded English one is a bug on a Hebrew-first site (7 were, in the lightboxes and the dashboard gallery); `tests/icon-tooltips.test.ts` scans for that, for an unlabelled sort/filter control, and for an i18n key present in one locale only. **A product card is an icon control by this test** (`<img>` inside a link/`role="button"`), so 2026-07-31 added the two ways its own caption still counted as "not on screen" and floated over the picture: the comparison now runs through `squash()` (drops whitespace + joining punctuation, so a label like "name — ₪120" matches a name and a price in two spans) and also against the `li`/`article`/`tr` the control sits in (the store card's photo is labelled with the name that is printed below it, outside the button). **A fifth, added by walking into it (2026-08-07): giving such a button a `title` to say the same thing REPLACES this tooltip with the browser's native grey box** — `candidate()` stands down for `[title]` by design, so the fix for "no tooltip here" is the aria-label, never a title. **Written down was not enough: on 2026-08-14 the owner reported it on the copy-address buttons, and 28 controls across the seller, buyer and admin trees had the same duplicate `title`/`aria-label` pair** — i.e. 28 buttons that had opted OUT of this tooltip by repeating themselves. All removed; on `CopyButton` it also means the label follows the swap to "הועתק" while the tick is up, which a static title never did. `tests/one-tooltip.test.ts` now walks the tree and bans the exact duplicate **on elements this listener can reach** (`button`/`a`/`summary`/`[role=button]` — the same predicate `candidate()` uses), which is a narrowed rule rather than an allowlist of excused files; it found five more the hand grep had missed. Two `title`s survive and are decisions: the products table's SEO column heading, whose content is a WORD not a glyph so this listener cannot cover it (it takes `data-tooltip`, the explicit opt-in), and the private-note chip, whose title is a note of up to 2000 characters that a 15rem tooltip would render as a wall.
- **Store categories = one shared vocabulary (2026-07-28, `lib/store-taxonomy.ts`):** the store-level tags that drive `/stores`' filter chips and the homepage's category shelves. Was a free-text comma field, which let one seller write "אלקטרוניקה" and the next "חשמל ואלקטרוניקה" — splitting the filter AND the shelf, so neither half reached the shelf minimum. Now a curated `SEED_CATEGORIES` list unioned with everything stores already use (most-used first), so a category one seller invents is offered to the next. `StoreCategoryPicker.astro` on BOTH the settings form and the create-store card. Adding a new one is allowed (no owner approval — zero-touch), guarded by `proposeCategory()`: unsafe wording is refused via the shared `spam-filter.ts` word list, and a label sharing a significant word offers the existing category first as a SOFT block (two clicks to insist). **Similarity is measured on WORDS, not the string** — the pair above shares no prefix; and a 3-letter shared word only counts when it's the whole label, or "כלי נגינה" gets flagged as "כלי עבודה" (both caught in-browser, not by tests). `sanitizeStoreCategories()` re-runs server-side on every save and on create — the picker is convenience, never the rule. **Icons (2026-07-29, `lib/category-icons.ts` + `CategoryIcon.astro`):** every platform category carries a hand-drawn SVG — on `/stores`' chips, and on a homepage category shelf heading where it REPLACES the rotating colour dot (`HomeShelf` prop `categoryLabel`; a non-category shelf keeps the dot). This is only possible because the vocabulary is closed; a seller-added label inherits the nearest seed's icon via the same `findSimilarCategories`, else a neutral tag. `ALIAS_ICONS` adds artwork for common NON-seed labels (צמחים/לגינה/יודאיקה/ציוד מחשבים/לילדים/מוזיקה/קוסמטיקה/טבע/נקיון/חד פעמי) — **icon coverage ≠ picker vocabulary**: an alias never adds to `SEED_CATEGORIES`, which is the user's product decision. A store's own free-text tree deliberately gets NO icons (memory `project_category_icons_scope`).
- **Store readiness gate (2026-07-27):** `store-readiness.ts` is the hard line between "still setting up" and "a shopper can buy here" — deliberately separate from the onboarding checklist, which is only guidance (memory `project_store_readiness_gate`). A store with **no visible product** is excluded from `/sitemap-content.xml` and from site search's store hits (filtered BEFORE the limit slice), and its page renders `noindex`. Deliberately **not** a 404 — the direct URL keeps working so the seller can preview/share. Self-pickup-without-address is NOT a blocker here (`/api/store` + both checkout paths already re-check `store.address`; re-gating it would be a second source of truth). Extension point: business/legal identity (ח.פ. / terms) should become a blocker here once those `Seller` fields exist — every surface then inherits it.
- **Dashboard sort/filter/search/pagination (2026-07-15):** Products/Orders/Messages tabs are server-paginated+searched (15/page; Products also gets a 10/20/50/100 page-size picker) via AJAX fetch + DOM patch, not full nav. `seller-{products,orders,messages}-query.ts` run search/sort/filter server-side before the shared `paginate()`; SSR page-1 and each AJAX endpoint call the same lib functions so results can't drift. Row-builders (`buildRows`/`buildOrderCard`/`buildMessageRow`) mirror server markup + re-bind delegated listeners. A single-row state change does a local recheck, not a full re-fetch. Filter-dropdown distinct values come from a server-embedded full list, **never scanned off DOM rows** — only one page's rows exist in the DOM. **The PAGER is ONE control, shared by all four paged lists** (products, orders, messages, the buyer's
orders — `scripts/dashboard/list-pager.ts`, 2026-08-14/15). Four copies had drifted apart and all
four carried the same complaints, so the rewrite is the fix and the guard test is what stops a fifth.
Shape: `‹ הקודם  עמוד [3 ▾] מתוך 12  הבא ›`, the middle being the site's own dropdown
(`select-dropdown.ts`) listing every page. It was a windowed strip of NUMBERS with `…` markers for a
day; the owner threw that out ("זה מסובך מדי למשתמש") and he was right — a windowed pager exists
because a page of links had to be crawlable, which a dashboard table is not. Deleting it removed a
window algorithm, a per-width slot count on a ResizeObserver, a jump field and a clamp rule, and the
control got BETTER: the strip could not reach page 14 of 40 in one click. **Don't re-propose
numbers.** Each tab mounts it TWICE, top and bottom, keyed by `data-list-pager`, one write filling
both — and on products the top one rides IN the search/page-size row (`.dash-toolbar--paged`, whose
`__controls` wrapper is `display:contents` above 640px so the existing `flex-1`/`ms-auto` keep
meaning what they meant, and a real nowrap row below it). Four rules that were each a reported bug:
the click AWAITS the fetch before it scrolls, and only when the list top is above the pinned chrome;
`createFetchGate()` lets only the newest press write and `abort()`s what it supersedes, because
every answer used to write `currentPage` and the last to land won (deliberately NOT a debounce —
"בן אדם לפעמים רוצים לדפדף מהר"); `markListBusy()` dims the outgoing rows IMMEDIATELY, not on
`LOADING_CUE_DELAY_MS`, since that threshold is for a cue that REPLACES content and behind it the
dim never appeared at all; and the `hidden` ATTRIBUTE does not hide these navs (it loses to `flex`)
— `empty:hidden` on the seller side, a `:empty` rule inside the buyer page's own UNLAYERED `<style>`
there, where a utility would have lost.
- **Dashboard sort/filter toolbar:** one pattern site-wide — desktop sort/filter buttons live in real table headers, mobile falls back to `.table-toolbar`; search sits outside it. Dropdowns render into a shared floating portal (`toolbar-portal.ts#createFloatingPortal`) — viewport-clamped, flips above when no room below, repositions (not closes) on scroll; also backs the order-status dropdown + performance range picker. **Outside-click uses `e.composedPath()`, never `target.contains()`** — a portal click that re-renders `innerHTML` can detach `e.target` mid-bubble. Filter state is `Map<col,Set<value>>` (OR within column, AND across). Products adds a **stock-status filter** (2026-07-21): synthetic אזל/נמוך/תקין buckets over the numeric stock column, resolved server-side in `seller-products-query.ts#stockBucket` (thresholds mirror `LOW_STOCK_THRESHOLD`) for both SSR + AJAX (`tests/seller-products-query.test.ts`). **Column-width lock (2026-07-28):** filtering re-renders the rows → an auto-layout table resizes its columns → the header slides out from under the (correctly stationary) portal. `table-column-lock.ts` pins the table's own width AND each column in px while a filter is active: take the lock at the top of the apply function, while the pre-change rows are still on screen; release it when the last filter goes; never on portal open/close. Pinning the columns *without* the table width still shifted it ~2px — an auto table may overflow its container to fit min-content, a fixed one may not. **A "clear filter" with nothing selected must be a no-op** (`toolbar-portal.ts#filterClearButtonHtml` + a guard in every handler); admin orders instead compares the URL it would navigate to, since its filters apply via `swapPanel`.
- **What the LAZY PANELS broke, and the three rules that hold it (2026-08-15/16).** One panel per
  request (2026-08-11) was the right change — ~865KB of HTML for one tab's answer — but it silently
  invalidated the assumption every client module was written under: *the whole page is in the
  document*. **Five bugs came out of it in five days and every one was found by the owner, not by
  us:** a product's edit form showing "ללא קטגוריה" and every client-rebuilt row losing its chip
  (the category tree island was inside the SETTINGS panel); the sale's category menu a dead button
  (the pickers were bound by a sweep that runs with the PRODUCTS chunk); creating a category posting
  an empty store id (read off an element in Settings); the overview's three attention tiles dead on
  the landing page (bound from the ORDERS module); and cross-tab live refresh silently declining
  forever (`isBusy` asked `!getElementById(x)?.hidden`, which on a MISSING element is `true`). The
  rules, one guard each: **data a client renderer needs belongs to the page, not to the panel that
  edits it** (`tests/dashboard-shared-data.test.ts`); **a control that arrives with a panel is wired
  by that panel's own chunk** (`tests/lazy-panel-wiring.test.ts`); **absent must never read as a
  state** — an optional element lookup is compared against a definite value, never negated
  (`tests/lazy-panel-reads.test.ts`). A tile that needs the target panel to DO something records a
  `panel-intent.ts` intent and lets that panel collect it, rather than reaching across.
- **Products table responsive — `table-layout:fixed` at EVERY width above the card breakpoint (641–999 and ≥1000, two width sets):** no column hides; every column gets an explicit `%` (or `rem`, for the cells holding a fixed-size widget: checkbox, thumbnail, kebab). The ≥1000 set was added 2026-08-05 because its absence was a bug: `auto` layout sizes a table to its own min-content, so the longest product name in the catalogue decided the width (983px, against a 918px panel at a 1000px viewport) and it overflowed the panel between ~1000 and ~1082px — appearing "suddenly", with no CSS change, the moment a seller adds a longer name. The breakpoint is not the bug, it is where the shares change: narrow, price/date cannot ellipsize readably so `name` yields; wide, `name` takes the most. Playwright-verified 641–1920px that table width == panel width. Memory `project_table_shrink_traps`. Mobile card (≤640px, reworked סשן ב׳): a 4-row × 2-col invisible-table grid so title↔stock, price↔purchased, category↔likes, date↔SKU line up row-for-row — the title is a single cell, NOT spanning both columns, which is what lets it share the top line with stock; equal 4-item columns mean no dangling empty cell. Likes sit under נרכשו, SKU stays bottom-corner, thumbnail fills the card height (`min(100%,96px)`). **Gotchas:** the ≤999px `%` widths need an explicit `width:auto` override on the card breakpoint's blanket `td` reset or cells shrink to a sliver; an inline `color:` needs `!important` on the mobile full-contrast override (inline beats stylesheet specificity); a fixed-size inline `<svg>` in a narrow `table-layout:fixed` cell is clamped by `reset.css`'s `svg{max-width:100%}` — pin such icons with `shrink-0 max-w-none`.
- **Discounts & sales (סשן א׳ — `lib/discounts.ts` is the ONE place a shown/charged price is derived):** exactly two levers. `StoreProduct.discount` (percent or ₪ off one product — the products-tab bulk "מבצע" dialog writes that same per-product record for N selected items, `/api/product` `bulk-discount`, scoped server-side to the seller's own store) and `Store.sale` (a store-wide banner with an OPTIONAL percent that applies only to products carrying no discount of their own). `resolvePrice()` takes the lowest of the two (tie → the product's own), returns `percentOff` + `showBadge` (the badge is the seller's opt-in, per discount and per sale), and is pure/isomorphic so client-built markup can't drift from the server.
