A sale's scope is the whole store, **one or more categories** (multi-pick 2026-07-29) each **plus its descendants**, or hand-picked products — flattened at SAVE time into `sale.categoryIds` (`store-sale-scope.ts`) because the resolver also runs in the browser, where the category tree isn't available. **Two category lists, never conflate them:** `pickedCategoryIds` = what a human chose (what the banner NAMES), `categoryIds` = that expanded downward (what a price is MATCHED against); read the first only via `salePickedCategoryIds()`, which falls back to the pre-multi-pick `categoryId` — still written as the first pick so a rolling deploy's old readers stay correct. `resolveSaleScope` drops foreign/duplicate ids and caps at `MAX_SALE_CATEGORIES`=8; deleting one of several picks narrows the sale instead of switching it off. **Every scope names itself on the banner, the widest one included** ("על כל החנות") — while only the narrow ones spoke, an unlabelled banner was ambiguous between "covers everything" and "the seller didn't say". One wording rule for banner + dashboard preview + picker label, in the pure isomorphic `sale-scope-label.ts`: 1 pick = full path, several = plain names, tail past 2 = `store.saleAndMore` ("ועוד N") since a 375px banner wraps. `initCategoryPicker` is configured by data attributes — `data-picker-multi="on"` (ticks, menu stays open, ids comma-joined into ONE hidden input; **`updateLabel()` must run BEFORE the `input` event** or a listener reading that label renders one click stale), `data-picker-add="off"` (creating a category is a CATALOG decision, not a side effect of running a sale), `data-picker-more-label`, plus a `picker:sync` event for when the consumer clears the field itself. Tests: `tests/sale-scope.test.ts`. Untrusted input is parsed apart in `discount-input.ts` (percent clamped 1–95 via `MIN/MAX_DISCOUNT_PERCENT`, ₪-off bounded by the price, free text length-capped) so the resolver stays pure math. Markup is generated as STRINGS (`price-html.ts`, `discount-field.ts`) rather than components, because half the surfaces build their HTML client-side (store "load more", PQV, store modal, header search, the products table's rebuilt edit rows). Seller UI: `PromotionsPanel.astro` + `promotions.ts` (מבצעים tab) and `StoreSaleBanner.astro` on the store page + every product page of that store. A stored cart re-prices against `/api/cart/prices` (`cart-prices.ts`) so a sale that started after an item was added shows before `/api/checkout` resolves the same numbers — a cart is a list of intents, never a price lock, and displaying one price while charging another is the one outcome this feature must not produce. **Re-pricing is tied to moments of ATTENTION, never a timer (2026-07-29):** page load, `visibilitychange` back to a left-open tab, a bfcache `pageshow`, drawer open, and — forced via `maxAge:0` — the pay button; in between, a completed answer is trusted for `FRESH_MS`=60s (stamped even on failure, so an offline tab doesn't hammer). Once-per-load was the original design and it left an hour-old tab quoting the price the sale had at load while `/api/checkout` silently charged the current one. `watchCartPrices()` is the shared installer: every surface registers its own re-render over one set of handlers and one in-flight request (a bfcache restore fires both events — the round is deduped so one return = one toast). **The safety is never paid for by the buyer — the reaction is scaled to the change, not to the fact that there was one:** `applyServerPrices` returns `CartPriceChange[]` (line, `from`→`to`), and the pay-button gate stops the charge ONLY via `hasIncrease(changes, isSelected)` — a line being bought NOW that got MORE expensive. A drop, or a rise on an unticked line, goes straight through; stopping on any movement would spend a real interruption on the common, welcome case. When it does stop it's a heads-up, not a failure: `#co-price-notice` in the warning tone, `role="status"`, button live, one more press pays. It also can't cost the purchase — it rides inside the existing "מעבד..." state and `CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS`=2500 aborts it, after which submit proceeds (the server re-derives regardless). Passive changes are announced, never swapped in silently: the drawer raises ONE toast site-wide (`cart.pricesUpdated` / `cart.pricesDropped`), and checkout stays quiet because `CartDrawer` is in `BaseLayout` and already on that page. Guarded by `tests/cart-prices.test.ts`. **Checkout tells the buyer what they saved:** struck-through original per line, a per-store "חסכת" strip (only with 2+ stores — with one it repeats the footer), and one saving row above the grand total; every figure is the real gap between the seller's own listed price and what is charged, never an invented compare-at. `CartItem.basePrice?` carries the pre-discount figure for DISPLAY only (money still comes from the server), and is deleted when a sale ends so nothing keeps a strikethrough over a price that is no longer a discount. **`formatPrice` shows agorot as two digits or none** (`49.50 ₪`, `100 ₪`) — a discount routinely lands on `.5`, and one trailing digit reads as a truncated number.
- **Store header logo (2026-08-09, owner) — an OPTION beside the name lockup, and the header still cannot move.** A seller may put their own logo at the top of their store instead of the avatar-and-name pair. Two columns, never one (`stores.header_logo` + `header_style`, migration 0021, read through `stores.ts#storeHeaderLogo`): uploading a logo does not adopt it and choosing the name back does not delete it, so the seller can compare without losing work. **Header-only** — every other surface (store cards, search rows, the saved-stores menu, emails) keeps reading `profileImage`, because each is a fixed square or circular slot and a logo's whole meaning is its aspect ratio. **The no-stretch guarantee is the BOX, not validation of the file**: `.store-header__brand` fixes `height:32px` (the avatar's own, so the store-page grid row lands where it does today) and `max-width:min(100%,11rem)` (a MOBILE number — at 375px the row is logo + search + actions), and the `<img>` is `object-fit:contain` with both `max-` rules — so an 8:1 banner renders 176×22, shorter and whole, rather than cropped or wider. `min-width:0` is what stops the img's intrinsic width becoming a min-content floor the flex column must honour. Delivery is `cdn.ts#cdnContain` (`c_limit` on BOTH dimensions — fits inside, never crops, never upscales); every other helper here FILLS, i.e. crops, which for a wordmark takes off the last letters. The store NAME survives the text being replaced: it is the link's `aria-label`, so screen readers and crawlers still get it. Seller UI: `HeaderStyleCard.astro` in Settings shows BOTH lockups as live previews built from the header's own classes (a preview from its own rules drifts silently — the store-sale banner taught this), with the logo radio disabled until an upload exists; behaviour in `scripts/dashboard/header-logo.ts`, which shares the uploader and the background worker with `store-image.ts` by import but has no crop flow, because a logo is never cropped. `/api/store` re-narrows `headerStyle` to `'name'` whenever no logo is stored — "logo selected, file removed" is the one combination the header cannot render. Guarded by `tests/store-header-logo.test.ts`.
- **Coupon codes (סשן ג׳, 2026-08-09) — a THIRD promotion, deliberately not a third price lever.** `lib/discounts.ts` decides what a product COSTS, and every consumer of that answer is public: the product page, the catalog, JSON-LD, the sitemap and the Google/Meta feed. A coupon is a discount the buyer has to KNOW, so the published price must not move — folding one into the resolver would put a figure in the feed that no page shows, and price mismatch is one of only two documented Merchant Center suspension classes while we run ONE account for every seller. So a coupon is an ORDER-level lever: it applies to one store's SUBTOTAL after the two price levers have priced each line, and it writes the order-level discount slot `order_stores` already had (until now only a seller editing an order afterwards). **That reuse is the design, not a shortcut** — `order-totals.ts`, `admin-stats.ts#orderNetForStore`, `order-reporting.ts`'s NET, `reconcile.ts`'s bounds, the seller balance and the commission basis all already subtract that column, so a coupon was correct in every money surface on day one instead of being a new number six places had to learn. The only thing the slot could not say is WHICH code did it: `order_stores.coupon_code` (migration 0020), provenance only, and a seller who later edits that discount REPLACES the number and clears the code with it. Modules: `coupons.ts` (pure/isomorphic — the browser previews and `/api/checkout` charges through the same `checkCoupon`), `coupon-input.ts` (untrusted seller input), `store-coupons.ts` (storage + `claimCoupon`, a conditional UPDATE whose affected-row count IS the answer — the `decrementStock` shape, and what makes "first 50" mean 50 under concurrency; `releaseCoupon` gives a use back on every path that restocks, so a declined card cannot burn a capped code). **UX rule, and it is the whole answer to "without weighing the existing UI down": the field does not exist unless there is something to type in it.** `/api/cart/prices` reports which of the cart's stores is running a live code (a BOOLEAN per store, never the codes — a public list of live codes is the promotion given away), and only those stores render a "יש לי קוד קופון" disclosure, which is closed until pressed. A permanent empty coupon box sends the shoppers who cannot fill it off to hunt for a code they will not find. Buyer lookup is `/api/cart/coupon` — terms only, never a money figure, `unknown` collapses "no such code"/"off"/"out of dates" so it cannot confirm a guess, and it is throttled per store per address through `rate-limit.ts#couponLookupRules` (a short human-typable secret on a public endpoint is a guessing surface even though it is not a credential). A code that dies between typing and paying answers `409 coupon-invalid` and is handled exactly like a changed price — cart corrected, button live, one more press pays. Seller UI: a third card in the מבצעים tab (`CouponsCard.astro` + `scripts/dashboard/coupons.ts`, list fetched on first tab open), where only the code and the value are required. Tests: `tests/coupons.test.ts`, `tests/store-coupons-db.test.ts` (50 concurrent claims of a cap-10 code → exactly 10), and the coupon block in `tests/checkout-e2e.test.ts`.
- **CSV bulk import/export:** `csv-bulk.ts` + `/api/store-product/bulk.ts` + dashboard `csv-import.ts`/`csv-preview.ts`. Export catalog or sample template; import → server-side dry-run preview → confirm → commit. **A blank cell on an update row means "leave unchanged", not clear** (`stock`/`category`/`tags`/`sku` all preserve-on-blank — the opposite once caused real data loss). Row `id` (filled) matches first; an id-less row matches by `sku`, else creates. `sku` unique-validated across product-level **and** every `variantSku`; export cells sanitized against formula injection; rows capped. Excel `.xlsx` is not parsed (help text says save-as-CSV) — revisit with server-side SheetJS only if sellers struggle. **Preview hides no-op rows (2026-07-26):** an update whose every field equals the stored product (`updateChangesProduct`, mirrors the write exactly, conservative: unsure→changed) is flagged `unchanged`, never listed, only summary-counted, and skipped on commit — so a re-uploaded catalog shows just the real changes. Each listed update shows current→new name as the guard against a scrambled row updating the wrong product (RTL arrow is `←`, dir-aware). For a variant update where only some combos changed (2026-07-27) the preview pins the exact changed combo(s) via `changedCombos`, not the whole product's line span. **Variants via CSV (סשן ב׳; generic dims 2026-07-27):** item_group_id-style — rows sharing a non-empty `group` column merge into one variant product (`variant-csv.ts#mergeVariantGroups`, a pure pass after per-row validation), each row = one combo with its own `sku`+`stock`. **Rows also group by a REPEATED existing `id` when `group` is blank (2026-07-27)** — otherwise a variant product's N combo rows surface as N duplicate updates and overwrite its matrix N times; a unique id stays a plain single-product update. **Dimensions are generic Shopify-style `option{1..3}Name`/`Value` pairs** (`OPTION_SLOTS`) so ANY dimension works (צבע/חומר/נפח…), not a fixed color/size; the seller's own dimension name is stored verbatim and `comboKey` sorts names so column order is irrelevant. `finalizeGroup` requires every row in a group to declare the SAME dimension-name set with a value. Export emits one row per combo with a readable `group` = product name (UUID `id` still does the update-match); a product past `CSV_MAX_DIMENSIONS` (= `OPTION_SLOTS.length`, never a second literal 3) exports as one flat row. **A flat row can never move a variant product's stock (2026-07-30):** a purchase decrements the combo's own bucket (`resolveStockField`), so a single `stock` number on an option-less row used to write a figure that governed nothing while reporting "updated" — the oversell a sku+stock feed walks into. Such a row is now an error, with two messages because the fixes differ: within the cap "fill in the option columns", past it "dashboard only" (that file has no 4th column pair to point at). An unchanged stock cell still reads as a plain no-op, so the flat export round-trips silently. Feed-mapping has Shopify "Option1 Name/Value" synonyms. **A trailing `weight` column (grams) since 2026-08-05** — `CSV_FIELDS` is APPEND-ONLY, so a seller's older file still imports with the missing cell reading blank = unchanged, and this is the only practical way a catalogue of dozens gets weights at all. Unlike every other column an unusable weight **fails the row** rather than importing weightless: a seller who typed 2.5 meaning kilograms would otherwise find out from a wrong carrier quote months later. **The preview also names what the file CANNOT say (2026-08-12, `csv-import-advisory.ts`):** there is no image column, so every product a file creates is image-less — live, indexed and buyable, and dropped from the ad feed (`isProductAdvertisable`) where the seller only learns of it when a campaign comes back paused. Counts of image-less and thin-description rows show as an advisory note over the rows that would actually be WRITTEN (threshold reused from `product-seo-hints.ts`, never restated); it disables nothing, because both fields are optional in the product form too and a catalogue whose photos come later must still load. The panel copy says it before the upload as well. **And a commit that skipped rows now says how many** — it closes the panel and clears the very error list the seller had just read, so 3 bad lines out of 300 reported a clean "Import complete". **Gotcha:** `mapHeader` must lowercase the bare-key alias too, else camelCase keys never match a key-header. Tests: `tests/store-products-import.test.ts`, `tests/csv-import-advisory.test.ts`.
- **External inventory sync (2026-07-26):** for a store managing stock in another system (POS/ERP/Shopify/Woo/spreadsheet) — its own dashboard panel (`feed-sync.ts`), distinct from the CSV panel. **Inbound:** upload a foreign file OR save a feed **URL** + "sync now" (server pull, `feed-fetch.ts`, SSRF-guarded). A pure translator (`feed-mapping.ts`) guesses each foreign column → our canonical key (he/en synonyms; seller corrects once, mapping saved on `Store.feedSync`), `buildCanonicalCsv` rewrites it into our format, then the SAME pipeline runs. `resolveSkuMatches` resolves an id-less row's sku→existing product and backfills blank name/price — **this runs on EVERY import including the manual CSV upload**, so a seller can re-upload their own Excel (skus, not our ids) to update stock with no mode to pick; id always wins so export→edit→import is unchanged. Manual and feed paths are byte-for-byte identical through the shared `runProductImport` (`store-products-import.ts`). **Outbound:** `Store.feedExportToken` (192-bit, gen/rotate/revoke via `/api/store`) exposes the live catalog at `/api/store-feed/[token]` as CSV/JSON — the mirror image, so a seller isn't locked in. **Automatic since 2026-08-03 (stage 4a):** the URL pull runs hourly for every store with a `feedSync.url` that may sell, driven by the scheduler; the sequence lives in `store-feed-sync.ts` so the route and the job run the same code. The SSRF DNS-rebinding gap is closed too — the vetted IP is pinned onto the socket (`lookup`), which is what moved this module off `fetch`.
- **Images:** Cloudinary upload; BG removal in a Web Worker (`isnet_fp16` on WebGPU, cpu fallback on init failure, sticky for the session; mask computed on a 1024px copy, applied to the full-res original); crop output capped 2048px; manual cleanup modal (erase/restore brush, independent zoom+pan); crop and bg-removal keep separate undo snapshots. **The crop itself is lossless** — a canvas draw from the decoded source at the exact pixels selected, out as PNG — so "editing loses quality" was never the editor: it was `w_` alone meaning `c_scale`, which INVENTS pixels above the source (2026-08-05, now `c_limit`; see Project structure → cdn.ts for the measurement). Up to 5 images/product; widths home tile=220/440 · store card=400 · carousel=300 · thumb=2x its CSS box · main=800 · lightbox = whichever of 800/1200/1600 fits the screen (`lightboxWidth`, DPR capped at 2). **Delivery (2026-07-29, `lib/cdn.ts`) — see Hard rules → Images for the standing rule:** ONE module turns a raw URL into an optimized one, and every surface in the app now routes through it (~20 call sites swept: cart, header search, order rows, buyer/seller/admin tables, checkout, lightboxes, modals). Non-Cloudinary hosts go through Cloudinary **fetch delivery**, which is what made the demo catalogue survivable — its host sends `cache-control: no-store`, so every refresh re-downloaded every image and the skeletons replayed (homepage measured 3.88MB → 343KB across 69 tiles). The homepage's hand-rolled IntersectionObserver lazy-loader was DELETED for native `loading="lazy"`: with the URL parked in `data-lazy-src` the preload scanner saw no images at all and nothing loaded until the page module ran. `tests/image-optimization.test.ts` is the guard. **Retina (2026-07-27):** a single `cdnSrc(w)` gets browser-upscaled → blurry at DPR 2/3, so `cdnSrcSet(url, widths[])` emits a width-descriptor `srcset` + `sizes` on every surface showing a seller photo large (store + home product cards incl. the deferred-slide hydration and the client `renderProductCardHTML`, and the product-page main image). Those use a plain `<img>` — Astro's `<Image />` can't carry a manual srcset for a remote URL (matches the banner + deferred-slide precedent). `cdnSrcSet` returns '' only when the URL can't be transformed at all (relative/data URI/dev-only host/already-transformed/no cloud configured) so the caller keeps its plain src. **BG-removal UX (2026-07-22):** worker `preload()` is warm-fired on gallery-panel open so the one-time model download starts before the click; progress % is relayed to the button label (download + compute both surface as %, so the bar sweeps to 100 twice — user chose that over a blind wait). **Cleanup output** bakes a background (transparent default / gray / cream / black + a colour picker) and/or a soft drop-shadow into the saved PNG; Apply shows a dot-pulse while the slow encode runs. Preview box + cutout sit on `--color-surface` (white), never `--color-bg` (memory `project_transparent_image_bg`). **Saved bg colours are per-STORE** (`Store.bgColors`, `/api/store` `add-bg-color`, hex-validated + ownership-checked, cap 12) — persisted only when an edit is *applied*, so abandoned picks don't stick and the palette follows the store across devices. **Batch BG removal** (≥2 filled slots): tap-to-exclude selection → sequential queue (the model is compute-bound, true parallel isn't faster). **Drag-and-drop:** every gallery slot accepts a drop, and ONE shared off-screen file input drives click-to-choose — a real per-slot hidden `<input>` is still a native drop target and double-processes. **Gotchas:** `galleryWidgetHtml()` already returns its own `.gallery-widget` root — never wrap it in another (duplicate-file bug when multiple galleries mount). `setLoading` defaults its label to the bg-removal message, so the crop path must pass `gallery.loadingImage` explicitly or "מסיר רקע…" shows on a plain crop. Two fixed skeleton bugs worth not repeating: search-results cards had no `initImageSkeletons()` wiring, and the product page's skeleton CSS was defeated by an inline `style="background:…"` on the same element. **Enlarging (סשן ה׳, `lib/lightbox-image.ts` — one mechanism for all three viewers):** the frame is never blank — it opens on the render the gallery already holds, softened (`blur-[10px]` + 3% overscan, which hides the blur's edge falloff), and settles onto the full one after `decode()`; the `.dot-pulse` pill only appears past 180ms, so a cached open shows no loading state at all. The preview is sized to the box the full render will occupy (`fitPreviewToFrame`), or the frame visibly pops when a smaller preview is replaced. **Cost of a width nobody asked for yet: ~1.2s of Cloudinary derivation before a byte moves** (~0.3s warm, measured 2026-07-29) — which is why the ladder is short and shared, why hovering the image warms the render before the click, and why `neighbourIndexes` preloads ±1 (not the gallery) after the current image settles, at low priority, skipped under Save-Data/2G. That cold derivation is now paid at SAVE time instead (`lib/image-derive.ts`, wired into `/api/product` add/edit/patch-images): a `HEAD` per image per width renders them while the seller saves — a HEAD triggers the derivation exactly like a GET but transfers no bytes to us (verified). Only images the save actually ADDED, and bulk import is deliberately not wired (it would burst thousands of requests — see the file header).
- **Custom domains + editable store URL (2026-07-26, Cloudflare-for-SaaS):** stores live at the ROOT (`/[slug]`, not `/store/…`) — `isReservedSlug` stops collisions with real routes. The slug is a seller-chosen **latin** name required by the create-store form (`normalizeSlug`) so a Hebrew store name no longer yields junk, and it's **editable in settings**, SEO-safe: old slugs 301 to the new via `Store.previousSlugs`. A rename migrates ALL slug-keyed data (`renameStoreSlugInUserData`: favorites/recent/wishlist/saved cart · `renameStoreSlugInOrders`: order items + subtotal keys, so pre-rename history survives · page-views), and EVERY consumer that takes a client/stored slug tolerates an old one (`getStoreBySlugOrPrevious` / `findStoreBySlugOrPrevious`, then key off `store.slug`; ads key off the immutable storeId) — **checkout included, tested + live-verified**. Old slugs stay globally reserved so no other store can break a 301. Security-reviewed: no cross-tenant leakage (current-slug-wins), platform-domain claim blocked, checkout re-validation intact. A seller can also connect their **own domain** (opt-in, verified): `Store.customDomain{hostname,status}`, provider-agnostic `custom-domain.ts` (real Cloudflare adapter when `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`+`CLOUDFLARE_ZONE_ID` are set, else a dev stub; `CUSTOM_DOMAIN_DEV_AUTOVERIFY=1` fakes verification). `middleware.ts` maps an active custom Host → the store from root and **404s an unclaimed external host** (`PLATFORM_HOSTS` exempts staging/health hosts; IPs/loopback auto-exempt). **SEO:** an active custom domain becomes the canonical AND **every platform URL 301s to it** (`customDomainRedirectUrl`, inherits accumulated ranking — Shopify-style); store cards + dashboard links point there (`storeHomeHref`) and the content sitemap skips it; on the custom host `custom-domain-links.ts` strips the redundant `/slug` prefix. **A hostname may be CLAIMED by anyone and SERVED by only one store (0029, row-5 audit 2026-08-16).** It was globally `UNIQUE`, so a pending claim — an assertion, the field takes any string — excluded everyone else permanently: type a hostname you do not own and its real owner is answered `domain-taken` for good. Unique now only `WHERE custom_domain_status = 'active'`; `isCustomDomainTaken` matches, and the promotion re-asks before writing `'active'` because that is where two claimants have to be decided between. **A domain is re-checked hourly, not only when a human presses a button (2026-08-06)** — `custom-domain-verify.ts` + job `custom-domain-check` + `custom_domain_checked_at` (0014): a lapsed CNAME used to leave the store 301-ing into a dead host forever, and a domain that verified after the seller closed the tab stayed 'pending' forever. Both directions notify the seller. **And leaving a domain no longer kills its links:** `store_previous_domains` (0015) remembers the old hostname and 301s it to wherever the store lives now, exactly as `previousSlugs` does for a rename. The two machine surfaces follow the store to its host: a custom-domain store serves its OWN `/sitemap-content.xml` there (it is excluded from the platform's, so it used to be in none), and IndexNow submits on that host with that host's `keyLocation` — Bing verifies one submission against one host. `robots.txt` is an SSR route for the same reason (`src/pages/robots.txt.ts`): a static `public/` file named the platform's sitemaps on a seller's domain, which every engine ignores across hosts, so that domain declared none; a `public/` copy would silently outrank the route and `external-contract.test.ts` refuses one. A domain is ONE hostname in our record and TWO spellings at the registrar — the www/apex twin 404'd on the seller's own brand and now 301s onto the claimed one (`hostnameAlias` + `middleware.ts#unclaimedHostRedirect`, which also serves the migration-0015 previous-domain 301). `normalizeHostname` refuses any host `isPlatformHost` claims (incl. `PLATFORM_HOSTS`), and the platform host skips the whole custom-domain block — it was an indexed SELECT in front of every page load that could only ever return null. Real-account steps: GO_LIVE §1. **Every address the seller reads is copyable, and spelled ONE way (2026-08-14):** `CopyButton.astro` + `copy-value.ts` — delegated on the document, because these sit both in the always-rendered header and inside the settings panel, which only exists after the click that opens it. It copies the LIVE text of its target, never a render-time snapshot: `store-url.ts` rewrites the slug in place without a reload, so a baked-in value would keep handing out the old address (memory `project_href_frozen_at_render`). The tick shows only on a successful write — `navigator.clipboard` is absent on an insecure origin. The header line carries the WHOLE address rather than the `/slug` path nobody can be sent to, and NO `https://` anywhere it is displayed: the same address in two spellings on one screen reads as two addresses, and the scheme stays only where the string is a real link (`cd-visit`'s href) or a machine reads it (the feed URL Merchant Center fetches). **A custom domain is a second browser ORIGIN, and the transaction stays on the platform (2026-08-06)** — `platform-routes.ts` names which paths those are and carries the decision. The cart is `localStorage` and the session is a host-scoped cookie, so crossing used to mean logged out, empty basket, ad click lost; third-party-cookie and cross-origin-storage workarounds are gone (ITP / storage partitioning). So: browsing is the store's, checkout/login/buyer area are ours. Links are rewritten in the page (`custom-domain-links.ts`) and the middleware 302s the same paths as a floor. The cart crosses in the URL **fragment** (`cart-handoff.ts` — never sent to a server, so no handoff table and no CSRF hole); `sn_vid` + `sn_attr` cross in a **signed, 10-min** query token (`cross-origin-handoff.ts`) because GA4 cross-domain linking needs a hand-maintained domain list this model can never have. Guarded in `tests/cross-origin-boundary.test.ts`, including that the two route lists account for every `RESERVED_SLUGS` entry. **A custom domain is also the ONLY way a store stops appearing inside us in a Google result (verified 2026-08-13 against Google's site-names page): the name printed ABOVE a result is a property of a domain or subdomain and is NOT supported for a subdirectory** — so nothing markup can do makes `dezabin.co.il/<slug>` say anything but ours, and everything we CAN own is: the title carries no ` | Dezabin` on a store-owned page, `og:site_name` is the store's, and the store's front page on its own domain emits a `WebSite` node (name+url, publisher → its own `Store` `@id`) — the first two sources Google reads. All three, plus why the WebSite node is gated to the real home page of a real custom domain, in `tests/store-owns-its-title.test.ts`.
- **Store banner + avatar (`scripts/dashboard/store-image.ts`):** one widget for both, cropping to the shape its render target actually paints — 3:1 for the banner, and for the avatar a CIRCLE, because StoreAvatar is round everywhere and framing to a square quietly cut a fifth of what the seller lined up. **"התאם תמונה" re-frames an image already saved**, cutting from the uncropped original kept beside it (migration 0012) so it never crops a crop and never degrades; an image from before that, or one whose source upload failed, falls back to re-cropping the delivered file. The circle is honest for every ON-SITE appearance and the favicon; the iOS touch icon and the OG/ad creatives get the full square, corners included, which is why the stored crop stays square. **"הסר רקע" (2026-08-05) — the LOGO only**, since it lands on a white card, in a circle and in a tab, while a banner IS its background; the same Web Worker the product gallery uses. The cutout becomes the new uncropped SOURCE, not just the crop, or the next "התאם" re-frames the original photo and the background comes back. Two consequences of transparency now being reachable: StoreAvatar and the widget preview paint `--color-surface` behind the image (an `<img>` with none of its own reads as two different marks depending what it sits on), and `cdnFill`'s `f_jpg` flattens alpha onto WHITE (verified, not assumed) — right for an ad creative, which is why the browser slots keep `f_auto`.
- **"Did I save that?" (2026-08-05, `scripts/dashboard/unsaved-guard.ts`):** switching tabs never loses anything (panels are hidden, not destroyed) and that is exactly why nothing spoke up. A floating bar names the section holding unsaved work, in WORDS — only while that section is OFF SCREEN — and "בטל שינויים" beside save is the way back, disabled while there is nothing to discard. A tab dot was built and rejected: see Project structure → UnsavedChangesBar for why no glyph works there. **Rewriting a form's fields raises TWO events and they are not the same statement:** `dash:fieldsrewritten` = "repaint from the field", fired by BOTH discard and the draft restore — every widget that paints from a hidden input (image cropper, both category pickers) must listen to it or it shows one picture above a field holding another; `dash:discarded` = "thrown away on purpose", fired only by discard, and it is what deletes the stored draft, which a restore must never do.
- **Store page `/[slug]`:** Product grid; the product modal + lightbox are the shared `StoreProductModal.astro` (`store-product-modal:open` with just a slug, self-fetching, pushState URL sync) — the SAME component the product page's related cards open. **Default ranking "מומלצים" (2026-07-27):** `product-listing.ts#rankDefault`, deterministic so SSR / load-more / re-filter agree (SEO-safe): in-stock above sold-out (a sold-out item sinks but stays listed) → demand tier `platform_bestseller›bestseller›popular›new›standard` → units sold, then newer. No manual per-product order field. `purchasedUnits` is passed by BOTH the SSR page and `/api/store-products` so paged results rank identically, and tiering reuses `performanceTier` from `product-labels.ts` so storefront ranking and the ad feed can't disagree on what a bestseller is. Tests: `tests/product-listing.test.ts`. **Search:** the header bar always searches the whole store (remembers the filter via `preGlobalSearchTag`); a local search appears only once a category is picked. **Every grid refresh — category, sort, search — scrolls up to the results region** so a buyer who filtered mid-page doesn't land inside results they never saw: one guarded call inside `refreshFromServer()` (the single funnel for all three), via RTL-safe `animateScrollTo`. Back-navigation restores exact scroll+filter/sort/search (`scroll-restore.ts`), gated to real returns by `document.referrer`; the anti-flash head IIFE reads the slug from `<body data-store-slug>` (root-URL + custom-domain safe). **Banner:** `aspect-ratio:3/1` MUST equal the dashboard crop tool's `aspect:3` — a mismatch lets `object-fit:cover` re-crop per viewport. **Curtain scroll (2026-07-26):** only the banner image is sticky-pinned; details then products scroll over it as one opaque flow. The curtain's leading edge carries `--shadow-bar-up`, but only once the page has actually scrolled (keyed off the header's own scrolled flag) — at rest it is covering nothing. The site header's shadow is off for the whole time the pinned banner is what sits under it, and comes back only when the curtain passes the header line; all three phases are in header.css. The covering layers are `position:relative` with **no z-index on purpose** — `z-index:auto` creates NO stacking context, so the fixed category drawer / quick-view still escape above the site header (a z-indexed wrapper traps them under it). **Catalog is CRAWLABLE (2026-08-03) — the SEO half of this page.** It used to link its first 24 products and stop: "load more" is a fetch and the chips were `<button>`s, so 76 of a 100-product store had no in-site link at all. Categories are real URLs now (`?category=נעליים` — slug via `categoryUrlParam`, self-canonical, own title/description/CollectionPage/BreadcrumbList), chips and breadcrumb are `<a href>` that a plain left click still intercepts to filter in place, and every grid page is addressable as `?page=N` — carried by **“load more” ITSELF, which is an `<a href="?page=N+1">` drawn as a button** whose click handler cancels the navigation and appends in place (2026-08-05). A numbered pager beside the button was tried for two days and removed: two controls for one job that contradict each other (press it four times and the row still says page 1), and “מה המשמעות ליוזר שיש גם טען עוד וגם עמודים?”. So the crawl path is a CHAIN — page 2's button links to page 3 — and the href is **omitted on the last page**, server-side and client-side both: `hidden` on the wrapper is no defence, and an href that outlives the last slice points at a URL that redirects to page 1, whose button points forward again (a measured loop). Past the last page redirects rather than serving an empty shelf, and q/sort views stay `noindex` (same products, another order, unbounded URLs). Verified by walking it with a crawler: 100/100 products reachable, sitemap 878→1003. Guard: `tests/store-catalog-crawlable.test.ts`. **Exactly one chip is always filled** — `aria-pressed` is invalid on an `<a>`, so the CSS keys off `aria-current="page"` too, and the SELECTED category leads its own row (a leaf category used to render an empty one). **Category nav:** drill-down breadcrumb + a single scrolling chip line (`chip-scroller.ts`, which also drives the row's `.edge-fade`); the row is explicitly `overflow-y:hidden` — `overflow-y` computes to `auto` (never `visible`) once the other axis isn't visible, so a one-line chip row was a vertical scroll container that only *happened* to have nothing to scroll; a chip's hover flyout must be `position:fixed` (the row's `overflow-x:auto` clips absolute overflow at any z-index), and the mobile drawer lives OUTSIDE `.store-controls` or that sticky context traps it under the header. **Sticky controls glass (2026-07-21):** `.store-controls` frosts only once stuck (`sticky-bar.ts` sentinel) — the blur/tint/full-bleed geometry AND the "never put transform/filter/backdrop-filter on this element" warning are written out at length in `store.css` itself; read them there rather than here. **Filter-transition jump (2026-07-15):** chip and grid rebuild must stay inside one `refreshFromServer()` — two reflows read as a layout reset. **Never manually compensate scroll for a height change here** (tried once; `scrollY` isn't scoped to one target, it moved unrelated elements). **Perf:** `content-visibility:auto`+`contain-intrinsic-size` scoped to `.product-grid>.product-card` (bare `.product-card` collapses width inside horizontal carousels) — measured 61 → 18 image requests at first paint. **Deferred carousel slides (2026-07-23):** only a card's FIRST image gets a real src, the rest hold `BLANK_PIXEL`+`data-src` until `hydrateCardSlides()` fires on first engagement (38 → 18; `loading="lazy"` alone still fetched them). A src-less `<img>` renders its ALT text — hence the blank pixel — and the skeleton wiring must skip `[data-src]` or that instantly-'loaded' pixel clears the shimmer while the real image is still on the wire. **Slide 2 is fetched when the CARD enters the viewport, not on hover (2026-08-03):** measured 789ms from the gesture to that photo being painted, of which the `src` swap was 0ms — the wait was entirely the download, so the request has to start before the gesture or the gesture waits for it. One neighbour only; the rest still wait for the interaction, which is what keeps the deferral worth having (789ms → 1ms, 24 of 31 images still deferred). `dir=auto` on the name+price block so an English product aligns LTR. Saved-store star = grey `currentColor` when pressed, not accent; wishlist stays available on out-of-stock products.
- **Product page `/[storeSlug]/[productSlug]`:** Image gallery+lightbox; qty stepper; add-to-cart reveals a checkout button beside it; the top nav's "לתשלום" was the one checkout on the page visible BEFORE anything was added, so it opened an empty checkout on a fresh visit — it now does the whole gesture (choose → add → pay) through the same variant gate and the same addItem, stays a plain `<a href>` for no-JS and for a sold-out product, and does not re-add an item already in the cart (2026-08-10); wishlist; related products (cards open the shared `StoreProductModal` — replaced an older bespoke copy that had drifted, and a stint on PQV which read as "the cart modal"); BreadcrumbList+Product JSON-LD; specs table. **Info column (2026-07-20):** CTA top-aligned (no `mt-auto`) so a variant-less product doesn't open a dead gap between title and buttons. Out-of-stock variant options are disabled (dimmed+strikethrough), computed server- then client-side. Mobile (<768px): the CTA row mirrors into a sticky bottom bar via IntersectionObserver. **Variant-selection gating:** add-to-cart on every variant picker requires an active choice per dimension; a blocked click shakes + ring-highlights the missing dimension(s). **Product-card CTA:** one pill — minus/plus flank a center segment that is both live qty and add-to-cart trigger; variant products open the quick-view instead of adding blind. `.product-card__footer` is `margin-top:auto` so buttons share one baseline per grid row; a reserved 2nd name line was rejected 2026-07-21 (added a gap to every card). The whole card opens quick-view via `e.composedPath()`, not `closest()` — a button's innerHTML swap on click can detach `e.target` mid-bubble. `formatPrice()` is number-then-symbol site-wide ("100 ₪"). **Gallery (2026-07-20, reworked):** the main image is a native horizontal scroll-snap carousel with no dot overlay — the thumbnail strip is the indicator (active thumb updates via rAF at each halfway point). `overscroll-behavior-inline:contain` stops an edge swipe triggering browser-back; vertical scroll is never captured. **Custom wheel-hijack cycling was tried twice and removed** — it trapped page scroll and fell through to browser-back at the last image. Selected-thumbnail border is `--color-text`, not accent. **Thumbnail hover preview (2026-08-10):** hovering a thumbnail cross-fades that image in a `#preview-img` overlay ON TOP of the carousel — the carousel is never driven. Both alternatives were built and rejected the same day: scrolling to the hovered slide can only jump (a scroll cannot fade, and five queued smooth scrolls read as shaking), and it made the preview commit itself, since the scroll listener is what decides the selection. One delegated `mouseover` on `#thumb-strip`, so the gaps between thumbnails and the empty run past the last one end the preview too — bound per button, it stayed up whenever the pointer slipped off a thumbnail without leaving the row. The selection ring never follows the preview; a slide whose image is not yet `complete` is skipped rather than faded in as an empty box; pointer-only (`matchMedia`), because on touch the hover fires on the tap that is already selecting. A click while previewing scrolls INSTANTLY — the image is already on screen, so animating plays a scroll watched through a dissolving copy of its own destination. The lightbox navigates **independently** of the gallery behind it (don't re-sync `activeIndex` — user flagged it) and decodes the next image before swapping (no blank flash). **Tags are NOT shown** — search/discovery metadata only, still emitted in Product JSON-LD `keywords`. **Gallery warm-up (2026-08-01):** after `load` and then at idle, the remaining slides' own `loading` is flipped to eager — never a URL recomputed here, which is what the previous version did and why scrolling still waited on a download (it warmed a plain w_800 while the srcset resolved w_1200). The `load` gate matters on its own: rIC alone fires between long tasks mid-load, so the warm-up could contend with the page itself. Slides 1..n are `fetchpriority=low` (slide 0 is high, the LCP candidate) so neither path competes. Skipped on saveData/2G. **Out of stock renders no cart row at all** — the live stock line above (`#stock-status-text`, outside the cart block, so it renders at any stock level) already says it, and the chip that used to sit here said it a second time. **This does NOT transfer to `StoreProductModal`**, where the stock line lives inside the in-stock branch and the chip is the only indicator — removing it there was tried and reverted the same day. **The gallery image and its thumbnails carry `data-no-tooltip`** (icon-tooltips.ts's opt-out): the picture is the thing, so naming it adds nothing a sighted user had not already got. aria-label stays.
- **Share a store / a product (סשן ב׳, 2026-08-13, `ShareMenu.astro` + `lib/share.ts` + `scripts/share-menu.ts`):** a "שיתוף" control beside "שמור חנות" on the store banner and beside "אהבתי" on the product page — WhatsApp, Facebook, Telegram, email, copy link, plus the phone's own share sheet where `navigator.share` exists (that is where SMS/Instagram live; nothing app-specific is guessed at). What travels is always the page's CANONICAL, so a store with a custom domain hands out its OWN domain and an ad landing never leaks its marker into WhatsApp. **The link's own appearance is half the feature and is not in these files** — every network builds its card from the shared page's Open Graph tags, so `Seo.astro` is the other half: the product page's `og:image` was the raw multi-megabyte upload until this session and is now `cdnSrc(…,1200)`, a pre-derived `LIGHTBOX_WIDTHS` rung, because a scraper that times out falls back to a link with no picture at all.
- **Cart & Wishlist:** Cart per-store in localStorage + `CartDrawer` (grouped by store, PQV on item click), server-side sync via `user-carts.ts`+`cart-sync.ts` (guest→user merge on login, debounced). Per-store checkout CTA is compact + end-of-row; a CSS-only i-tooltip by the drawer title explains pay-per-store vs together, shown only with 2+ stores. **Stock ceiling:** `addItem()` caps accumulated qty at `product.stock`; the `stock:` param must stay the **raw total** (it does its own prev-qty-in-cart math internally) — a separate net-of-cart "remaining" value drives the UI ceiling only, and conflating the two double-subtracts. Wishlist: localStorage + server sync through the SAME authenticated endpoint as the cart (`/api/user-cart`); removal is local and then synced, with no per-item endpoint to roll back from — the old `/api/wishlist` took a bare product slug with no store and no session, so it could neither name a product uniquely nor say whose wishlist it was, and anyone could move a number the seller reads. **Store sovereignty (סשן ג׳):** in both drawers the store the shopper is currently inside floats to the top (stable sort) with no heading, the rest sit under a muted "מחנויות אחרות" heading — shown whenever inside a store, even if the current store has nothing in the cart. NO `hr` before that heading (it is its own separator); rejected visual treatments are listed in memory `project_cart_store_sovereignty`. Current store detected via **`<body data-store-slug>`** (server-resolved → works for root URLs AND custom domains; replaced a dead `/store/[slug]` URL-parse that broke on the root-URL migration). A "מחנות זו" heading was tried and dropped as redundant (`inThisStore` key kept, unused). **Heart "like" animation:** on activate only (never un-like) `spawnHeartBurst()` (`burst.ts`) fires from the **heart icon itself**, honouring `prefers-reduced-motion`; heart buttons are excluded from the store-page delegated ripple so the burst is the sole cue (no stacked signals). **Header badges:** cart/wishlist count distinct lines, not summed qty; all three share one `updateBadge()` (`badge-ticker.ts`) whose reel is absolutely positioned inside its wrapper so flex centering can't split it mid-row, and whose appear/disappear pop is JS-class-driven (never a bare `:not([hidden])` selector, which replays on an SSR-already-visible badge).
- **Homepage `/`:** Tabs (Discover/Liked/BuyAgain/New) reuse the dashboard's `.dash-tabs` shell; Discover is default and always populated for a guest, the others render a `HomeShelf` or `HomeTabEmpty.astro`. Discover holds category shelves (`buildHomeFeed()`) + a spotlight of per-store carousels in a scroll-snap track. **Sticky tab strip (2026-07-27):** the sticky element is a **full-bleed wrapper**, not the inner `.container` (a container-width sticky bar shows content through the side gutters), and its containing block is `.home-body` so it stays pinned across every panel. **Hero:** a breathing CSS `radial-gradient` dot pattern — many louder alternatives were tried and reverted; **don't rebuild without being asked again, and get a concrete reference first.** **Vertical-scroll trap, root cause (fixed 2026-07-21):** `overflow-x:auto` with no explicit `overflow-y` makes the browser compute `overflow-y:auto` (the spec forbids one axis scrolling while the other stays `visible`), silently turning `.home-carousel`/`.home-shelf`/`.home-spotlight-track` into VERTICAL scroll containers — a vertical wheel got trapped, fired `scroll` → paused the carousel, and a stray `scrollTop` clipped the hover-lift. Fix = **`overflow-y:clip` on all three.** Shelves edge-fade via the shared `.edge-fade` utility (`utilities/utils.css`) — a `mask-image` whose state is the `data-fade` attribute (`l`/`r`/`lr`/empty, PHYSICAL sides) set by `initEdgeFade`; the spotlight's carousels use plain gradient divs instead (a real `mask-image` on the scrolling element forces a compositing layer that broke hover-shadow + arrow timing). **`.edge-fade` had a second, conflicting definition in `store-card.css` until 2026-07-30** — `[dir] .edge-fade` (0,2,0) silently outranked a bare `.edge-fade` (0,1,0) and masked rows with properties nobody set, i.e. a zero-width gradient that renders as a hard clip and looks exactly like no fade: **grep a class name across `src/styles/` before defining one.** Empty `data-fade` means no mask at all, so a row that fits costs nothing. `edgeState`'s tolerance is `EDGE_SLOP=12`, not 4 — `.home-shelf`'s 8px `padding-inline` rests scroll-snap at `scrollLeft≈±8` even at the true start. **Auto-advance:** one store every 4.5s, paused while backgrounded — scrolls the *track* (`track.scrollTo`), **never `slide.scrollIntoView()`**, which also scrolls the page vertically and yanks a reader down mid-read. Pause = an explicit resumable button (replaced a permanent teardown-on-interaction), hover-pause, and a real track scroll (auto-scroll masked by an `autoScrolling` flag; an earlier `wheel` listener wrongly paused on any vertical scroll). **Card entrance** (`scroll-reveal.ts`): only a card confirmed off-screen via `getBoundingClientRect` gets the hidden class, so nothing visible at load flashes; replays per tab switch, reused by `/stores`. Above the fold is a SECOND, separate arrival (2026-07-29): the `eagerFirst` row alone fades in as one block on first paint — `shelf-arrive`, opacity only, server-rendered class with `both` fill so it is never painted solid and snapped back. Deliberately not `card-reveal`, whose rise+spring+stagger suits a row you scroll TO and is too busy for content already on screen. **New Stores** backfills with recent stores if genuinely-new ones don't reach `MIN_SHELF_FILL`. **Recently-visited (סשן ב׳):** a store OR product visit counts (a product-card version was tried and dropped — it demanded a product open and left the shelf empty too often); SSR from the `sn_recent_stores` cookie unioned with the account list (`recent-stores.ts`). "גלה עוד" sits *below* the spotlight, minus stores already in recently-visited. **Tab underline** is one JS-positioned sliding bar (physical-rect math → RTL-correct) that moves **only on activation** — following the pointer read as noise (סשן ג׳). **Hover is a pointer-tracked "flashlight beam"** (`.home-tab::before`); it takes its edge from a `drop-shadow` (alpha-shaped, so it moves with the beam) and **never from a darker tint** — the full colour reasoning is in `home.css`. Two traps on the spotlight's "כנס לחנות": `.btn--ghost:hover` fills with `--color-bg`, which IS this page's background (needs an `!bg` override), and the arrow's `translate` applies *after* `rtl:-scale-x-100`'s flip, so RTL needs its own branch. Header search is a dropdown-only preview (shared `site-search.ts`); Enter/chip/"view all" goes to `/search?q=`. **`/stores`** swaps results via one `innerHTML` replace of the same server-rendered URL a real nav would load (no JSON endpoint to drift), and its category filter is the store page's own bar reused (`.store-controls`/`.category-filters-row` + `chip-scroller.ts` + `sticky-bar.ts`) — one scrolling line with floating arrows, sticky under the header, instead of a wrapping block that grew to 4-5 lines and pushed the grid off-screen. Chips stay real `<a href>` links (crawlable, JS-free); only the arrows need JS. **Both the scroller and the sticky sentinel live inside the swapped region, so `armChipScroller()` must re-init them after every AJAX filter.** **Two silent breakages fixed 2026-07-30, both from reusing the store page's CSS:** (1) `.stores-directory__chips` still carried `flex-wrap:wrap` from the pre-scroller era, and on a `flex-direction:column` parent (which `.store-controls` is) wrapping makes the line size to CONTENT instead of stretching — the row resolved to 1571px inside a 1280px parent, so `scrollWidth === clientWidth`, the arrows correctly hid themselves and 16 categories were simply clipped by `html{overflow-x:hidden}`. **A class reused for spacing must add spacing ONLY.** (2) store.css hid `.category-filters-wrap` on every touch device (the store page swaps to a drawer there) — `/stores` has no drawer, so mobile showed ZERO categories; that rule is now scoped to `.store-controls__row >`. The directory's chips are flat `<a href>` with no flyout, so the scrolling row IS the right touch UI. `.stores-directory` also trims `.section`'s top `padding-block:3rem` to 1.25rem, since it opens directly under the fixed header. **Launch mode (2026-07-28, `lib/launch-mode.ts`):** under 5 live stores Discover collapses to ONE placeholder-padded row (`HomeShelf invites={n}` → `StorePlaceholderCard.astro`) and `/stores` pads its grid to 12; 5–10 stores gets one closing placeholder. That row is a **grid** (`HomeShelf grid`, 2 cols / 3 from 900px, 4 preview thumbs, 2026-07-29), not the scroll row: widening cards inside a scroller pushes the tail off-screen and the tail is where the invite card sits — the same burial `MIN_SHELF_DEMO` exists to prevent. A grid row is NOT a `.home-shelf`, so it carries a CSS-less `home-shelf-grid` marker that `REVEAL_SELECTOR` must include (forgetting it silently dropped the whole shelf out of the entrance animation). The **New-stores tab** pads with placeholders too (floor `MIN_SHELF_INVITES`, so the invite survives a full row) but **keeps the scroll row on purpose** — using the grid on both made the two panels render identically and the tabs stopped reading as different places (user, 2026-07-29). Composition is `planLaunchShelf()`, not a concat: real stores, then ONE showcase store, then placeholders — never all-stores (3 real + 3 showcase is exactly the 6 slots and the invite card silently vanished) and never past the ~4.5 cards the row actually shows. The tab strip, titles and spotlight are untouched — **nothing on screen announces the mall is new** (a "החנויות הראשונות" heading + buyer waitlist were built and deleted the same day for exactly that). Placeholders reuse the real `.store-card` classes with line-art from `placeholder-art.ts` — **never a fabricated store**: no name, no price, no category label. Art and copy rotate on cycles coprime with 2/3/4, or the same row repeats down every grid column. DEV only: `?preview=launch` (2 real) / `?preview=empty` (0 real — day one as it ships, showcase stores present) / `?preview=blank` (nothing at all). **Rejected here, don't rebuild:** a decorative full-bleed drifting art band where the spotlight sits (too wide, foreign to the page); and a cross-store product row closing the launch page — mixing every store's goods into one row reads as a bazaar, the opposite of the mall being sold. Launch mode ends on the spotlight on purpose: the page still scrolls and the seller CTA card closes it below the fold (1743px page vs a 900px viewport, measured). "The page feels short" is not a reason to re-add one. **Showcase stores (2026-07-28, `lib/demo-stores.ts`; content rebuilt 2026-08-12):** 4 platform-owned demo stores — סהר apparel / שקמה home / Teklar tech / אדנית urban nursery — **400 Hebrew products**, categories two levels deep where a shelf is big enough, variants with per-combo stock, shipping weights, and stock that includes zeros so the sold-out states are actually demonstrated. Content lives in `scripts/lib/showcase/` (`identity.mjs` = who they are + the photographic language; four `catalog-*.mjs`); the seeder is a writer only. **No real brand appears** — the old catalog listed "אייפון 13 Pro" beside a scraped photo of one, which is the Merchant Center misrepresentation class that suspends the whole platform account. **Product photography is generated — DONE 2026-08-13, 752 images** (`npm run showcase:images`, Gemini → Cloudinary, idempotent by `image-manifest.json`). Two blind API runs had bought 161 pictures in a rejected direction, so the order became: sample, he approves, then the full run — seven sample rounds, and **almost every note traced back to a clause asking for the opposite of what he wanted.** The four causes worth remembering, all now fixed in `identity.mjs`: the stores looked identical because ONE shared clause said "sophisticated, expensive, editorial" and the shared region clause naming terracotta sat AFTER each store's palette (last word wins — the store's direction is last now); products kept getting cropped through three rounds because the `main` view modifier asked for "filling most of the frame" while three other clauses forbade cropping (framing is stated as MARGIN now); gallery views repeated the hero because a reference image pulls hard toward reproduction and "a different angle" is a judgement the model satisfies by changing the wall (each view NAMES a camera position — overhead, low, macro at 45°); and שקמה was beige because its CATALOG was (7 "natural", 6 "cream"), fixed by a per-product `colorways` list since the English subject line is never shown to a shopper. Each store has its own photographic world AND its own hero backdrop colour, which is what tells them apart when the frame is otherwise empty (graphite / deep teal / white sweep / sunlit sand). **A gallery view is generated FROM its product's main image** (`SAME_ITEM_CLAUSE`) so the four show one object — which is why the mains must exist before the galleries and `--batch` holds them back if they don't. **Banner and logo carry the store's name in the PICTURE** (reversing the SVG-overlay decision on evidence: eight test images spelled Hebrew correctly once each name was asked for in its own alphabet), and the four logos differ by MEDIUM, not just by drawing — varying only the drawing produced one house style twice. `--cost` prices any job list for free; `--batch` is the same models at half price ($73 → $37) and every chunk is submitted before any is collected, because waiting on each in turn made a four-chunk run three days instead of eleven hours. Flagged `Store.demo`, always labelled (`StoreDemoBadge`/`StoreDemoBanner`) — they exist so the spotlight actually pages (arrows + auto-advance need `spotlight.length > 1`). Out of every machine surface: `noindex`, out of `/sitemap-content.xml`, the product feed and IndexNow (guarded inside `indexnow.ts` so no call site can forget), and not counted toward `isLaunchMode`; hidden from shoppers past 5 real stores (`DEMO_HIDE_AT_REAL_STORES`) while their pages stay reachable by link. Cart works on purpose, **checkout is blocked server-side** in `/api/checkout` before any stock is reserved (403 `demo-store`) — but on the page that block is **per ITEM, never per cart** (`splitDemoCarts`, 2026-07-29): a demo item renders view-only and is excluded from the selection, so a real product beside it still checks out; only a cart with nothing else disables the pay buttons. Refusing the whole order made the platform's own filler cost the buyer their real purchase. That UI is convenience; the 403 is the rule. Their products carry variants **per product TYPE**, not one set per store (dresses S–XL + colour, shoes 38–43, phones colour + storage, bags colour only, furniture none on purpose so the plain single-SKU case is still shown) — a blanket store-level set put "מידה: XL" on a handbag. Every PRODUCT in such a store carries a quiet "מוצר לדוגמה" line too (grid card + product page — **both the SSR and the JS card renderer**, they must match). Conditions: `GO_LIVE_CHECKLIST.md` §6.2. **Every row begins AND ends on the container line (2026-07-30, memory `project_home_row_alignment`):** a card's width is a SHARE of the row — `max(share(--shelf-count), share(--shelf-fit))`, where `--shelf-count` is the row's card count (inline, from `HomeShelf`) and `--shelf-fit` is a per-breakpoint 2/3/4 ladder in store-card.css (the carousel has its own 2/3/4/5, `--carousel-fit`). A short row fills; a long row shows a whole number of cards and scrolls, so no row ever ends mid-card beside one that ends flush. Reported twice: first as a 24px tail under a flat `max-width` cap, then as the sliced-vs-whole mismatch the cap's removal exposed. **Four traps, each cost a round:** declaring `--shelf-count`'s default *inside* the card rule shadows the inherited value (must be a `var()` fallback); `calc(… / min(a,b))` does not parse and fails SILENTLY to `flex-basis:auto`, i.e. content-sized cards of differing widths — hence `max()` of the two shares; a flex item's default `min-width:auto` floors a card at its own min-content width (~200px), so `min-width:0` is what makes the share authoritative; and a scroller with `padding-inline` needs a matching `scroll-padding-inline`, or `scroll-snap-align:start` rests the row 8px out of line with its non-scrolling neighbours. Mobile shows 2 whole cards with NO peek — the wider 40px `--edge-fade-w` is the "there's more" signal there. **Gotcha:** Tailwind v4's `scale-*` compiles to the standalone `scale` property, so `transition: transform` does NOT ease it — use the `transition-transform` utility. **Tab strip on a phone (2026-07-30):** the four labels need 441px against 343px of strip, so it scrolls — edge arrows (`.home-tabs-arrow`, hidden by CSS off the `at-start`/`at-end`/`is-scrollable` classes `initDashTabs()` already maintains, so arrows and fades cannot disagree and a JS-less load shows none), and the injected `.dash-tab-fade` now paints `--dash-tab-fade-bg` (the homepage strip is `--color-bg`; the white default was invisible on it). Switching tabs from far down the page no longer JUMPS: the panel swap used to shrink the document (2281px → 812px on an empty tab) and the browser clamped scrollY in one frame — `.home-body` is frozen at its height, the swap happens under it, `animateScrollTo` walks up to the strip, then the freeze releases. **The sliding underline must add `tablist.scrollLeft`** — it lives in the strip's scrolled coordinates while its two rects are viewport ones, so any recompute while scrolled parked it a full scroll-width off (96px, under a different tab). **The store avatar halo takes the STORE's colour, not the platform accent (2026-07-31):** `--store-glow` — from the generated mark's hue server-side when there's no logo, else read off the logo's pixels on the client (`store-glow.ts` + `dominant-color.ts`, which returns the VIVID quarter of the dominant hue, not that hue averaged). Its fallback is a light grey, never the accent: a blue halo around a black-and-white logo asserts a colour that store hasn't got. The same colour tints the card's HOVER BORDER, at a strength that leaves the no-colour card's navy edge unchanged (store-card.css). The halo is `.store-glow-wrap` (utils.css) and its colour lands on the nearest `[data-glow-host]`, so a surface that draws it without declaring one silently stays grey. **The store card is the only surface that can carry it (settled 2026-08-05, after trying the spotlight header and reverting):** a glow at the edge of a scroll-snap slide is clipped, widening the track to free it reveals the next slide's header, and insetting the content moves the products off the line every other row ends on. The spotlight header kept the rest of that change — hover lights its title AND its "כנס לחנות" button through `group/hdr`, and a stretched `::after` makes the whole header (empty middle included) one link, since a header that lights up under a pointer that cannot click it reads as broken. All of it guarded by `tests/dominant-color.test.ts`, including the halo's ABSENCE from the spotlight. **A seller CHOOSES what their store card shows (2026-08-14, migration 0028):** `getStorePreviews` drew the four newest photographed products and nothing chose them — not the owner, not a seller — on the only picture of a shop a stranger meets on the homepage. `store_products.featured` + `setProductFeatured()` + `/api/store-product/featured`, with a kebab item, a row chip and a tooltip naming both the place and the fallback. The query reads `featured DESC, created_at DESC`: picks first, remaining slots FILLED with the newest, so a store that never touches it is ordered exactly as before and a new store looks full from its first upload — there is no state in which the feature empties a card, which is what made it safe to ship to every store at once. The cap (`STORE_PREVIEW_SLOTS`) lives inside ONE `UPDATE` whose `WHERE` carries its own count and whose affected-row count is the answer — the `decrementStock` shape, because count-then-update is a read-then-write race between two tabs; unpicking is never capped. `featured` is deliberately ABSENT from `PRODUCT_UPDATE_FIELDS` so the PATCH, bulk edit and CSV import cannot route past that statement. **The dashboard has TWO renderers and both draw the chip and the item** — the client one rebuilds every row on sort/filter, so state written only by the click handler vanishes on the first sort. **The invite card (`StorePlaceholderCard`) says the offer in three registers now:** a rotating hook, one short promise (“חנות משלכם — כוח של קבוצה”), and the capabilities as chips in the tag row that card was already rendering EMPTY to reserve height. It must keep the row’s one-line contract — name one line, tagline one line, tags reserved — because the card stretches to its neighbours but its CONTENT does not, so a taller head pushes only its own pictures down; that was the misalignment, not the thumbnail size (a `max-height` on the thumbs was tried and reverted). A chip needs `.store-card__tag` AND `badge-finish` — the latter IS the fill, border and clip — and differs only by `--badge-hue`; the invite chips take the card’s own ink at weight 600, the weight `.store-sale-chip` settled on for the same errand in the same row. **Its colours are `--color-invite-*`, a SEPARATE list from `--color-tile-*` (2026-08-14)** — those are `MARK_HUES`, what every store's generated identity mark is built from, and `tests/store-image.test.ts` pins the two equal, so editing one to change an invitation card re-colours every store on the site. ELEVEN of them, because the pool size must stay coprime with 2/3/4 (the grid's column counts) or a colour runs down a whole column — which is why "one more shade" became four, and why 11 leaves exactly one repeat across the 12-slot grid instead of five. Ruled out and not to be re-proposed: RED (a warning on a card that is asking for something), GREY and any near-neutral dark hue (it greys out at every wash depth — a slate, the site's navy, and that navy INVERTED to a dark tile were all tried), and VIOLET (the signature of AI-generated apps). `maxWash` is per hue, not one global cap: the cap exists so the line-art keeps 3:1 against the deepest wash behind it, and a dark hue has far more room than a light one — with one cap of 22 the dark hues washed out to nothing and their cards read as unfilled tiles. Every ratio is measured and written beside the value it justifies; `ink` splits the art colour from the wash colour for YELLOW alone, because nothing light enough to read as yellow clears 3:1 against itself. Order is enforced, not described — each hue declares a `family`, no two of a family are adjacent, and the tight pairs are never side by side. Slot 0 is the yellow and slot 1 the olive, by his call, which only holds because the CALLERS pass a 0-based index: they passed `stores.length + i`, so the first invitation's colour depended on how many stores happened to exist. The tiles are lit from ONE direction (`TILE_LIGHT_ANGLE`) — three tiles at three gradient angles read as pictures hung crooked — and the card sets `--store-glow` so its hover edge and the halo behind its plus are its own colour, the same mechanism a real store uses. **/stores closes with OPEN categories:** seed categories nobody sells yet, capped at four and spread every Nth across the vocabulary, linking to registration — a dashed `--open` modifier on the same chip, last in the row, shrinking to nothing on its own as the catalogue fills. **Banner + logo, round two (2026-08-14) — four rounds of “it is cut” were ONE bug and it was never the picture:** `cdnBand` reaches the 3:1 band with `ar_3,c_fill,g_auto`, a SALIENCY crop that cannot be argued with from inside a prompt, and at 16:9 it threw away 41% of the height. Generated at **21:9** now (probed live — the old “16:9 is the widest the API offers” note had never been measured) and **STORED at 3:1**: `cropToDeliveredRatio()` centre-crops through Cloudinary before the upload, so delivery crops nothing, ever. That is conforming rather than special-casing — a real seller’s banner is already 3:1 because the dashboard’s crop tool makes it so, and these were the only banners entering delivery at another shape. Pinned by `tests/showcase-banner-ratio.test.ts` (the number lives in two files that cannot import each other). The MEDIUM moved out of `bannerPrompt()` into a per-store `bannerStyle`/`bannerLettering`, exactly as `logoStyle` holds it for the logo — one hardcoded “wide photograph … polished brass, warm gold” was making four shops one picture four times, and it gilded the store whose whole world is a white studio. `bannerPrompt` also stopped appending `NEGATIVE_PROMPT` (it opens “no text of any kind”, “no logo” — on the one image whose job is the shop’s NAME) and `nameClause` stopped saying “no other writing” one sentence before the tagline is requested: both are prompts arguing with themselves, which a model resolves by picking a side. **A reference image works only when the target IS the reference’s whole subject and shares its medium** — a logo drawn from the whole banner came back as a blurred copy of the shop with no mark in it, so `logoRefCrop` cuts the reference down to the mark first; and a reference pins what a shape looks like, never which way round it is drawn, which is how one crescent faced two ways in two pictures (direction is WORDED on both sides now). Per-store extras: `backdropAccent` + `backdropAccentAlways` (a minority of hero shots on a second backdrop, described as LIGHT on a plain wall so it adds no object to a view whose whole design is one product on an empty field), and `cardProducts`, which the seeder turns into timestamps (`cardAt()`) so the homepage card leads with chosen products — it reshuffled between two reseeds an hour apart before that, because same-day products tie-broke on a `uuid()` minted fresh every seed.
- **Checkout `/checkout`:** Guest+session; email pre-fill; `/api/checkout` re-validates prices server-side and saves `buyerId`; PQV modal; `patchQty()` (no re-render on +/−); stock enforcement (memory `project_checkout_ui`). **It is also where a cart handed over from a seller's own domain lands** — merged from the URL fragment BEFORE anything reads the carts, then the fragment and `?h=` are stripped with `replaceState` so a refresh cannot merge twice (custom-domain bullet above; `cart-handoff.ts`). A handover with no `?store=` supplies the intent slug itself. **Per-item/per-store selection:** checkbox per item + tri-state per store + a global "select all" bar shown only when >1 store; default all checked; unchecking dims the card, live-updates the total, and hides that store's shipping row when nothing is checked. Only checked items are sent and removed from the cart after success (matched by `storeSlug`+`cartKey`). Selected counts shown live per store row + grand total. **Delivery methods (2026-07-27, platform-provided model — "our shipping only", no seller-own; memory `project_shipping_model`):** per-store row = an app select-dropdown of courier / pickup-point at central platform rates (`lib/shipping.ts SHIPPING_RATES`) + self-pickup (free, shows the store address; seller opts in via a settings toggle, blocked without an address). `/api/checkout` takes `deliveryMethods{slug→method}` and **re-validates + re-prices each server-side** (spoofed/unavailable → courier, never zeroes shipping); the choice is saved on `StoreSubtotal.deliveryMethod`. **Payment seam — AUTHORIZE → order rows → CAPTURE (rewritten 2026-08-07, owner's rule: an order exists iff money was really taken).** `lib/payment.ts` `PaymentProvider` (`authorize`/`capture`/`voidCharge`) + `MockPaymentProvider`; its header carries the argument and the full failure table. It used to `charge()` first and write orders second, so a throw in between was a live charge with no order — which the `payment_ref` UNIQUE bug (migration 0017) caused on EVERY multi-store cart. Now the hold comes first, rows are written **'pending'**, and only a successful capture marks them paid and notifies sellers; a decline/throw/failed-capture releases the hold, restocks, and (on capture) marks the written rows failed+cancelled rather than deleting them. `voidRefund` never throws and a failed release journals `charge_voided` + a critical alert — the one outcome that costs a real person money. **Gotcha:** the payment accordion opens via the `data-open` attribute, NOT an `.is-open` class — every opener must set `data-open` (a stale `.is-open` in `ProductQuickView.astro` rotated the arrow but never expanded the section, fixed 2026-07-27). **Order-confirmation nudge (2026-07-27):** if the cart still has items after purchase, `home-cart-chip` rows appear on the success page with a store link + open-cart button (reuses the global `cart:open` drawer). **Stock that runs out UNDER the buyer (2026-07-29) — two layers, and the buyer-facing half is the point:** the guarantee was already there (`decrementStock` is atomic and runs BEFORE the charge, and every failure path restocks), but the refusal reached the buyer as the generic "שגיאה. נסה שוב." — advice that walks them into the identical refusal forever with nothing on screen saying which item. `/api/checkout` now answers `409 {error:'out-of-stock', outOfStock:{storeSlug,productSlug,productName,available,selectedVariants?}}` — a code plus the live count, taken from `decrementStock`'s own `before` inside the mutex, never a second read. The page corrects itself from it: `applyStockLimit` (cart.ts) clamps the quantity, a sold-out line **stays in the cart but leaves the payment** (deselected, danger border, "אזל מהמלאי" note) so the rest of the order still goes through on the next press, and the sentence lands in `#co-price-notice` — the same warning line, `role="status"`, button live — because nothing failed and the buyer did nothing wrong. **The second layer catches most of it earlier:** `/api/cart/prices` now returns each line's `stock` too, so the existing moments-of-attention re-price (load / return to tab / pay) applies it; `applyServerPrices` flags `clampedTo` / `soldOut` and `stockCorrections()` is what the pay button checks BEFORE the price gate — a quantity the buyer chose being silently reduced at the moment they press pay is the one outcome this must never produce. **Stock is answered per variant COMBO, and withheld entirely when the request didn't name one** (an empty `selectedVariants` counts as not named): the shared pool is a different line's ceiling, and a wrong number is worse than a missing one. Hebrew needs its own singular for these ("נותרה יחידה אחת", not "נותרו 1"). Guarded by `tests/cart.test.ts`, `tests/cart-prices.test.ts`, `tests/cart-prices-api.test.ts`, `tests/checkout.test.ts`; verified in a real browser against `astro build` for both the clamp and the sold-out path, on load, on tab-return, and at the pay button. **A filled photo box is `--color-surface`, never the line colour (2026-08-07, owner):** the 80px cart-card thumbnail was filled with `--color-border`, so every background-removed product sat on grey on the one page where the shopper decides to pay. The rule was already written and already guarded — but the guard read `src/styles/**` only, and this was a Tailwind bracket class in markup; `tests/image-container-bg.test.ts` now scans markup too (a fixed, clipping box filled with `--color-bg`/`--color-border`), which no hairline divider or progress track matches.
- **Orders — seller (audited end-to-end 2026-08-02, user: "אסור שיהיו שם טעויות"; three defects found and fixed, each with a guard test): (a) `PATCH /api/seller/orders` never bound the orderId to the caller's store — any seller could cancel/restock/rewrite another store's order by pairing its id with their own slug (`orderBelongsToStore`); (b) the order total was computed inline in five places and three dropped the seller's discount, including the BUYER's own order history, where it then contradicted the discount-aware total on the same card (`lib/order-totals.ts`); (c) the filter badge was a hardcoded hidden "0" while the first paint already showed the "active" preset, and the client kept a second, drifting copy of the status list.** Accordion cards; sort/filter/search via `.orders-toolbar` (server-paginated); live poll 15s inserts a new card only on page 1 (the sorted-by-recency assumption only holds there) and trims page 1 back to 15; toast/badge fire regardless of page. Status dropdown on the shared floating portal; order edit modal (buyer details, item soft-delete, discount). **Fixed (2026-07-15):** `pollOrders()` seeded known-order-ids from the DOM (page-1 only) instead of a full fetch — any store with >15 orders re-toasted old orders as new. **Order-age chip (סשן ג׳):** every not-yet-delivered order shows an age pill (`order-age.ts`, isomorphic SSR + client), background-less; a `pending` order **escalates** calm→amber→red, an in-progress one shows a calm muted age only; no blink (dropped on user request); delivered = no chip. The admin platform-wide Orders panel (`AdminOrdersPanel.astro`) renders the same chip identically calm — the red card border + admin pulse were dropped 2026-07-23 as too loud. **Status vocabulary (2026-07-23):** labelled **"סטטוס הזמנה"**, not "משלוח"; `ready`/ממתין-לאיסוף is omitted from BOTH filters since no seller can reach it (it returns carrier-driven once shipping is wired); admin shows the payment badge **only when NOT paid** (it's an always-'paid' stub today, so "שולם" was pure noise). The seller changes status straight from the collapsed card — the badge opens the same portal menu plus an inline save, and `applyStatusSave()` is the single persist path shared with the in-card Save button. Status→buyer-notify pipeline + cancel/restock: memory `project_order_automation`. **Collapsed card (2026-07-26, narrow layout reworked 2026-07-31; full layout detail in memory `project_order_card_layout`):** wide = price in a fixed slot at an identical x on every card, then ONE fixed-width status cluster holding age-chip · note-icon · status packed together; the cluster's fixed width is what keeps the price column-aligned. **Narrow is a container query on the CARD** (`@container/ordcard`, flips at 640px of the card's content box) — never `sm:`, because the admin copy sits beside a sidebar and viewport width says nothing about the card's room. Under it the header is a 3-column grid with both wrappers on `display:contents`: row 1 id/date · buyer · chevron, row 2 price · cluster, the price `text-start` in column 1 so it ends at the same x as the id above it (owner: a price in a slot of its own "לא קשור לכלום"). The old mobile column-stack let the cluster claim its full 186px min-content beside the buyer name — min card width 479px against a 375px viewport, the card scrolled sideways and the buyer name was 0px wide. **Only the age chip yields:** its slot may shrink and clip (`min-w-0 overflow-hidden`) while the chip itself is `shrink-0`, or the squeeze lands on the 11px clock SVG (measured at 0px wide — the squish the owner caught once, by a new route); the cluster is 13.5rem because at 12rem the longest real pair overflowed and the chip printed through the price. `tests/order-card-layout.test.ts` pins all of it across the three renderers; admin↔seller identical. **Multi-note seller notes (2026-07-26):** `Order.sellerNotes` is `Record<slug,string[]>` (read via `orderStoreNotes()`, coerces legacy string→list); `/api/seller/orders` PATCH takes a full-replace `sellerNotes:string[]`, ownership-gated, managed independently of the status save. Delete is an **inline ✓/✗ confirm on the row, no modal**; `renderNotes()` sets text via textContent (no XSS).
