TDZ gotcha: `bindOrderCard` reads note i18n, so `let ordersDashI18n` must be declared ABOVE it — it and `tt()` now sit at the very top of `initOrdersTab`. **The client rebuild was never translated (fixed 2026-07-31):** ~30 Hebrew literals in `orders.ts` (card body, cancel-confirm, new-order toast, the whole edit-order modal) plus an `orderAgeChipHtml(..., 'he')` pinned to Hebrew, so switching the dashboard to English gave an English shell around Hebrew order cards. The `?? 'Hebrew'` fallback pattern is what hid it — the key existed, so the code read as translated. One `tt(key, n?)` accessor, no per-site fallback, and the language off `document.documentElement.lang`; `tests/orders-i18n.test.ts` fails on any Hebrew literal outside a comment or a key either dictionary lacks. Admin stays Hebrew-only by design.
- **Payments tab (תשלומים) — the seller's own money.** Rewritten three times on 2026-08-11 — for SCOPE, for SIMPLICITY, then for scope again when the first pass had not gone far enough. **Scope, final form:** both tiles are THIS store's, and so is the whole overview money row — *"כמה שפחות לערבב בין חנויות יחד, זה אמור להיות מידע משני ולא מרכזי. הוא נמצא על חנות א׳ אז הוא צריך לקבל מידע על חנות א׳"*. Exactly THREE things on the seller's dashboard are account-wide, all multi-store-only and each saying so on ITSELF rather than in a heading over everything: the two lines under the tiles (`תשלום קרוב מכל החנויות שלך` / `ממתינים מכל החנויות שלך` — the same pair as the tiles, asked of the account) and the note on the bank card. The subtitle's old "כולל את כל החנויות שלך" is gone; so is the overview's cross-store tile. `order-payout-line.ts#payableHeadlineAgorot` is the shared rule behind the headline — a multi-store seller sees the shop's RELEASED money, a single-store seller the account figure — so the tab and the overview cannot answer differently about one shop, and `reporting-invariants` pins that every shop's held share sums to the account line beside it. **Simplicity (owner, סשן א׳: *"מאז שהתחלנו עם התשלומים הכל התבלגן לי… זה החלק הכי רגיש בפלטפורמה והוא צריך להיות מאוד פשוט למשתמש"*):** the hold rule is stated ONCE, in `#pay-how` at the bottom — four questions, four short answers, every constant interpolated from `payout-schedule.ts` — instead of restated in the subtitle, three tile hints and every table row; per-order reasons are LABELS (`טרם נמסר`), not the thirty-day story, with the ADMIN copy of the same keys keeping the numbers because that is a support screen; `ממתין לאישור סופי` became `ממתינים` with a `פרטים נוספים` link; a filled-in bank account collapses to a masked line + `ערוך`, since six open inputs read as unfinished work; and `שולם בעבר` plus the payout history LEFT for the Reports tab, where a period and an export make them reconcilable. **The held-orders TABLE is gone** — it was a second order list one tab from the Orders tab; what is left is three lines splitting the waiting money BY REASON, and the per-order sentence moved to the order's own card (`lib/order-payout-line.ts`, shared by all three card renderers and the admin's). Each reason links into this store's Orders tab filtered by the `סטטוס תשלום` column (`?opay=`, a real filter column — the first version faked it by naming shipping statuses, which is the hold rule restated in a URL), the tab says why it is narrowed and offers the way out. **Bank + business details live in this panel only** (`feedback_seller_form_burden`; a store-opening copy was tried and rejected the same day): `lib/payout-details.ts` validates them (bank = all four fields or none, since `payout-run.ts#bankOf` cannot transfer on three; business id = 9 digits, check digit deliberately NOT enforced) and composes the closed summary's business line for both its renderers, `/api/seller/payout-details` writes with its OWN statement so a clear reaches the column as NULL, and a change is ANNOUNCED in a notification that names no part of the account — it is read where the dashboard is not, and will be an email. Cancelling an edit goes through `unsaved-guard.ts#discardChanges`, never `form.reset()`, which after an AJAX save restores what the page LOADED with and would silently put the previous account back. A banner and a red tab dot appear on ONE condition — `payableNowAgorot > 0 && !hasPayableBank` — from the same predicate. **Every number comes from `buildSellerAccount` and none is computed in the component**, and `reporting-invariants` proves the three routes to payable-now (screen, header dot, payout run) agree. Server-rendered — that is what pays for the tab badge — and it re-reads the server's own rendering when an order moves (`dash:mutated`), because a JSON endpoint plus a renderer here is the `project_client_renderer_i18n_drift` class on a money screen. Nothing on it pages any more, and the rule that machinery taught survives it: a handler bound where the MARKUP is stacks another copy on every refresh, so it goes where the PANEL is. **`#pay-how` answers "why 21 days", not "why"** — the owner asked *"למה 21 מרגע המסירה ולא 15 למשל?"*, so the question carries the number and the answer carries the statutory 14 (`STATUTORY_RETURN_DAYS`) that makes it checkable. ⚠️ Two wrong arguments were made for that number in one day and both are recorded at the constant; the short version is that a DECLARED cancellation and a RETURNED product are different events at different times, so a day counter is a proxy for the returns mechanism nobody has built. **Its one skeleton is for the REFRESH, not first paint** (`data-skel` + `setPayoutsBusy`): server-rendered means nothing to wait for on load, but re-reading the panel after a status change showed the figures from BEFORE it — stale money wearing the confidence of fresh money. A failed refresh puts the numbers back.
- **Order SLA job (2026-08-10, GO_LIVE §5.0-ב) — the only job that gives money back to a person:** `lib/order-sla.ts` had been written, tested and never called, so an order the seller never shipped left the buyer's money with the PLATFORM indefinitely (the payout gate refused to pass it on, and nothing sent it back). `order-sla` in the registry (daily) → `lib/order-sla-run.ts`: warns the seller on day 7, cancels + restocks + records `refund_due` on day 14, reports the agorot owed. SQL narrows to a NECESSARY condition (erring a day wide, because the business calendar and a raw timestamp disagree by hours) and `fulfilmentStatus` decides. Idempotent through the status table (a cancelled order is not a candidate) and through the seller's own notification feed (the notification IS the record of "already said"). **The extraction is the load-bearing half:** `lib/order-status-change.ts` now owns every consequence of a status move — journal, refund obligation, restock, buyer notification, pending store closure — with `settleStatusChange` for a caller that did its own write (the seller route) and `moveOrderStatus` for one that has no route (this job; the courier webhook next). A second copy of that sequence would have been a second definition of what a cancellation does to stock and to money.
- **Reports tab (דוחות, 2026-08-10):** four exportable reports — sales by ORDER (date · customer · before-discount · discount · sale total · commission · your payout · counted?), products (units + revenue per product beside live stock), stock (a count sheet, no period — a stocktake is about NOW), and **payouts** — `העברות לבנק` (2026-08-11, owner: the Payments tab's lifetime "שולם בעבר" felt accounting-shaped and belonged here with a period and an export; renamed from "תשלומים מהפלטפורמה", and its commission column from "עמלה שנגבתה", once he read them and asked what they meant) — transfer date · earned-period · transferred · platform commission · state, the ONE report that is account-wide rather than about the store in the switcher (`ACCOUNT_WIDE_REPORTS`; the slug is still authorization, the rows come from the session's seller id), windowed on `sentAt` because that is the day the bank statement shows, and a `failed` transfer is listed and excluded from the totals like a cancelled order. Its picker card carries a `כל החנויות` chip drawn FROM `ACCOUNT_WIDE_REPORTS`, so the scope is answered before the click and a future account-wide report is marked without touching the panel. CSV only, by owner's decision: it is what a bookkeeper asks for, `csv-bulk.ts` already produces one Excel opens in Hebrew, and a PDF would buy a rendering library and a second layout for nothing. `lib/seller-reports.ts` is PURE (rows from orders+products the caller holds) so the invariants are assertable without a DB; `seller-report-shapes.ts` holds ids/row types with no server import, because the client needs them and `seller-reports.ts` reaches `db.ts`. Rules are never re-implemented: `countsAsRevenue`, `orderNetForStore`, `businessDayISO`, `commissionOnAgorot` — the SAME function Performance's expense line uses. The one new sum is `money.ts#allocateAgorot` (largest-remainder), because an order carries ONE discount and a per-product report must still add up to the sales one; `tests/reporting-invariants.test.ts` asserts all three agree to the agora over one window. **Nothing says "נטו"** — the field name leaked into the UI and in Hebrew נטו means what is left AFTER deductions, so two columns competed for it; the row spells the arithmetic instead. Dates: DD/MM/YYYY on screen, ISO in the FILE (a spreadsheet parses ISO anywhere and renders it in the reader's locale; `03/08/2026` is March 8th on a US machine). Range picker + sort header are the SHARED ones (`date-range.ts#periodRange`/`PERIOD_PRESETS`, `.sort-btn`/`.sort-icon`) — both were invented locally first and both had to be redone. A new REPORT resets the sort, a new PERIOD keeps it.
- **The site's own field-validation message (2026-08-10, `lib/field-validity.ts` + `scripts/form-validity.ts`):** every form outside checkout showed the BROWSER's bubble — OS-styled, in the browser's UI language, gone on the next click. It cancels the REPORT, not the checking: `preventDefault()` on the `invalid` event kills the bubble while the browser still refuses to submit. Three things fall out — a form written with `novalidate` says "I validate myself" and never fires it (checkout is opted out with no flag), forms rendered later are covered since the listener is on `document`, and if the module fails to load the native bubble is still there. The signal is the field's LINE turning danger plus a `role="alert"` line under it; focus moves to the first bad field, which cancelling the event otherwise costs.
- **A failed request either speaks or says why it does not (2026-08-10, audit — `lib/toast.ts#showActionFailedToast`, `tests/silent-failure-guard.test.ts`):** every `fetch` in the app was walked. Sixteen handled failure, eleven did not, and it was one habit rather than eleven oversights. Dead actions fixed: four message sends, order status save + quick-menu + CANCEL (which also restocks), note save and delete, filter/sort/page on orders+products+messages, the ad campaign list. Failures dressed as FACTS, separated: the store grid's "טען עוד" hid its own button and category filters blanked the grid (a failed request claiming an empty catalogue), header search said "no results", the coupons list showed its empty state, the performance breakdown said "no sales in this period". **`ConfirmModal` was the big one** — the funnel EVERY destructive action goes through ran `try/finally` with no `catch`, so a delete whose fetch was dropped closed the dialog exactly as a success does. Bulk product delete counted nothing and claimed everything. The guard requires a `catch` around a `fetch` to report or carry a `silent:` marker with a reason; error toasts are now tellable from success ones (danger bar + `role="alert"`).
- **Performance tab (ביצועים):** revenue/orders/visits + unique-visitors KPIs, charts and top products over a date range (`seller-performance.ts`; `chart-svg.ts` isomorphic bar/line/donut SVGs). Revenue = bar; **orders = a line over the same buckets** (2026-07-31) — on this dashboard bars carry ₪ and lines carry counts, so the form says which unit you are reading before the axis does, and orders shares the revenue accent because both are the sales story. Revenue+orders sit side by side, visits spans the full row beneath them, and `chartPixelWidth(id)` now measures **per chart** — one shared width would render the full-width one at half size and let the browser stretch it. Visitors = a **two-line** chart — unique (solid, area fill) vs total visits (dashed muted envelope) on one axis, the gap between them being returning traffic; one hover column shows both series. **Unique visitors** dedupe via a stable first-party httpOnly `sn_vid` cookie set in `middleware.ts`: `store-pageviews.ts` keeps a `{total,visitors[]}` daily bucket per store (legacy bare-number rows coerce), range-unique = the union of daily id sets, conversion = orders/**unique** (falls back to total views when no ids exist, e.g. seeded demo data). Default range **thisMonth** — the SSR default must match the picker's default preset or they drift on first paint; presets include today + thisWeek (Sunday-start); preset date math uses **local-calendar ISO (`toISODate`), not `toISOString`** (UTC shifts the 1st back a day). The picker's `maxHeight` is raised so all presets + the custom row show without an inner scroll. **Profitability card:** gross revenue − platform commission = net profit — reporting only, the real deduction happens at the processor. The commission arithmetic itself is `pricing.ts#commissionOnAgorot` (money.ts's `percentOf` is the ILS version and rounds to two decimals, which on agorot leaves a fraction of an agora), shared with `lib/seller-balance.ts` so this figure and the admin's accrued balance cannot drift by a rounding. Top-products bars fill by **share of the period's whole product revenue** (`summary.productRevenueAgorot`, via `lib/top-product-share.ts`) — it was a share of the 5 shown until 2026-08-11, which made those five add to 100% by construction however long the tail behind them was, so the leader read as a third of the platform either way. Rows are one component + one client renderer (`TopProductsList.astro` / `performance.ts#renderTopProducts`); the three Astro copies they replaced had already drifted, two of them printing agorot through `formatPrice` (a SHEKEL formatter) so every money figure on the admin's per-store performance page rendered ×100 until the first client repaint — `shared-ui-recipe-guards` now scans `.astro` too, which is where all seven sites lived. On the ADMIN platform list each row also names the store that sold it (linked) and a name search filters the list alone: it narrows neither the KPIs nor the denominator, so a searched row still reports its share of the period (`?productQ=`, filtered in the query's OUTER select while `grand_total` is a window over the INNER groups). A revenue bar click opens a **donut modal** of that bucket's full product composition (reuses `/api/seller/performance?products=all`; month-end via `Date.UTC` to match the summary's UTC buckets); the legend lists every product and scrolls, `sliceColors` falls back to a generated evenly-spaced HSL scale past the 6 brand hues so no two slices merge. **Per-product drill-down (2026-07-20):** a searchable product picker (shared floating portal, sticky opaque search whose sticky `top` is pulled up by the portal's own padding so rows can't peek above it, + a clear row that collapses the section) → `/api/seller/performance?productId=` → that product's KPIs + charts for the current range (`buildProductPerformance`, `PerfProductSection.astro`, shared with the admin per-store page). **Product "views" = page loads + quick-view/modal opens, TOTAL not unique** (`product-pageviews.ts`); an info-tooltip spells this out. **Chart gotchas:** a dashed overlay line must skip `pathLength="1"` (it normalises the path to length 1, so the dasharray covers it end-to-end and renders solid) — only the animated primary keeps it. Line-chart tooltips (`tooltip.ts`) must match the `.chart-point` group, not the rect (the dot is a sibling, so hovering it misses `.chart-bar` and blanks the tip) and re-position only on point *change* to avoid flicker; bars stay cursor-anchored. **No SVG `<title>`** on any mark (stacks a native tooltip). A donut tooltip needs `mountTooltipIn(dialog)` — a `<body>`-anchored one paints *behind* a `showModal()` dialog. Charts render at the container's real pixel width (`ResizeObserver`, seeded from SSR) + `preserveAspectRatio="none"` + **`style="direction:ltr"`** — a `dir` *attribute* is ignored for SVG text, so on the RTL page date/number labels bidi-flip and clip off the edge. Lines are **smooth monotone-cubic curves** (`smoothPathD`, 2026-07-31) — monotone, not Catmull-Rom, so the curve can't overshoot a point and dip the area fill below the zero baseline. **The entrance animation cost four rounds and has three rules (2026-07-31):** (1) SSR renders **static** (`animate:false` on all three surfaces) and only the client's first paint animates — SSR and client draw different geometry, so whoever draws the FINAL one must own the animation; (2) the SVG **pins `height` as an inline style**, because `reset.css`'s `svg{height:auto}` beats the attribute and derived it from the viewBox ratio instead (measured 186.6px SSR vs 200px client — every chart grew on the handoff, and the axis text was squashed 6% until then); (3) `armEntrance()` holds a below-the-fold chart at frame 0 (`.chart-hold`) until an `IntersectionObserver` says it's on screen, then **strips the animate-* classes on `animationend`** — a CSS animation restarts on every `display:none`→visible, which is what the tab strip does, so without the strip "leave the tab and come back" replayed it at random. A resize repaint still passes `animate:false`. The Y-axis gutter is sized to the value labels (`yGutter`); zero-value bars draw no stub; the modal opens with `min-height` + 180ms-delayed loading (no flash on a fast fetch) + `autofocus` on the body so `showModal` doesn't ring the close button. **Only humans are counted (סשן ה׳, `lib/bot-detect.ts`):** every view counter and funnel event is gated on `isBotRequest` in BOTH entry points — middleware (page loads) and `/api/store-product` (a quick-view open counts a product view too). `robots.txt` deliberately invites 13+ crawlers, and each one was landing in the numbers a seller reads; "480 views, 0 orders" teaches them the mall sends traffic that doesn't buy when most of it was never a person. Detection is User-Agent only (we only need to EXCLUDE a bot, never to trust its identity), biased toward false positives — a missed view costs nothing, an inflated one gets decided on. `tests/bot-detect.test.ts` imports the `AI_AGENTS` list from the `robots.txt` route itself and asserts every agent named there is filtered, so inviting a new crawler can't quietly re-open this. **Nor is the seller's own storefront a visit:** middleware and `/api/store-product` both skip the counters (and the funnel `page_view`) when `getSellerSession` matches the store's `sellerId` — the dashboard's "צפה בחנות" sends a seller here constantly while setting up, and that page IS the live store, so every look was inflating the number they judge the mall by. Visits to OTHER stores still count; there they really are a shopper. **Same family, dev side (historical):** the app used to write pageviews into `data/`, so each write read as a source edit and forced a reload that wrote again (5 reloads in 20s, 1642 phantom `page_view`s in a day). `data/` is gone; the watcher exclusion in `astro.config.mjs` is its residue.
- **Advertising tab (פרסום):** seller-controlled ad budget — **not wired to a real Google/Meta API yet**, demo data only; swap the data source, keep the UI. **How to VERIFY that connection once it exists is written down (2026-07-30, GO_LIVE §2.5)** — feed approvals in Merchant Center, Tag/Pixel assistants, the two official sandboxes, then the one that actually settles it: our numbers cross-checked against Ads Manager for the same range. The dashboard fabricates its own figures, so it looks identical whether the connection works or not. (The "hide the tab / mark בקרוב" gate that used to be here is gone: §2 became launch-blocking on 2026-08-04, so a real seller and an unconnected tab never coexist.) **§2.5 layer 1 stopped being a thing you remember to do (2026-08-06)** — job `merchant-status` asks Merchant Center and Meta Catalog every 4h what they did with our feed, because both reject rows silently and the manual version was one reading of a state that changes on every product edit. Four outcomes, and separating them is the point: no answer / feed-level rejection or empty catalogue / ids matching no product (checked over EVERY row — a broken join looks like a healthy report about nobody) / one row disapproved, the only one that reaches the seller. A mass rejection is held and reported as ours, like the domain check's demote ceiling. **The rule the whole thing rests on: an unrecognised answer is `null`, never "no problems"** — these two APIs cannot be tested against reality until the accounts exist, so a wrong field name that parsed as "zero rejected" would report health forever. Inert until four env vars exist; from a PROD build with them missing it says so weekly. **Scope (2026-07-30):** a boost covers the whole store, hand-picked products, or whole categories — the two narrowed scopes are multi-pick (one product is a list of one, stored in the long-standing flat shape), both forms come from the single `BoostFormFields.astro`, and the wording from `ad-scope-label.ts`. Budget is a ladder of presets + "סכום אחר" over one shared rule (`ad-budget.ts`); picking "סכום אחר" leaves the field **completely empty** — no leftover preset, no placeholder, no spinner arrows and deliberately not `required` (2026-07-31) — and an out-of-range amount is told in ONE Hebrew line under the field **on blur, never per keystroke** ("5" on the way to "500" is not a mistake yet; it clears live once shown). The submit guard reuses that same line, so an empty field and a bad number can never produce two different messages, and the numbers in it come from the constants rather than prose. **A campaign is stopped by whatever it advertises, and WHO stopped it decides who may restart it** (`ad-campaign-health.ts`): off the storefront — hidden/blocked/deleted product, or an admin-blocked store — pauses it as `'unavailable'` and only a human resumes; everything sold out pauses it as `'out-of-stock'` and the same sweep resumes it by itself when stock returns; a fixed duration that ran out archives it into history. Partly gone/sold out keeps running and the card says how many. Resume is refused server-side (409) in all three cases, and the create form's rule — on the storefront AND in stock — is enforced in `buildCampaignInput`, not only in the picker. **Campaigns are never deleted (2026-07-30):** cancelling archives (`archivedAt`) into a collapsed, read-only "קמפיינים קודמים" block on both the seller tab and the admin twin (`CampaignHistory.astro`, 50 shown, all rows kept) — every reported figure is summed from these rows, so a delete rewrote a past month's ad spend, and `updateCampaign` refuses an archived row. **Two figures were lying and were fixed the same day:** (a) spend is PACING × DELIVERY (`ad-metrics.ts#spendOver`) — a fixed-duration budget is a total for that period so pacing divides by the duration, not always 30 (a 7-day 500₪ boost reported ~117₪ after running its full week), while delivery is a seeded 82–98% because a cap is a CEILING and the auction decides what is actually spent; pacing alone would have turned "משלמים רק על הוצאה בפועל" into a guaranteed charge, which is a different product. (b) the baseline card claimed "active" + a flat number for every store, including ones the feed doesn't carry — `ad-baseline.ts` now answers both over the 30 days its label names. (c) ROAS was seeded independently of conversions, so a card could read "0 מכירות" beside "x4.96 תשואה" — it is now derived (sales × a seeded basket), and zero sales means zero return. **(e) "מכירות" is an ATTRIBUTION, not an order count (2026-07-31, user asked what it counts)** — the label now reads "מכירות (משוער)" and the glossary says so plainly. The rule the real integration must report under lives in `ad-metrics.ts`'s `RangeStat.conversions` JSDoc: click-through with a lookback window (Google 30d, Meta 7d-click), so a buyer who clicked, left and returned days later **is** counted — "only bought immediately" is the wrong mental model. It cannot be exact (cross-device, cleared cookies, modelled conversions, and two networks can each claim the same sale). The deterministic version is first-party (`gclid`/`fbclid`/`utm_*` → cookie → stamped on the order), and **its capture half is now BUILT (2026-08-04, `lib/attribution.ts` + migration 0010)** — brought forward past its original "when the ad accounts open" trigger because attribution can only be recorded as it happens, so every month without it is a month of orders nothing can attribute in hindsight. **Nothing reads `orders.attribution` yet, on purpose**: until real accounts exist no link carries those parameters, and a true 0 beside mock thousands is worse than either. The report that reads it, and removing "(משוער)" from the label, go together with the real integration — `GO_LIVE_CHECKLIST.md` §2.5 layer 5. **Two silent defects were found and fixed writing it, both invisible from any screen:** (i) the feed's `g:id` was the product uuid while all four `dataLayer`/`fbq` events sent the SLUG — and both networks join a catalog item to a browsing event by exactly that id, so they matched on nothing; the slug was also not platform-unique (0001 measured 47 shared across stores) and follows the product name, so a rename would have retired one id and invented another. `lib/ad-item-id.ts` is now the single definition both sides build from. (ii) `trackAddToCart` sent that same id to `/api/analytics/event`, which takes only a uuid and drops anything else — so every first-party `add_to_cart` was recorded with NO product, and the admin Data tab's "top added"/"top abandoned" lists have always been empty. `TrackItem` now carries the two ids as separate fields and reports each half independently. (d) the fee a seller is TOLD about and the one the platform BOOKS were two numbers — `pricing.ts#boostFeePercent` makes the config field an override of `AD_PLATFORM_MARGIN_PERCENT` instead of a separate, unset truth. **DECIDED 2026-07-30 (owner): the management fee comes OUT of the budget, as a percentage of the actual ad spend.** So a cap is a ceiling on what the SELLER PAYS: charge = adSpend × (1 + fee%), and the advertising it buys is the charge minus the fee — never a bill on top of the cap. `RangeStat` therefore carries BOTH `spend` (his cost) and `adSpend` (what reached Google/Meta): impressions are priced off `adSpend` (charging for exposure the fee never bought was the alternative), platform income is their difference rather than a percentage re-derived at the revenue view, the admin exposure box counts `adSpend` (media, not income), and the fee split appears on the ADMIN card only (`data-show-fee`) — never the seller's: his fee is disclosed where he DECIDES (the budget tooltip, before committing) and belongs on the invoice, while repeating it on every card is a number he cannot act on competing with the ones he can. The platform's own brand campaigns have `adSpend === spend` — it does not bill itself. Boost campaigns carry a **duration** (7/14/30/ongoing) + an **audience** (`gender`+`age_group`) **auto-derived from the product** (`audience-infer.ts`, pre-filled, overridable) — see Hard rules → Ads for why these are product-*for* feed attributes, not viewer targeting. A **store-scope** boost hides the audience fields entirely (each product self-targets; the server drops any posted audience for store scope). A collapsible PPC glossary + info-tooltips explain the concepts for a non-PPC seller. **Product feed — DONE:** `/api/feed/products.xml` exports every visible product as a Google Merchant / Meta Catalog RSS 2.0 feed (`product-feed.ts`), variants expanded to `item_group_id` rows, public+cached, blocked-gated — only needs a real Merchant Center account + real domain to go live. **PRE-BUILT since 2026-08-09, and so is `/sitemap-content.xml` (GO_LIVE §7):** both used to assemble the entire platform catalogue in memory inside the request, on the one event loop every shopper shares (6.1s at 45 stores here; hundreds of MB in one process at a thousand sellers). A job builds each every 30 minutes into `generated_artifacts` (migration 0022) and the routes stream it. **The half that is easy to miss: the jobs run in the SAME process, so moving the build off the request path is only half the fix** — it is built in windows of 20 stores and flushed every ~256KB, and every flush is an `await` that hands the loop back. Output is unchanged and that is asserted byte for byte (`tests/catalog-artifacts.test.ts`), and `tests/public-route-unbounded-build.test.ts` scans `src/pages/` so the next route that fans out over every store fails instead of shipping. **The second bug the first one uncovered, same day:** a sitemap FILE may hold no more than 50,000 URLs and is rejected WHOLE above that (sitemaps.org, checked — not recalled), so at the very scale this work was for the symptom would have been a platform with no content sitemap and no error anywhere; `/sitemap-content.xml` is a `<sitemapindex>` now over shards cut at 45,000, written even when there is one shard. **Hardened 2026-08-02 against SILENT rejection — the whole class is in memory `project_feed_silent_rejection_class`, and every one of these looked fine from inside the app (the product sits on the storefront, no ad ever runs, nothing says why):** (a) XML-illegal control characters — a description pasted out of Word carries U+000B — cannot be escaped into legality, so ONE anywhere made the document unparseable and Merchant dropped **every store's** products; `xmlText()` is now the single gate both escapers call. (b) `description` is a REQUIRED attribute but `api/product.ts` defaults it to `''` (only `name` is enforced) → falls back to the title. (c) title/description now clamped to Google's 150/5000 without splitting a surrogate pair. (d) `image_link` must be ABSOLUTE — `sanitizeImageUrl` keeps a site-relative path by design, so `toAbsoluteImageUrl` resolves it against the feed origin and a product ships if ANY of its images survives. Verified across all 2,253 stored images: 0 changed, 0 dropped. **A VARIANT product was the un-audited half, and it has no parent row to fall back on (2026-08-06 — found by the owner asking "what happens if a product is entirely variants?"):** four things that describe the COMBINATION were derived once and spread onto every row — the image (a `color=אדום` row shipped `images[0]`, i.e. the blue photo, which for a colour variant is the one mismatch a reviewer checks a variant row FOR), the link (every row landed on a page that pre-selects nothing, so a paid click for red/42 arrived with nothing chosen — now `variant-landing.ts`, read server-side so the swatch, stock line and add-to-cart ceiling are right in the first paint), `custom_label_2` (from the shared pool while the availability ATTRIBUTE came from the combo's bucket, so "clear low stock" bid on rows Google served as in-stock), and the page's JSON-LD (`InStock` whenever ANY combo had stock, contradicting an `out_of_stock` row that had just landed on it). **The durable half is `tests/feed-spec-conformance.test.ts`** — the published Google AND Meta specs written as data (they are NOT one list: Meta requires `brand` + `condition` where Google recommends them, and the caps differ) and run over a catalogue built out of edge cases, parsed with a real XML parser. That is the shape that catches this class: the existing feed tests asserted what we MEANT, and every silent rejection here has been a case where each side was internally consistent and only the join was wrong. **Metric tiers (סשן ג׳):** cards show **CTR** + a נמוך/בינוני/גבוה chip on CTR & ROAS only (`ad-tier.ts`, pure/isomorphic) — volume metrics (impressions/clicks/spend) are deliberately untagged since low/high there just scales with budget and would mislead. `AdCampaign.platform` gained **'both'** (Google+Meta together, blended CPM, now the default). **Per-campaign lifetime model (סשן ד׳):** pausing **FREEZES** accrued metrics at the pause moment (`pausedAt` + `ad-metrics.ts#runPeriod`), never zeroes them; cards default to **lifetime** ("מתחילת הקמפיין") with a run-period label. The range picker sits above the campaign list (one trigger + floating portal matching the Performance picker, `lifetime` default preset) and windows only the cards; baseline exposure is a stable lifetime figure rendered SSR, decoupled from the picker. `resolveAdRange` (date-range.ts) + `withCampaignStats` (ad-metrics.ts) are shared by both ad routes (was byte-duplicated). **Billing (2026-07-21, locked with user — memory `project_boost_billing_model`):** the budget is a **CEILING, pay only for actual spend**. Labels say "תקרת תקציב" (per mode via `budgetLabel`/`adBudgetLabelFor`), never "תקציב חודשי"; duration labels are **verbal** (שבוע/שבועיים/חודש), not "7/14/30 ימים"; a static `adBillingTimingNote` appears in the seller boost form AND both admin advertising surfaces — the transparency the user asked for. Sellers top up a live campaign via an inline "עדכן תקציב" editor — **find the card via `[data-status]`, NOT `[data-campaign-id]`, since the button carries that id too.** `ads.boostCommissionPercent` is a `null` config placeholder until the % is decided (GO_LIVE). **Who pays whom (discussed, not built — GO_LIVE §2 + §3):** the platform's own ad accounts pay Google/Meta and the platform rebills the seller via auth→incremental-capture; that is reselling a service, NOT money transmission. **Store-level imagery (2026-07-28, `lib/store-image.ts`):** every store resolves to four exact ratios (logo 512² / square 1080² / portrait 1080×1350 / landscape 1200×628) — the seller's upload cropped by Cloudinary to the exact size, or, when there is no upload (or the URL can't be transformed, so the ratio isn't guaranteed), a deterministic generated mark served as a real PNG from `/api/store-image/<slug>/<format>.png`. **⚠️ These are NOT boost creative, corrected 2026-08-09 after the owner refused to believe they were ("מה שמעניין לפרסם זה את המוצרים" — he was right twice while I argued):** every boost scope advertises PRODUCTS and the creative is the product's own feed photo. `ad_campaigns` has no image column, the boost form has no file input, and migration 0016 pauses a campaign when ITS PRODUCTS lack a photo. **'store' scope is not a brand ad** — the form promises the seller every product, each auto-targeted by its category. The only uploaded ad creative here is the platform's own `brand-campaigns.ts`, which has one *because* it has no product. What the map genuinely serves is `og:image` and the mark endpoint's whitelist. Rasterised in-repo (`png.ts`, no sharp/resvg) and **font-free on purpose** — a text glyph would need a server font, and Hebrew is exactly where that silently renders boxes. Same source feeds `og:image` on store+product pages; JSON-LD gets `logo` ONLY for a real upload (a generated tile is the store's mark, not a claim about the business). **In Meta, the round avatar on an ad is the Page identity — always the platform's, never the store's** (one Page runs everything); Google PMax takes a per-asset-group logo, so a store boost there can carry the store's own. **Dropdown gotcha (2026-07-21):** the portal menu is pinned to the trigger's EXACT width and must be re-aligned immediately on open — `open()` first measures it at its INTRINSIC width (`whitespace-nowrap` = max-content, so a long option stretches past the trigger despite min-width) and positioned against that, so it opened offset until the next scroll snapped it back. `refreshSelectDropdown()` re-syncs the trigger label after a programmatic value set.
- **Orders — buyer / buyer dashboard:** filtered by `buyerId||buyerEmail`; accordion with thumbnail strip. No payment-status badge (an order existing implies paid); shipping-status only. **ONE CARD PER PURCHASE, not per store (2026-08-05, `lib/buyer-purchases.ts`)** — checkout writes a row per store so each seller owns an isolated record, and that split was leaking to the buyer as two cards, two ids and two dates for one click. Grouped by `checkoutRef`+`paymentRef` (8 hex chars is thin for a key that decides which rows share a TOTAL), headline = the least-advanced live slice, per-store status/tracking/subtotal inside, grand total only when there IS more than one store. Two names then "ועוד N", never a CSS ellipsis through a store's name. Server-paginated 5 PURCHASES/page (grouping happens before filtering, or a page splits one order), AJAX with a card skeleton shown only after a 200ms delay; "פעילות"/"היסטוריה" split on the `buyerAwaiting` column, never a bare `=== 'delivered'` — which left a cancelled order in "active" forever, unlabelled, printing the raw English status. Search is a plain single field — a recent-searches dropdown was tried and reverted, keep it simple. **Card header is a 3-column grid with named areas (2026-07-30):** the order-id/date line is its own full-width row ABOVE the thumbs at every width — same place on every card, with or without images (user's call). Inline it collided: three 52px thumbs leave the text column ~145px at 375px while the id + a date-with-time need ~165px. The thumb strip keeps `margin-inline-end` rather than a grid `column-gap`, because an imageless order renders no `.o-thumbs` and a gap would leave a phantom indent; the loading skeleton mirrors the same two rows or the swap reads as a jump. **Fixed:** Chrome's password manager offered saved emails into the search field because hidden `type=password` inputs existed elsewhere in the live DOM — they now live in a `<template>`, cloned in on first "show password" click. `escB()` gained quote-escaping (a real stored-XSS gap in AJAX-rebuilt cards), and an SSR/AJAX inconsistency where the two read different i18n sections for the same status label. The client rebuild reads its strings off a `#buyer-i18n` dataset with an ENGLISH literal fallback, so a key the element does not publish renders English inside a Hebrew page — silently, and only on the AJAX path; `tests/buyer-dashboard-i18n.test.ts` fails on a half-wired key and on a status either label map is missing. **Mobile is full-bleed (2026-08-02):** panel + sticky hamburger bar both edge to edge on one 0.8rem gutter, replacing ~38px of chrome per side. The dock lost its block padding too — the grey stripes only ever existed to keep a FLOATING card off the header and stop the page showing through its sides, and a full-width opaque bar has neither problem. Each seam carries one hairline: bar and panel drop their top border because the element above already draws one.
- **Admin dashboard (`/admin`):** built on the seller dashboard's tab shell — overview, sellers, stores (a flat list, because the Sellers tab buries stores 3 levels deep), orders, messages, alerts, and the three money tabs (payouts → money journal → accounting statement, in that order: what is about to happen, what already did, what a closed period adds up to). **No `attention` tab since 2026-08-11** — it could only ever say one thing, "0 מוצרים", about stores the Stores tab already lists with that count on every row, so it is a chip there (`?stempty=1`) that composes with the state filters and the search it never had. View-only either way; it never gated a seller. `admin-auth.ts` centralizes the cookie check. **Alerts tab (2026-08-05):** every row carries a severity badge (`lib/error-severity.ts`) and a plain-Hebrew line saying what the failure MEANT, above the technical message — `/api/checkout` + `500` say everything except whether a customer was hurt. Chips filter by severity; `?alref=<code>` is the alert mail's deep link and narrows to that one row with its details already open. The row, the mail and the copy button all render one `errorCopyText()`, because they had drifted into describing the same failure three ways. Every list tab — the money journal included since סשן ד׳ — is server-paginated (`pagination.ts`, 15/page); a tab that renders its whole list on one page is the bug, not a shortcut. Multi-value filter params go through `encodeList`/`decodeList` — a plain comma-join breaks on a value containing a comma. **Every filter param has ONE owning tab (`ADMIN_TAB_PARAMS`, סשן ד׳):** all tabs share the one `/admin?` URL and the tab controller only rewrites `panel`, so an unowned param trailed the admin into every other tab and came back on the next reload as a filter nobody asked for; `tab-nav.ts` strips the foreign ones on each switch (hooked on `dashtab:show`, so keyboard switching counts too) and `tests/admin-tab-params.test.ts` fails if a param is read but unclaimed. **In-panel AJAX nav:** `swapPanel()` fetches the same URL, swaps only that tab's innerHTML, pushes state — **but only while that panel is still visible**: the fetch re-renders the whole dashboard (~1s) and pushing after a tab switch put the LEFT tab's URL back over the new one. **A tab's filter must be validated against its VOCABULARY, never against the rows just loaded** — the money journal checked `events.some(e => e.type === requested)`, so filtering to a type the journal had no rows for fell back to "no filter" and rendered the entire log (`isMoneyEventType`, סשן ד׳). **A filterable list still isn't a navigable one (סשן ג׳):** the money journal could be narrowed by type and paged, and reaching one specific row was still impossible — it needed free-text search, a date window, a date on every row (not only on the day heading), and a per-row `?mev=` permalink the SERVER resolves to a page, since a row's page moves as the journal grows. Treat "can I get to ONE item" as its own requirement on every list tab, separate from "can I filter". **`createFloatingPortal()` must stay a module-level singleton, not re-created per panel-swap** — it wires document-level listeners on every call. **Fixed-column card grids use CSS Grid with fixed widths, not flex** — content-sized columns drift row-to-row on digit count. Overview stat cards click the matching tab instead of navigating; Sellers/Stores/Orders/Alerts carry a `(N)` badge counting items created since the admin last **left** that tab (`admin-tab-views.ts`) — Messages is the exception, it already had an exact live unread count. **"What exactly is new" (2026-07-28):** the badge alone never said *which* rows it meant, so those 4 tabs also mark each qualifying row with `AdminNewBadge.astro` and offer an `AdminNewOnlyToggle.astro` filter chip (`snew`/`stnew`/`onew`/`alnew=1`, applied after the tab's own filter+sort so it composes) — badge, chips and filter all read the same `newSince` boundary. **The boundary is written ONLY on leaving a tab** (`tab-nav.ts` → POST `/api/admin/tab-view`); rendering `/admin` must never advance it, or every in-panel AJAX refresh (search/sort/pager re-fetch this same route) silently wipes the marks mid-session. **All three surfaces clear TOGETHER on leaving (2026-07-29)** — `clearTabBadge()` strips the tab count, every `.admin-new-chip` inside that panel, and the filter chip's `(N)`: the panels are SSR HTML and a tab switch only toggles `hidden`, so the chips used to keep claiming rows were new long after the count had gone, until a full page reload. `AdminNewBadge` takes `block` (own line, `w-fit`) instead of a preceding `<br />`, so removing a chip can't leave a stray break. Orders word the chip "לא נצפתה", not "חדש" — "חדשה" is already a shipping status there. **Per-store performance + advertising pages** (`/admin/store/[slug]/performance|advertising`, admin-guarded) mirror the seller tabs 1:1 for *any* store by reusing the pure libs and the seller's own client scripts, which read an optional `data-endpoint` (defaulting to the seller route) — the same pattern in both. Their SSR `#perf-initial-summary` blob is `set:html`-injected, so the admin one escapes `<`→`&lt;` to kill a `</script>` breakout — a cross-tenant XSS the seller's own dashboard doesn't face (same class as memory `project_json_script_xss`). Store-row actions share one flex group; block/unblock is a plain red text link routing through the global `ConfirmModal` before `/api/admin/moderation`. **Stores-tab product moderation:** a store row's product-count button expands an inline server-paginated (8/page) + searched product list, each product's block routed through `ConfirmModal`. The Sellers tab no longer nests products/block toggles — each store there links into the Stores tab (`?panel=stores&stq=<store name>`; the search matches name+slug and the readable name shows in the box, not the slug) so all moderation lives in one place. **Accrued seller balances (2026-08-05, `lib/seller-balance.ts`):** a tile on the seller card — "רווח מצטבר" = gross − commission at that seller's OWN tier (one platform-wide percent breaks silently, in the platform's favour, the moment a second tier is sold), plus a per-store line inside the card. **THREE money tiles, and the third is what made the other two readable (2026-08-11):** lifetime earnings, "תשלום קרוב" (what leaves on the payout day) and "יתרה ממתינה" (`payouts.ts#getHeldBySeller`) — the gap between the first two is money already sent PLUS money still in hold, and with only two tiles on screen the pair looked like it contradicted itself. **Derived, never stored** — a table would be a second home for the same fact and would drift on the first cancellation/refund/slug change — and it costs no query, being built from the revenue map the cards already hold. NOT net of the monthly fee, which is billed to the card rather than withheld from sales. **One purchase = one card, and the page is paged BY PURCHASE (2026-08-07, owner):** a multi-store cart is N order ROWS, and the tab drew N cards with N order numbers and N totals for one thing bought once. `lib/checkout-group.ts` holds the grouping key in BOTH spellings (JS + SQL) because the page slice is a query — `tests/checkout-group-key.test.ts` requires the two to agree. A filter selects which PURCHASES to show and then returns every row of them; filtering the rows would print a partial total, which is a wrong number, not a narrow one. `countOrdersSince` counts purchases to match; the Overview tile deliberately still counts ROWS and is renamed "הזמנות למוכרים" for it — that figure is what `reconcile.ts` cross-checks money against, where the unit must be the per-store slice. **Toolbar range picker (`lib/toolbar-range-picker.ts`, 2026-08-07):** shared by the money journal and Alerts; presets היום/השבוע/החודש/30 יום apply on the press, and the selected one is derived by comparing the current window to what each preset resolves to TODAY — a stored `?preset=` would still claim "היום" tomorrow. Alerts filters on the BUSINESS day (`created_at` is UTC; anything after 21:00 local carries tomorrow's date), a malformed bound means NO bound, and unlike the journal this tab has no default window. **Panel busy state:** `swapPanel` sets `data-busy` after 180ms — a "מעדכן…" CHIP inside the panel, never a bar across its top: the first version was already panel-sized and still read as a page loader, because a full-bleed line at the top edge is what "the page is loading" looks like everywhere (owner, 2026-08-07). Rows stay visible and barely dimmed — they are still the right answer to the previous question. One indicator in swapPanel, not one per tab. **Speed (2026-08-07):** the page made ~41 queries in ~17 SEQUENTIAL awaits against Neon, where every round trip costs ~64ms whatever the query — ~2.3s TTFB in dev AND production. Regrouped into 3 dependency waves (`Promise.all`, never floating promises: an unawaited rejection kills the process) → ~1.2s. **Then: ONE panel per request (2026-08-07).** All eleven were built on every request and `swapPanel` threw ten away; every read is now gated on the tab being asked for (`only()` in the page) and the rest load on the click, through the same `swapPanel`. Measured on the production build: overview 1056ms/522KB → 390ms/49KB, data 1183→183ms, alerts 1384→293ms, worst tab 623ms; HTML 522KB → 49–167KB. `AdminPanelShell.astro` renders the CONTAINER for all eleven whatever happens — `DashTabsBoot` toggles `hidden` on it, `swapPanel` replaces its innerHTML and `wirePanelLinks` binds one delegated listener to it — and each body carries its own `&&`, because Astro evaluates a slot child's props at the CALL SITE (a 500 on every request until it did). Its placeholder is static: a shimmer on ten hidden panels is ambient looping motion. **The prerequisite, and it was not optional:** three of the five `(N)` badges were JS filters over lists the page happened to hold (`sellers`/`stores`/`errorsAll`), so lazy panels would have set them silently to zero — no error, and an owner still trusting them. All five are one statement of five COUNTs (`lib/admin-tab-badges.ts`) fetched on EVERY request whatever tab is open, because a badge exists to report a tab you are not looking at, and they are **live**: `GET /api/admin/tab-badges` polled every 45s (paused while hidden), with leaving a tab sequenced against the poll by a generation counter — a response that left before the `tab-view` write landed carries the old count and would put back the badge just cleared. `tests/admin-tab-badges.test.ts` fails if any `newCount` stops coming from that one object. **Alerts search (2026-08-07, owner):** `?alq=` matches BOTH spellings of an id — the short `#a1b2c3d4` a person sees and the full uuid a log line carries, `#` optional — plus route/message/store/status; distinct from `?alref=`, which is the mail's exact-match deep link. **Sellers-tab card (2026-07-26):** per-seller metrics are labelled mini stat-tiles in a fixed grid so tiles line up across cards; on mobile the kebab is pinned to the card's top corner via explicit `grid-column:2;grid-row:1` (auto-flow otherwise dropped it below the stats row).
- **The platform's own books — admin `כספים` card + `דוח חשבונאי` tab (2026-08-11).** The owner could not answer *"מה ההכנסות הקרובות, מה אני מחזיק שהוא לא שלי אלא של המוכר"* from any screen, and the reason is structural: every money surface was PER SELLER, and summing the payout plan does not give the liability — those rows exist only for sellers already out of hold, so a normal week of undelivered orders is missing from it entirely. So the platform figure comes from a platform aggregate (`payouts.ts#getPlatformAccrual`, one query with the released half as a `FILTER` over the same rows, so held and released are two parts of one read). `platform-ledger.ts` turns that plus the payout plan into the overview card: sellers' money we hold, split ready/waiting, what leaves on the payout day, and the commission earned-but-not-yet-collected. Nothing is floored at zero — an overpayment is the one state worth acting on, and flooring would break the identity the card is read for. **The statement (`platform-statement.ts`) is a DOCUMENT, not a dashboard:** one month (or a free range), both bases side by side because mixing them is the classic error — accrual dated by when the sale happened, cash dated by when money moved — bridged by the one figure printed in both. Opening balance is the SAME expression as closing evaluated over everything before the period, so months chain (July's closing IS August's opening) and periods add; `reporting-invariants` pins both plus the cross-surface one that matters: a statement run to today closes on the same number the overview card shows. CSV and screen come from one loader (`admin-statement-load.ts`), printing is the browser's own engine behind a `@media print` block. **Three things are deliberately absent and the document says so on its face:** ad revenue (every ad figure here is a deterministic mock until Google/Meta are connected — an accountant may not be handed an invented number), VAT and invoice numbers (owner-blocked on the רו״ח), and line-by-line detail (rejected as overload). It also says it is computed live, so cancelling an old order restates a period already printed. **What it is NOT:** a formal Israeli financial statement. It is the source document a רו״ח is handed — no expenses, no depreciation, no VAT.
- **Where the money is, and why it is not moving (2026-08-11).** `payouts.ts#getHeldBreakdown` splits held money by REASON — the seller has not shipped (no clock has started; the only actionable line) versus inside the buyer's return window (resolves itself on a date) — and each links into the orders behind it. **Both dashboards now filter orders by the same five payout states**, and getting there took a SQL twin: the seller's list is client-rendered and classifies in JS, the admin's is paginated in the database, so `payout-hold.ts#PAYOUT_CLASS_SQL` sits directly under `RELEASABLE_SQL` with a branch order mirroring `orderHold` exactly, and `tests/admin-orders-page.test.ts` runs both over a fixture reaching all five states (pickup included — the one row where the two arms of the CASE disagree). **The vocabulary is the feature.** Three namings were rejected before this one: "סטטוס תשלום" meant the buyer's charge on one dashboard and the seller's payout on the other; "שחרור הכסף" described ordinary hold as confinement (*"כאילו הכסף מוחזק איפשהו, נשמע רע"*); and naming the mechanisms (`בתוך חלון ההחזרה`, `מוכן לתשלום`) answered a question nobody asks. What works is the JOURNEY, identical on both screens and on the order card itself: ממתין לשליחה → בדרך ללקוח → ממתין לסיום ימי החזרה → אושר לתשלום, plus לא ישולם — ההזמנה בוטלה או שהחיוב לא עבר. The last one is a STATE and not a promise about timing on purpose (owner: *"משהו שיהיה אפשר להמשיך איתו"*): the chain continues into `נשלח לבנק` and `הועבר`, which `seller_payouts.status` already records per TRANSFER — ⚠️ and cannot yet be said per ORDER, because nothing links an order to the payout that settled it. Until that link exists an order paid out in March still reports as approved. The buyer's charge is its own column, "חיוב הקונה", sharing the verb (עבר/טרם עבר/נכשל) so the two families read as one vocabulary. **"תקוע" is kept for exactly one line** — a released balance with no bank details — because that one really is stuck and needs a person.
- **Platform performance — admin `ביצועים` tab (סשן ד׳):** app-wide twin of the seller Performance tab, placed right before advertising. `platform-performance.ts#buildPlatformPerformance` aggregates EVERY store by calling the single-sourced `buildPerformanceSummary` per store and merging, so totals can never drift from a store's own view. Two money cards: **GMV/payouts** (buyer money passing through) and **income by source** (`platform-revenue.ts#buildPlatformRevenue` — sales commission + subscription fees pro-rated per seller tier + campaign margin; **ad spend is pass-through to Google/Meta and never income**, only `AD_PLATFORM_MARGIN_PERCENT` on it is, and platform BRAND campaigns are an expense so they're excluded). Breakdown table (סשן ב׳): server-paged 10/page over every store, name+slug search, sort on any column, all via AJAX to the same endpoint — no reload, charts untouched. `selectStoreRows` (search→sort→paginate, the post-DB query order) owns the universe rule: **browsing shows only stores active in range, a SEARCH deliberately spans ALL stores** (else a 1-day range makes 40 of 45 unfindable) and returns them `active:false` for the "ללא פעילות בטווח" row label. Reuses the shared `performance.ts` client via `#perf-*` ids + an admin `data-endpoint`, which appends `picker.dataset.extraParams` to every range fetch so a range change preserves the live search; the admin client owns table+cards, synced on `perf:loaded`. `buildZeroPoints` builds the empty axis without a throwaway summary call. Per-store admin pages take `?from=stores|performance|advertising` for a context-aware back link. **This aggregation used to cost ~600ms and was paid by EVERY admin tab (2026-07-31):** `swapPanel` re-renders all 11 panels, so a store-filter click ran it too — measured 700ms end-to-end for one chip. All of it was `Intl` construction inside a per-store loop, none of it the panel-swap architecture: **`toLocaleDateString(locale, opts)` builds a whole `Intl.DateTimeFormat` on every call** (the axis labels, ~31 × 45 stores, and the aggregator throws them away), plus a 2 MB pageviews parse per store and `formatToParts` per order per store. Now ~80ms via memoised labels + the `store-pageviews.ts` read cache + a `businessDayISO` memo. Rendering all 11 panels per swap is still there and was left alone deliberately (64KB gzipped) — memory `project_admin_dashboard_render_cost` holds the shape of that fix and the rule that came out of this: bench it before optimising, the obvious suspect was wrong.
- **Money integrity — the defence layers (2026-07-29, full context in the `project_metric_integrity_audit` memory).** A systematic trace of every seller/admin-visible number to its source, plus the mechanisms that keep it true as the app changes. Four owning modules (`money.ts`, `business-day.ts`, `order-status-rules.ts`, `orders.ts#countsAsRevenue` — see Hard rules → Money & reported numbers) and five layers: **invariants** (`tests/reporting-invariants.test.ts`, run over fixtures), **fuzz** (`tests/reporting-fuzz.test.ts` — seeded PRNG, hostile prices, 100% discounts, every payment×shipping combo, midnight/month-end/DST timestamps; this is what found the negative-revenue bug), **tree-scanning guards** (`tests/money-guards.test.ts` — scans `src/lib`+`src/pages/api`+`src/scripts` for the forbidden patterns rather than a named file list, so new modules are covered on creation; it found 17 files nobody had looked at), **live reconciliation** (`reconcile.ts` — each headline recomputed by an independent route and compared, rendered at the top of the admin "יומן כספי" tab on every load), and an **append-only money journal** (`money-events.ts` — charge attempts incl. declines, order created, duplicate blocked, status/amount changes). Checkout is idempotent (`checkout-idempotency.ts` claim→complete/release + `checkout-attempt-key.ts` on the client, key bound to the buyer via `checkoutOwner`; the process-local `Mutex` is deleted — the stock decrement is one conditional UPDATE and correct on any number of instances), and the mock provider can be told to decline (`MOCK_DECLINE_MARKER`, `+decline@` in the buyer email, DEV-gated) so the rollback path is executable rather than only unit-tested. Bugs this closed: three different definitions of "which calendar day", an admin GMV headline that could never reconcile with the per-store rows, negative revenue from a discount based on subtotal+shipping but subtracted from subtotal, no double-charge protection, a stock leak on two checkout error paths, and a catch that restocked orders that already existed.
- **Money OWED — the area audit (2026-08-07, review-diff row 2 · `lib/refund-owed.ts`, `tests/money-owed-guards.test.ts`).** Every check above compares two views of the same ORDER rows, so none of them can see money that moved with no row behind it. The money journal is the independent record, and comparing the two is a second reconciliation family (`JOURNAL_ONLY_CHECKS`) that found three silent states: a seller cancelling a PAID order left the captured money with us with no total naming it (now `refund_due`, written where the debt is created); a capture that never completed left rows `pending` forever with stock off the shelf and nobody told (now `STUCK_PENDING_MINUTES`); an authorization with no order and no void — the 0017 shape — is now a live check. `PAYMENT_STATUS_RULES.moneyWasTaken` is the column that makes "was a real person's money taken" a different question from "does this earn anyone anything today", and a cancelled paid order is exactly where they differ. `refund_settled` exists and **nothing writes it on purpose** — it needs the provider's refund call, so every obligation stays open and visible (⚠️ GO_LIVE §3: until then each line on that card is money to return by hand). Also: capture succeeded + seller notification failed had NO trace anywhere; both notification catches log now, under `notify:*` rather than `/api/checkout`, so a badge failure cannot page a person for a checkout that worked.
- **First-party analytics + admin "נתונים" tab (2026-07-21):** our OWN funnel capture, independent of the GTM/Meta dataLayer (which only forwards to third parties and stores nothing queryable). `analytics.ts` is Postgres since 2026-08-02 (`analytics_daily`/`analytics_visitors`/`analytics_products`); a funnel STAGE is `COUNT(DISTINCT visitor_id)` over the range, which is why it can never be summed out of per-day figures. Events are captured **server-side where they truly happen** (never client-forgeable): `middleware.ts` → `page_view` / `view_item` / `begin_checkout` / `seller_register_view` (content-type-gated, `/admin` excluded); `/api/store-product` → `view_item`; `/api/checkout` after commit → `purchase`. Only `add_to_cart` is client-reported (unauth POST, strict whitelist, vid taken from the httpOnly cookie **not the body**, and the productId must be a real uuid — against the DB an invented id is a permanent row, not just a key in a day's tally). Pure aggregation helpers + a seller onboarding funnel (`seller-funnel.ts`: registerViews→registered→withStore→withProduct→withSale — "where sellers get stuck", a lifetime cumulative snapshot from existing records). Admin data tab (`AdminDataPanel.astro`+`data-panel.ts`) = buyer funnel with step drop-off + conversion/cart-abandonment/checkout-abandonment/bounce cards + top-added-vs-abandoned products + the seller funnel. Buyer-funnel data accrues from LIVE traffic (empty until real browsing — clear empty-states); the seller funnel is populated day one. **Funnel row display (2026-07-21):** bar width normalizes to the **widest** stage, not the first — the seller funnel's top stage (live-analytics only) can be smaller than its all-time lower stages, and normalizing to the first made every lower bar overflow to a flat 100%; the per-step % reads "X% המשיכו" and shows only when meaningful. Tests: `analytics.test.ts`, `seller-funnel.test.ts`.
- **Silent store promotion / "shop-window" (סשן ב׳):** admin-only `Store.promoWeight` (0/1/2) — never shown to or settable by the seller (`/api/store` whitelists fields; `/api/admin/promote` sets it with **no seller notification**, the opposite of moderation.ts). It floats a store forward only in the platform's OWN discovery real estate — home discovery+spotlight (`promoFirst`, stable sort preserves the daily shuffle within a tier) and the `/stores` directory — never a paid/external channel, and never fabricates ratings or social proof. Controlled from the Stores-tab row and the admin Advertising tab. Moral model = a mall's window placement: the platform's own space, the seller pays nothing, the buyer sees only real relevant products.
- **Content moderation / spam prevention:** `spam-filter.ts` — two automatic checks, no manual queue: a **word-boundary** blocklist (not substring — avoids false positives like "XXX-Large") and keyword-stuffing detection (a word repeating ≥8 times AND ≥30% of significant words). The admin-only store/product block is the one deliberately manual gate. **Every public surface must gate through `isStoreVisible`/`isProductVisible`, never an inline `!x.blocked` filter** — a review pass once found 3 surfaces that had forgotten it. `isProductVisible` covers admin `blocked` OR seller `hidden`, so both ride one gate. Blocking always notifies the seller — their reply is the appeal channel. **Images are a SEPARATE question and were never covered (audited 2026-08-13):** the bytes go straight from the seller's browser to Cloudinary on an unsigned preset, so our server never sees them and the classifier has to be Cloudinary's — an add-on enabled ON THE PRESET, which is an owner step (⚠️ GO_LIVE §2.6, still open). What is in code is the verdict half, `lib/image-moderation.ts` wired into `cloudinary.ts`: a `rejected`/`aborted` upload never becomes a URL, and `pending`/`queued` is refused too because Cloudinary does not deliver an unapproved asset. It is deliberately SILENT when no add-on ran — until the owner flips it, every upload passes. Never configure `manual`: it parks every seller's photo until a person approves, which is the admin-gatekeeping zero-touch forbids.
- **Visitor reports — "דווח על תקלה" (2026-08-13):** a form on `/contact` (`ReportForm.astro` → `/api/report`, unauthenticated by design and rate-limited per address), landing at the TOP of the admin's Alerts tab (`AdminReportsPanel.astro`) with the tab's own "(N)" counting reports alongside errors. Four kinds (fault/content/store/other); `content` is the human backstop for anything the automatic filters miss. **The reporter describes, the server attributes** — page, store, role and account id are resolved from the path and the session (`lib/request-actor.ts`, shared with the error log so the two lists on that one screen cannot describe the same person differently), never from the body. The page is captured from `?from=` or the `Referer`, so the footer's plain `/contact` link works from anywhere without a fifth footer link. Its own table, not `error_log`: that one is a rolling 500 and a burst of machine errors must not push out something a person wrote. Nothing is deletable — `handled` is the only state change.
- **Store lifecycle — pause / close / reopen (סשן א׳, 2026-07-31):** five states in ONE table (`lib/store-status.ts`: active·paused·closing·closed·blocked → reachable/discoverable/sellable/gone), transitions in `lib/store-lifecycle.ts`, seller UI in the settings tab, `/api/seller/store-lifecycle`. **Seller owns pause+close; admin keeps only `blocked`, which outranks both.** Pausing is immediate and unconditional (open orders are unaffected — they stay the seller's obligation); **closing is never refused** — with open orders it pauses and defers, and `settleStoreClosure` finishes it by itself when the last one is delivered/cancelled (`blocksStoreClosure` column in `order-status-rules.ts`). Nothing is ever deleted: every reporting surface reads `getAllStores()`, so historical totals are untouched. Paused/closed storefronts serve a one-line notice via `Astro.rewrite` at their OWN url — 200 / 410 (`store-unavailable.astro`, `store-gone.astro`; **Astro forces 404 on a rewrite to `/404`**, which is why the 410 needs its own route). Boosts stop through the existing starve path and are archived (never deleted) on final closure. Seller gets an email per state (`email/store-lifecycle-email.ts`) naming the open-order count as an obligation. Admin store list filters by state with counts. Guarded by `store-lifecycle-guard` (nobody re-reads the flags) + `i18n-parity` (a key added to one language only renders `undefined` and TS can't see it — the type is inferred from one side).
- **Unbuyable lines stay listed, marked, and out of every total (2026-07-31).** A cart or wishlist line whose product was deleted/hidden, or whose store stopped selling, keeps its photo/name/link and shows "לא זמין כרגע" — it is never silently removed, because a buyer who added three items and finds two assumes they bought something they did not. It leaves the subtotal, the grand total, the header count, and a store group with nothing buyable loses its checkout button. Both surfaces ask the SAME server question (`/api/cart/prices` → `gone`, via `fetchUnavailableLines`), and the mark is SET AND CLEARED, so a store returning from a pause restores its lines with nothing to click. Wishlist entries are never written to — the list is the shopper's own curation.
- **Email automation (סשן ד׳):** modular `src/lib/email/` adapter (**Resend** chosen after research; a console no-op in dev without `RESEND_API_KEY`) + a branded RTL template (`template.ts`, inline-hex mirror of the tokens — mail clients drop `<style>`/`var()`). Two live automations, both fire-and-forget and internally resilient so a mail failure never breaks checkout or a status update: **order confirmation** at checkout (buyer gets ONE email for the whole cart, grouped by store, with thumbnails and a "this is not a tax invoice" note) + a per-store **seller** notification (store name in the subject for multi-store sellers); and **status emails** (ready/shipped+tracking/cancelled) fired from `notifyOrderStatusChanged` so they reach **GUESTS too** — the in-app notification only reaches registered buyers. Copy is shared with the in-app channel via `order-status-copy.ts` so wording can't drift; all buyer/product fields `esc()`-escaped (tests: `order-emails`, `order-status-email`). **Only go-live step: connect the provider** (key + verified domain + SPF/DKIM, GO_LIVE §4). Invoices/receipts are the split-payment provider's job, NOT this email.
- **Messaging — and there is deliberately NO per-store contact address (dropped 2026-07-27 after review, moved here from the data models):** buyer↔seller contact is the in-app thread, and order/status mail already goes to the seller's account email, so a second per-store inbox is one nobody watches. Revisit only if a seller needs order mail routed somewhere other than their login address. `MessageCompose.astro` on store/product pages; seller+buyer dashboard tabs both use the sort/filter toolbar; threads expand in place; live poll 15s. No SMTP — the buyer sees the seller email directly (memory `project_messaging_email`). Unread detection must check reply messages too (a reply only carries `toSellerId`, matched via the thread root). **Multi-store scoping:** the seller messages tab filters by seller **and** the open store — it was showing every store's threads. **The inbox table sizes itself off its PANEL, not the viewport (2026-07-30, all three surfaces):** `.msg-table` is `table-layout:fixed` with column widths on the `<th>`s (a `<td>`'s `max-width` is ignored in auto layout, so the preview cell's min-content was its whole message and the table scrolled sideways from 641px to ~980px of viewport), and the collapse-to-cards rule is `@container msgpanel (max-width:760px)` with `.msg-cq` on the panel — which must wrap the `.table-toolbar` too, since that toolbar carries sort/filter once the `<thead>` is gone. Products keeps its own `@media` 640px (no container). Full trap write-up: memory `project_table_shrink_traps`. **A thread OPENS with a header (2026-08-10)** — the subject in full plus the product it is about (`.msg-thread-head`), because the table clips both and the card layout dropped the product entirely; the product line is back on the card too, keyed off `data-filter-product` so a thread without one shows no em-dash. **The reply box is closed behind "כתוב תגובה"** and nothing auto-focuses it: an always-open textarea under every thread read as a chat window. All FOUR renderers carry each of these (SellerMessageRow.astro, messages.ts#buildMessageRow, buyer/dashboard.astro, admin-messages.ts) — change one, change four. **Flooding is bounded on BOTH sides** by `lib/message-flood.ts`: a per-thread cap on unanswered messages in a row plus per-sender hourly buckets, refused 429 with the sentence already written in the sender's language. Every send handler shows that sentence — all three used to swallow a failed reply silently.
- **Admin↔seller messaging:** `admin-messages.ts` — flat, one conversation per seller (not subject-threaded like buyer↔seller `messages.ts`, since admin only ever has one relationship per seller). Seller side has **no separate tab**: the admin thread renders as a pinned "הודעות מערכת" row inside the regular messages tab. Both sides poll 15s.
- **Notifications:** bell with a numeric unread badge (shares `updateBadge()`); polls 30s + refetch on `visibilitychange`; deleted on read. **Stock alerts:** `low_stock`/`out_of_stock` fired from `/api/checkout` after the order commits, mutually exclusive per item, cleared once the seller updates stock. The seller alert dot is derived fresh each poll. Toast dedup via `toastOnce(key)` so the site poller and a page's own poll can't double-toast. Both indicators' initial state is **SSR-rendered** (avoids a hidden-then-fetched flash; see Hard rules → Scalability for the JSON-era cost). **Toast-on-fresh-nav race, fixed:** the poller could resolve before `ToastContainer`'s listener attached — an early `is:inline` buffer (`window.__toastQueue`) drains once ready.
- **Brand identity (2026-08-05, re-cut 2026-08-10):** one `BrandLogo.astro` — a drawn `D` (right half of a regular octagon) plus "ezabin" in **Chakra Petch 700 at −0.04em, thickened 0.014em**, the button gradient running as ONE ramp across the whole wordmark on light surfaces, plain white on brand ones; optional Hebrew slogan beneath, in Heebo. It is the header, both auth pages, the hero and the avatar menu's "בית" icon; `public/favicon.svg` and `public/brand/*.svg` are the same drawing.
  **THE LETTERS ARE OUTLINES, NOT TEXT, and that is the thing to understand first.** Chakra Petch stops at 700 with no variable axis, so "heavier" can only be an outline — and CSS cannot paint `-webkit-text-stroke` with a gradient, so a text wordmark would halo. Hence SVG; and once it is SVG, paths cost nothing and buy three things: **Chakra Petch never ships** (nothing else uses it, so this is not an eleventh preload), **the logo cannot paint in a fallback face** (`font-display:optional` refuses a face missing at first paint — how the lockup twice shipped 24px out in August), and the site's logo and the poster files become one artifact.
  **Everything geometric lives in the GENERATED `src/lib/brand-lockup.ts`** — `npm run brand:wordmark` (`scripts/generate-wordmark.mjs`, opentype.js, reading the two TTFs committed under `assets/brand-fonts/` with their OFL). Only four things are authored: the octagon's cut `H/(2+√2)`, the two optical ratios (0.799 horizontals / 0.9037 diagonals — they correct for the eye, not for a weight), the tracking and the thickening. The stem, the width, the mark's height and the gap to the `e` are read off the font, which is why a weight change was never one line. Every surface imports that module, so the old "four surfaces must stay byte-identical" problem is now one file; `tests/brand-lockup.test.ts` holds the static ones to it.
  Rules worth knowing before touching it: **(a) the DENSITY is load-bearing** — heavy + negative tracking is what makes the word one block; a lighter, opener version shipped once and was reverted within the hour (memory `project_brand_logo`), and the 0.014em thickening exists because Chakra Petch 700's stem (0.136em) is lighter than Heebo 800's (0.167em). **(b) Hebrew does not take tracking** — match the wordmark's width with SIZE at the font's own spacing. **(c) a line box is not its ink**, so the tagline carries a negative `margin-inline-start`; both its size and that margin then carry a BISECTION against the built site, because miter joins and rasterised bearings are not predictable on paper (the two constants sit at the top of the generator with their measurements). **(d) the mark stands to the ASCENDER, not the cap line** — deliberate, the owner's call. **(e) the drawn D is kept over the font's own**, which is a real choice and not an oversight: Chakra Petch chamfers its D at 45° exactly as the mark does, differing only in depth (17.1% of the height against the mark's 29.3%) — the owner compared them rendered and kept ours, and the icon is where that decision pays.
  A new slogan, weight, tracking or thickening needs `npm run brand:wordmark` **and then** `npm run brand:assets` re-run, and the two bisections re-measured. `npm run brand:assets` renders the three surfaces no SVG can serve: the email header, `og-default.jpg` (JPEG because WhatsApp drops previews over ~300KB) and the iOS home-screen icon — it now reads the same module, so it can no longer drift on its own. **A store page wears the STORE's own icon in the browser tab** (`store-image.ts#storeIconUrl`, gated on `storeSlug && !sellerMode`). Zero-touch: no upload falls back to the generated mark, and there must never be a "set your favicon" step. It is a CIRCLE on-site, because a store's identity already is one everywhere else here (StoreAvatar); the iOS touch icon stays SQUARE and opaque, since iOS masks it itself and composites transparency onto BLACK.
- **Navigation context:** `last-store.ts` (sessionStorage) saved on every store/product view; `Header.astro` renders a persistent "back to store" pill on any non-store page, surviving detours through notifications/checkout, suppressed + cleared on the homepage (landing there is a deliberate exit). The avatar dropdown's "בית" is the only way back to the platform home, and the row under it is **saved stores, as a FLYOUT beside the menu** (`/api/saved-stores`, lazy on first open) — `position:fixed` and measured against the VIEWPORT, because as a disclosure it grew the menu by its own height and pushed logout off a phone. Three things it must keep: the STAR, not a heart (the heart is the product-wishlist mark in six other files, one of them the wishlist button in this same header); no chevron (the row's trailing edge is the LEFT in Hebrew while the panel opens RIGHT, so it pointed back across its own label); and the lit-row rule in header.css rather than an `aria-expanded:` utility — `.user-dropdown__item` sets `background` from an UNLAYERED sheet, so the utility silently did nothing. That last trap is now guarded tree-wide by `tests/unlayered-css-beats-utility.test.ts`. **Only the logo / store-name is a click-to-home target — the rest of the header row is inert (2026-07-26):** the stretched store-mode logo column used to make its whole empty width navigate home, so `.logo`/`.store-name-link` are `inline-flex`. Header height/logo invariants: Architecture → Header (memory `project_header_layout`).
- **Mobile header:** ≤640px the store + homepage search hide behind a magnifying glass opening a fixed panel (DOM-teleport). Notif+wishlist panels are `position:fixed; left:8px; right:8px` at the button rect. All dropdowns mutually close with a fade-close animation. Flash-on-resize fix: `visibility:hidden` on closed drawers + dropdown animations gated on `:not([hidden])` + a `matchMedia` transition suppressor on the buyer sidebar. **The magnifier at the START of both header search bars IS the submit button (2026-08-07, owner: after typing there was no way to search but Enter).** `disabled` while the field is empty, so it looks and behaves exactly like the inert icon it replaced; live from the first character. It sits at the start on purpose — the field's END is where the browser draws `type=search`'s own clear "x", at exactly the moment this button appears. Same control inside the mobile panel, which teleports the whole bar.
- **PQV (`ProductQuickView.astro`):** native `<dialog>`, `pqv:open` event, lightbox inside; main image = scroll-snap carousel + dots only (no thumbnail strip). The "liquid" dot flow is shared across all three carousels via `carousel-dots.ts#initLiquidDots`. NB `StoreProductModal` (store cards + related cards) is a **separate, richer** modal from PQV (cart/checkout/dashboard): it adds a stock note, per-combo variant availability, and pushState URL sync. Scrollable body + pinned CTA footer; `dir=auto` on the name+price+desc block. **Lightbox backdrop (2026-07-23):** while the lightbox is open the modal CARD is hidden, else its white surface shows through the dim as a lighter patch.
